# Inflozo — Product Requirements Document

**Intended consumers:** BMAD agents (Analyst → PM → Architect → SM → Dev → QA) via Claude Code. This PRD is the single source of truth. Appendices A–I are normative, not illustrative.

**Marker convention (normative for how this document is read).** Three markers appear inline, and each means something a downstream agent must act on differently. **[ASSUMPTION]** — stated with the same confidence as a verified fact but *not* verified; every one carries its basis, and §7.6's verify-at-build list is the complete register of the external ones. **[AMBITION — not a requirement]** — a direction, carrying no gate and blocking nothing; it may never be treated as testable or added to the Definition of Done. **[KNOWN UNTESTED PATH]** — specified and buildable, but written against behaviour nobody has observed; it ships marked, and where it is launch-critical it carries a blocking gate (the Ghost(Pro) Preview-only path is the one such case). Deferred items are **non-goals** and live in Appendix G, which is binding in both directions. Anything resting on a research companion says so and names it, per the precedence order below.

**Normative companions** (same folder, each carrying its own `status` frontmatter; all are binding on the areas they cover):

- `sections-inventory.md` — Appendix A's full design inventory (**and the count**, generated from the export) and the Synthesis Defaults. **Normative for scope.**
- `addendum.md` — AD1 local-first persistence, AD2 the edit-lock protocol. **Normative for intent, not for scope:** it is the mechanism of record cited by FR-D9, FR-D10 and FR-D18, and the Architect designs against it rather than around it.
- `research-ghost-binding-contexts.md` — the citation-backed Ghost template-context reference. **Source of truth** for FR-H7 and `appendix-b1-template-contexts.md`.
- `research-ghost-empty-values.md` — the citation-backed **evidence base** for FR-H8's guard and fallback rules.
- `research-ghost-membership-pages.md` — the citation-backed Ghost membership-surface reference. **Source of truth** for A30's `compileTarget`, for FR-I1's membership emission and for Appendix B's Portal action set.
- `appendix-b1-template-contexts.md` — the Template Context Matrix. **Normative for FR-H7:** the editor offers only the bindings this matrix permits.
- `appendix-h1-string-catalog.md` — the chrome-string catalog. **Normative for FR-Q6:** its keys and English defaults are the catalog the compiler validates against.
- `research-ghost-koenig-cards.md` — the citation-backed Koenig card reference. **Source of truth** for FR-Q7's `card_assets` mechanism, the four cards whose JS must be vendored, and the style-guide fixture's card enumeration (FR-H3).
- `research-canvas-iframe-geometry.md` — how Webflow, Elementor and Gutenberg actually build an editing canvas. **Source of truth** for §7.3's canvas geometry, the chrome partition and the two-axis device resize.
- `research-contested-variants.md` — the six contested designs settled against Ghost source. **Source of truth** for A7 #12, A19 #11, A27 #11, A29 #13, A32 #10 and A32 #12, for Appendix B's four core helpers, and for NFR-3's Admin-key prohibition.
- `research-section-js-libraries.md` — the behaviour-module survey. **Source of truth** for FR-G7's module registry — and for its count, which is read there rather than restated, the per-module no-JS degradation statements, the licence filter, and FR-G8's pinned browser baseline.
- `verify-mechanical-ghost-claims.md` and `verify-mechanical-theme-and-math.md` — the verification passes. **They override the review findings they check**, including this document's own earlier validation report: gscan severities were read from `lib/specs` and re-proved by running gscan on purpose-built fixtures, and every arithmetic row was recomputed independently from this PRD's own inputs. Between them they caught **six wrong "fixes"** that would each have deleted or damaged something real.
- `spike-compiler/` — the runnable compiler spike (annotated HTML in, gscan-clean Ghost theme out). **Empirical validation** of the compiler decision; re-runnable, and the reference implementation of its mechanics.
- `../../design/claude-design-prompt.md`, `../../design/claude-design-prompt-2.md`, `../../design/claude-design-prompt-3-library.md` — the design method of record. Prompt 1 remains binding for the design system, voice and core components; **prompt 2 wins wherever they disagree**, because it encodes decisions made after prompt 1 was written; prompt 3 is the per-category library prompt, run once per category (§4).

**Precedence, so a future contradiction resolves itself without a ruling.** Highest first:

1. **The verification files** — on any claim they checked. They exist because assertions entered this document once with the authority of research and were wrong.
2. **The research companions** — on any external fact about Ghost. Three reviewers independently found them **more accurate than this document's body text**, and every spot-check of the membership and binding-context research matched current Ghost source exactly. **The regression risk in this project lives in the distillation step, not the research step** — so where `prd.md` and a research companion disagree about Ghost, the PRD is wrong and moves.
3. **The normative companions** — `sections-inventory.md` on scope, `appendix-b1-template-contexts.md` on where a binding is legal, `appendix-h1-string-catalog.md` on chrome strings.
4. **`prd.md`** — this file, on everything else, and on every product **decision** without exception. No companion overrides a decision; they override *facts*. The rank-3 companions win only inside the areas they are declared normative for above — which is why Appendix A says the inventory wins on scope and means it, without that being an exception to this line.
5. **`addendum.md`** — normative for mechanism, not for scope; it states its own subordination and that statement stands.
6. **`spike-compiler/`** — empirical and narrow. It proves a mechanism works; it never settles what should be built.

**Amendments of 2026-08-19 (v4.1), all made by execution rather than by review.** Four claims in this document were tested against the platforms they describe. Three were wrong and are corrected in place, each marked inline with its date and its evidence: §7.3's escaping remedy (verified **false** — Handlebars' backslash escape is not composable), a fourth binding compiler mechanic (a mustache abutting a closing brace is a **parse error**, and FR-Q5's token block emits exactly that shape), and a gscan rule this document had never named (`GS051-CUSTOM-FONTS`, now normative in §7.4 with its carve-out in §1.2 1b). The fourth was confirmed and **closes §7.6 item 19**. This is the standing rule working as intended: cite or execute. The evidence lives in `../../architecture/architecture-Inflozo-2026-08-19/MEASUREMENTS.md`, which joins the verification files at **rank 1** of the precedence order below for the claims it checked.

**The research companions are dated snapshots and are never edited to match this document.** When Ghost changes, a companion is re-run and the PRD moves to meet it — the reverse would destroy the only independent check this project has. **Two research companions can still disagree with each other, and precedence cannot settle that**: §7.6's item 14(b) is the live example, where the tiers-visibility conflict is resolved by executing E0 against a real Ghost, and until then FR-H6's explicit `visibility:public` filter is correct on either reading. That is the pattern for every research-vs-research conflict — resolve by execution, and choose the reading that is safe under both in the meantime.

User journeys are deliberately not authored here: journey and flow authorship is delegated to the UX workflow (bmad-ux), using the §3 personas. Its mandated floor is four **journeys** — connect → first deploy (which now carries the deferred Staff Access Token step **and** the decline path, FR-C1), blank-canvas build, Free-plan ship, downgrade recovery — plus **six flows** the PRD specifies as designed surfaces rather than warning toasts: the FR-J13 pre-deploy snapshot gate, FR-D18's three-party edit-lock choreography, FR-I4's guided routes-upload card, FR-J14's library-update confirm, FR-C2's Preview-only explanation with its clearing conditions, and **FR-I6's post-deploy template-binding checklist** — the sixth, added because a designed membership page is not finished until someone assigns it in Ghost and nothing in the product previously said so.

---

## 1. Goals & Background Context

### 1.1 Problem

Ghost is a world-class publishing platform with a primitive theming story. Customizing a Ghost site means hand-editing Handlebars templates, working within a marketplace of rigid pre-built themes, or hiring a developer. Visual building for Ghost is barely served: the most prominent shipping visual Ghost builder, Fantasma, falls short on the axes that matter (see §1.5). Ghost publishers who care about design are still stuck choosing between "someone else's theme," a shallow builder, and "learn to code."

### 1.2 Product Vision

**Inflozo is the visual builder for Ghost CMS.** Users assemble their site by dropping pre-made, professionally designed sections onto a canvas that **renders the same markup Ghost will render, styled by the same CSS Inflozo ships** — the canvas is the page, not a preview of it. Inflozo compiles the design into a clean, valid, hand-editable Ghost theme and deploys it to the user's site in one click.

**The promise, stated precisely.** Canvas and shipped output agree **by construction**, not by comparison: one authored source feeds both renderers (§7.3), so the markup and the styling are the same thing rather than two things kept in step. That is a stronger guarantee than a preview and it is the product's central claim. It is also **not a claim that the canvas is pixel-identical to the live page**, and the difference is honest rather than grudging — four things live on the real page that the canvas does not own:

1. **The user's own Ghost code injection**, which legitimately changes the live page and is outside Inflozo's control — which is why connecting a site carrying code injection surfaces a notice (FR-C2).

   **1b. The site owner's own font choice in Ghost Admin** *(added 2026-08-19)*. Ghost lets a site owner set typography from Ghost Admin, and gscan's `GS051-CUSTOM-FONTS` requires every theme to wire those settings up (FR-J2, FR-J6). Inflozo honours them, so an owner who sets fonts in Ghost Admin sees those fonts rather than the Style Pack pairing they designed with. **This is the owner's site and the owner's setting, and it is the only honest reading** — declaring support for the feature while ignoring it would satisfy the checker and lie to the user. It is a carve-out the product *discloses* rather than one it removes, and it sits beside code injection for the same reason: it is the owner overriding, deliberately, from outside Inflozo.
2. **Ghost's own injected surfaces.** `{{ghost_head}}` brings Portal's floating subscribe button (`position: fixed`, on by default on essentially every site), Ghost's native announcement bar, the comments embed and the search overlay. These are Ghost's, not the theme's. **Two of the four are shimmed on the canvas rather than merely disclosed** (FR-H5), because designing blind to them would be a real defect: Portal's button, which lands exactly where edge-rail designs place their own furniture, and **Ghost's announcement bar, drawn as a strip at the very top and taking real vertical space** — so a header, an overlay hero or an Inflozo announcement bar is authored knowing what sits above it. **The announcement bar is also the one a user can take back entirely:** its text can be copied into an Inflozo section and Ghost's own bar switched off, after which the strip disappears and it is designed rather than carved out (FR-C4, Appendix A §2) — a carve-out the product *removes*, not one it merely discloses. Only the comments embed and the search overlay remain scoped out.
3. **Real content.** The canvas never renders post or page body HTML (NFR-3) — it shows a style-guide fixture instead (FR-H3). Layouts that respond to a body's actual shape are designed against that fixture, and vary with the real article.
4. **Viewport height in desktop editing.** The canvas is a frame inside the app, so it is always shorter than the visitor's browser window; a full-height section cannot be pixel-exact against `100vh` while editing on desktop. Device preview resizes the frame to a real device viewport in both axes (FR-D8), where the number is honest.

Nothing else is carved out, and NFR-6(c3) diffs the canvas against a real Ghost page under exactly this exclusion set — so the claim is tested rather than asserted.

**The experience must be extremely easy and genuinely fun** — closer to playing with a well-made toy than operating software. **[AMBITION — not a requirement]** *the most beautiful UI/UX in the Ghost ecosystem*: this is the design north star and it is stated as one. It carries no gate and nothing is blocked on it, because it cannot be tested; G1 and G2 (§1.3) are the measurable claims about ease and play, and they are gated.

### 1.3 Goals (measurable)

| # | Goal | Target |
|---|---|---|
| G1 | Connect Ghost site → branded canvas with the user's real content and colors | < 10 minutes end-to-end for a first-time user, including creating **the one Custom Integration** in Ghost Admin (FR-C1) — the Staff Access Token is deferred to first deploy and is therefore outside this clock, which is what makes the target reachable rather than aspirational — p75 across a moderated usability test of **8 first-time users**, timed by the observer on the NFR-1 reference environment, pre-launch |
| G2 | **From an existing connection:** blank project → first successful deploy | < 15 minutes — p75 across the same moderated test (n = 8). The clock starts at an already-connected site; G1's connect journey is excluded, so G2 no longer contains G1. **It does contain the deferred Staff Access Token step** (FR-C1), since that lands at first deploy — including the path where the user declines it and ships anyway |
| G3 | Every deploy passes gscan | 0 errors, warnings surfaced, always |
| G4 | Generated theme quality | **Zero axe-core violations at WCAG 2.1 AA on every shipped design** — pass/fail, no score (NFR-5). Output performance is asserted as the CI-testable properties Inflozo controls, not a Lighthouse score (NFR-2). User token edits can still break contrast: FR-E3's live contrast check warns, and responsibility transfers to the user |
| G5 | Section library at launch | **the categories and designs Appendix A inventories** — the count is generated there, never restated (standing rule 3); grows post-launch per FR-J14, so the inventory is a floor, never a fixed total |
| G6 | Editor feel | 60fps interactions; every control change visible < 100 ms — pass/fail on the NFR-1 reference environment and stress fixture |
| G7 | Business | Freemium; Pro $15/mo or $150/yr via Dodo Payments. **Two break-evens, because one is misleading (Appendix F.5): cash break-even ≈11–15 Pro subscribers** (recurring cost only, the spread driven almost entirely by CI cadence) and **payback ≈160–180** (recurring plus the amortised build). Both on the stated 60/40 yearly/monthly mix, blended net ≈ $12.61/mo per Pro customer. The earlier "9–10" excluded the permanent Test environment and the CI lane, and priced no part of building the library |
| G8 | Activation | ≥ 30% of signups reach first deploy within 7 days · counter: 7-day rollback rate < 10% |
| G9 | Conversion | ≥ 3% free→Pro within 90 days of signup · counter: refund/dispute rate < 2% |
| G10 | Retention | Pro **steady-state** monthly churn < 5%, measured from month 2 — the month-1 export-and-cancel spike is modeled separately in Appendix F, not counted against this target · counter: cancellation always completes in ≤ 3 clicks (no retention dark patterns) |

G8–G10 are internal business goals: the owner tracks them manually outside the product. No in-app analytics or telemetry requirement exists in v1 (NFR-8's privacy stance stands); instrumentation is added only if and when the owner requests it. G1, G2 and NFR-1's percentiles are therefore **moderated usability-test** targets, not telemetry percentiles: one pre-launch session, 8 first-time users, timed by an observer on the NFR-1 reference environment. They gate that session and are measured nowhere else.

### 1.4 Differentiators

1. **Single-source WYSIWYG** — the canvas and the compiler consume the *same* section source: the canvas renders it directly, the compiler serializes it into the `.hbs` + CSS that ships in the theme. There is no second preview implementation to drift out of sync (§7.3).
2. **Variant Shuffle** — flip a placed section through every sibling design in its category (up to 18) with the user's content preserved. Choosing a design becomes play.
3. **Auto-branding** — on connect, Inflozo reads the site's accent color, logo, title, and navigation from Ghost and seeds the project so the first canvas already looks like *their* site.
4. **Impossible to make ugly** — a strict vertical-stack model, a **disciplined** control vocabulary with no raw CSS values (open to new control types when a section genuinely needs one, never to raw values — FR-F1, Appendix C), and token-driven Style Packs mean every possible output is cohesive.
5. **Safe installs** — "an Inflozo theme never breaks your site" is a brand promise enforced in the pipeline: gscan-gated compiles, pre-deploy checks, a **pre-Inflozo snapshot** of the site's previous theme captured at Inflozo's first theme *upload* to that site (deploy-only included — never activation-time, FR-J13), a pre-deploy confirmation that the user holds their own copy of the current theme and routes file, **drift detection** that halts any deploy which would overwrite theme changes Inflozo did not make, and one-click rollback (FR-J6, FR-J9, FR-J13). Capture is real **when the site Owner's Staff Access Token is present**, and honestly degraded when it is not — the token is deferred to first deploy and may be declined outright (FR-C1), in which case FR-J13 hands the user a designed manual path instead of a broken promise. With the token, Inflozo reads the live theme itself, which is not bound by the method allowlist Ghost applies to Custom Integration keys — that allowlist blocks every `GET /themes/*` call made with an integration key, on every version and every host.
6. **Deep Ghost nativity** — live tiers pricing, members-aware sections, paywall CTA designs, Koenig card styling, portal actions, routes.yaml building. Inflozo is not a generic builder wearing a Ghost costume.
7. **Evergreen themes** — the section library is versioned and updated as Ghost evolves; users pick up improvements by signing in and redeploying (FR-J14). Competing themes are frozen at purchase time.

### 1.5 Competitive Landscape

**As of August 2026**, Fantasma (fantasma.io, Pro $99/yr) is the most prominent shipping visual Ghost builder. Its public product material describes form-based sidebar editing (no inline WYSIWYG), 155+ presets, and — on Pro — **direct publishing to Ghost** via an Admin API key with connected-site preview. Absent from that material, and central to Inflozo: membership and monetization sections (tiers, paywall designs), deploy versioning and rollback, image optimization, and any gscan or code-quality story. Ghost theme shops sell static themes at **$35–149 one-time (most $69–99)**. Inflozo's premium $15/mo is justified against both by WYSIWYG fidelity, library scale (the Appendix A inventory at GA), Ghost-native monetization depth, safe installs, and evergreen updates.

Every competitor statement above is a **dated observation, not a standing fact**. Any competitor claim reused on the marketing site (FR-N1, FR-N3) must be re-verified against that competitor's live public material at publish time and carry its verification date; an unverified comparative claim does not ship.

---

## 2. Product Principles (binding on all design & implementation decisions)

- **P1 — The canvas is sacred.** With nothing selected, the canvas shows the true website: no outlines, handles, grids, or badges. Affordances appear only on hover/selection and vanish on exit.
- **P2 — No CSS knowledge, ever.** Users never see pixels, hex codes, class names, or units at the section level. All values are named (Compact/Comfortable/Spacious), visual (layout thumbnails, swatches), or binary (toggles). The control vocabulary is **disciplined, not closed** — it may grow when a section genuinely needs a new control type (FR-F1, Appendix C) — but it never admits a raw value at the section level. Raw values exist in exactly one place: the Style Pack editor.
- **P3 — Bake by default, expose deliberately.** Section copy and control values are compiled into the theme. The generated theme exposes `@custom` settings only where the user deliberately configures them in the Theme Settings surface (FR-Q): the dark-mode built-ins (FR-Q5) plus any user-defined custom settings — always within Ghost's 20-setting cap, enforced with a visible meter.
- **P4 — One source of truth per section.** A section is its **annotated HTML** source + CSS (+ optional JS). The editor renders that source directly; the compiler serializes the same source into the theme's `.hbs`. No parallel React re-implementations of sections, and no second copy of a section anywhere.
- **P5 — Deployed sites cost Inflozo $0.** Assets bundle into the theme zip and are served by the user's Ghost server. Ghost content loads in the editor client-side via the Content API (which is designed to be browser-safe and cacheable). Inflozo's infra pays only for editing sessions and the marketing site.
- **P6 — Delight in small doses.** Micro-interactions, playful copy, and one celebratory moment (first deploy) — never at the expense of speed or clarity.
- **P7 — Everything ships before launch.** The full library, all templates, all plans, all pages. Testing happens on the production Vercel + Supabase stack against real Ghost instances (there are no users yet; see §4 — "Live" names the post-launch environment, not this one).
- **P8 — Ghost *content* is read-only; Ghost *presentation* is what Inflozo is for.** Inflozo never creates, modifies or deletes the user's **content** — posts, pages, tags, authors, tiers, members — and never writes a Ghost setting that is not presentation. Deleting a section removes theme code from the project only. **What Inflozo does write, by mandate:** the theme (upload and activate) and **`routes.yaml`** (FR-I4), both of which are the site's presentation layer and both of which the user connected Inflozo in order to have managed. The Admin API is used for exactly that, plus connection validation, reading the live `routes.yaml` back to verify what was written, and reading the live theme for the **pre-Inflozo snapshot** and pre-deploy drift check (FR-J13). **One further write exists and is consented, never silent:** clearing Ghost's own **announcement bar** after its text has been copied into an Inflozo section (FR-C4, Appendix A §2). It is presentation — site chrome, like the theme — it is offered as an explicit one-click action rather than performed quietly, and it is non-destructive by sequencing, because the copy is already inside Inflozo before the clear is offered. It is named here so it does not become the next contradiction: this principle has already been broken once by a write that was approved elsewhere and never reconciled back. **The line is content versus presentation, not read versus write** — an earlier phrasing said "settings" without qualification, which the automated routes upload contradicts.

---

## 3. Users & Roles

**One user role.** Every account has identical capabilities, differentiated only by plan (Free/Pro).

Personas (for tone and for the UX workflow, not permissions — no requirement is prioritized by persona). They are stated with enough substance to author journeys from, because the PRD delegates four journeys and six flows to the UX phase and thin personas mean that phase invents them. Each carries the one thing that decides how the product treats it: **its Ghost credential reach** — FR-C1's Staff Access Token is per user and carries that user's role: every staff user has one, an Administrator's carries the permissions Inflozo needs, and the Owner is not required (FR-C1; read at docs.ghost.org/admin-api and in Ghost's `fixtures.json`, 2026-09-03).

- **The solo publisher** — writer or newsletter operator, one Ghost site, zero code skills, on Ghost precisely because it kept them out of a codebase. Wants the site to look intentional and to stop looking like a default theme. **Credential reach: is the site Owner, but is the persona least comfortable minting anything** — reads "Staff Access Token" as a warning sign. Lands on Free, ships from a starter, and hits FR-L3's exit block the first time they place something Pro. **Primary persona**, and the one FR-C1's deferral exists for.
- **The indie founder** — runs a company blog plus a changelog on Ghost; treats the site as marketing surface and time as the scarce thing. Wants a marketing-grade homepage without hiring a designer, and will pay to stop thinking about it. **Credential reach: full** — is the Owner and is fluent in API keys. Most likely to convert on the first exit block rather than swap designs.
- **The creator with taste** — designer-adjacent, opinionated, will open the Style Pack editor on day one, author dark mode deliberately and build routes. The persona most likely to find the ceiling of a closed control vocabulary and the one whose respect the library's quality is aimed at. **Credential reach: full.** Also the likeliest source of suggestions-board traffic.
- **The multi-site operator** — an agency hand or a publisher running several Ghost properties: 3–10 sites, recurring redesign work, the same brand system applied repeatedly. Values FR-B2's duplicate-project path, Site Remix and starters more than any single design. **Credential reach: variable** — frequently works on clients' sites with delegated access; as an Administrator they can mint a token that suffices, as an Editor or Author they cannot, which is exactly the case FR-C1's graceful no-token path serves; a hard token requirement at connect would have locked this persona out of the product entirely. Pro by structure rather than by upsell, and the persona whose churn (Appendix F.6) is driven by client attrition rather than by dissatisfaction.

An internal `admin` claim (Supabase custom claim, not a product role) gates suggestions-board moderation and support tooling; the first one is seeded from the owner's account at setup (FR-M4).

---

## 4. Release Strategy & Definition of Done

- **Single GA release.** No beta, no waitlist-gated feature drip. 100% of this PRD ships before go-live.
- **Production-infrastructure testing (pre-launch)** — owner mandate, must-have. Until go-live and the first customer, all testing runs on the real production infrastructure, exactly as an actual user would experience it: the production domains (**inflozo.com** marketing, **app.inflozo.com** app), production Supabase, real Resend sends, real Dodo transactions (test-mode → live-mode), plus real Ghost deploy targets. ("Live" is reserved for the post-launch environment below; these are Ghost *deploy targets*, not the Live environment.)
  - **T1** — a self-hosted Ghost **6.x** on a DigitalOcean droplet (owner-provided)
  - **T2** — a Ghost(Pro) **Publisher** site
  - **T3** — a self-hosted Ghost **5.x** droplet (owner-provided), retained as a **permanent** compatibility target. Ghost 5.x reached end of life in January 2026, so 5.x parity is a courtesy rather than an obligation — but it is a courtesy the product *advertises* (NFR-7), and a second droplet instance is the whole cost of testing it, so **T3 is not droppable**. Every gate that runs against T1 runs against T3, and the gscan rule sets differ between the v5 and v6 specs, so both are exercised. FR-C2 keeps accepting 5.x *connections* either way
  - **T4** — a Ghost(Pro) **Starter** site (verifies the Preview-only path). **[ASSUMPTION — KNOWN UNTESTED PATH]** T4 is **deferred until after MVP** by owner decision. A self-hosted droplet cannot exercise Ghost(Pro)-only behaviour, so until T4 exists, FR-C2's `hostSettings.limits` probe, the Preview-only state and the deploy-refusal message are all written against a payload **nobody has observed**. **Blocking gate before public launch, or before any marketing aimed at Ghost(Pro) users:** obtain a Ghost(Pro) Starter site (a trial suffices), capture what `GET /admin/config/` actually returns, and verify the detection against that recording. Until that gate clears, this path is documented as untested rather than assumed working

  **Deployable targets are T1–T3.** Wherever an exit criterion says "all Ghost deploy targets (§4)", it means: deploys succeed end-to-end on T1–T3. **T4's criterion is narrower and specific:** a deploy attempt against T4 fails with the friendly Starter message and sets Preview-only — and it is **gated to the post-MVP T4 acquisition above**, not to GA. FR-C2's `hostSettings.limits` probe sets the same flag at connect and clears it when the probe changes, so both the setting and the clearing path are testable on T4 itself; the deploy error remains authoritative.

  This is safe because the product has no users pre-launch. User-journey E2E tests exercise the production stack end-to-end as a real user. Of the four NFR-6 gates, **only two touch a real Ghost**: the canvas-vs-real-Ghost comparison (NFR-6(c3)) and the E2E suite (NFR-6(d)) — both serialized per target, because theme activation is globally stateful, so concurrent runs queue and never interleave. The render matrix (NFR-6(a)) renders locally and needs no Ghost; compile CI (NFR-6(b)) needs only gscan; NFR-6(c1)/(c2) run per-commit against committed snapshots and recorded output.

  **NFR-6(c3) is a dependency of the library epics, not of hardening.** The per-category owner gate below requires a real-Ghost comparison for the category being approved, so the comparison harness and the T1 droplet must exist before the **first** gated category — not at E15. **Cadence (normative):** the full render matrix runs nightly and before each release, per-commit runs cover only the designs a commit touched, and NFR-6(c3) runs nightly over a **risk-weighted rotating batch** sized to the serialization budget, such that every design has been compared against real Ghost within **30 days**. The rotation front-loads the designs most likely to diverge — anything sticky or fixed, the Koenig card treatments, member-state-dependent sections, announcement bars and edge rails. **The CI lane is a costed line item, not an aside** (Appendix F).

  **The §4 test targets are exempt from FR-J11's 10/hour per-site deploy rate limit.** A single DoD run is 30 serialized starter deploys (10 starters × the three deployable targets T1–T3), which the limit alone would stretch past three hours before compile time, and every re-run would pay it again.
- **Definition of Done for GA:** every FR implemented; **every category owner gate passed** (below) — one per category, the shell block's three included, so the count is the category count and never a number maintained separately; every section design passes the render matrix (NFR-6(a)) **and** the NFR-5 accessibility scan; every emitted theme passes the FR-J17 code-quality gate; 10 starter templates deployable end-to-end to all Ghost deploy targets (§4); marketing site live with full sections gallery; billing verified with real Dodo test-mode → live-mode transactions; rollback verified; **docs complete** — every page enumerated in FR-N4 exists, is non-empty, and carries the "Suggest an edit" affordance; **⛔ the Ghost(Pro) gate cleared** — a real Ghost(Pro) Starter `GET /admin/config/` payload captured and FR-C2's Preview-only detection verified against that recording, which also releases any marketing aimed at Ghost(Pro) users (§4 T4, §7.6 items 1–2); and **G1 and G2 measured, not asserted** — G1 (first-deploy time) instrumented and met on the E3 connect path, G2 (the never-lose-content play loop) green on FR-D17's gate. **Both are owned and both are gates, not aspirations:** **E15 owns the measurement** — the moderated 8-user test that produces both p75 figures is a launch gate it runs and cannot waive — while the paths being measured belong to **E3** (connect, and FR-C1's credential deferral is the change that makes G1's ten minutes reachable) and **E5** (the play loop). They are also the *only* gate on the ease-and-play half of §1.2's thesis, which is why they are measured rather than asserted; the beauty half carries no gate at all and is marked as ambition.
- **No de-scope contingency (owner decision, governing):** the full FR scope and the complete section inventory are not de-scopeable. **Quality and correctness outrank schedule** — the owner's stated constraint is *"I am okay if it takes time. But it needs to be perfect and work flawlessly without bugs."* If the library takes longer, **launch moves; scope does not**. Every consequence of that choice is a timeline consequence, and §7.6's risk row and Appendix F's runway are written accordingly. **Nothing in this document is an invitation to descope the library** — that option was considered and declined.
- **The library is designed and specified before it is built (owner decision).** Every design in the section inventory is **fully designed and fully specified before any of it is implemented**. Design and specification are **one pass, not two**: the per-category design session is also the session that fixes that category's content fields, its per-design control sets, its data binding, its responsive collapse rule, its empty states and its shared field list. The method of record is `design/claude-design-prompt-3-library.md`, run once per category. A category's design output is the input its build story is written from, and a category is not ready to build until that output exists.

- **Build order (normative).**
  1. **The FR-D4 mark-emission spike** — first, ahead of everything. Every path by which user text becomes markup depends on it, and it is the one mechanic in §7.3 that has never been demonstrated (§8 E0).
  2. **The E0 platform-verification spike** — **every one** of §7.6's verify-at-build items executed against a real Ghost, before the epics that rest on them (§8 E0). The list is open — it grows whenever this document rests on an unexecuted external fact — so the spike is scoped to the list as it stands when E0 opens, not to a fixed count.
  3. **The complete shell block** — all templates plus every design in Group 1: Headers & Navigation, Announcement Bars, Footers. Not a minimal shell. Nothing else can be seen on a real Ghost site until the shell exists, so it is built first — **and it is gated, not waved through.** Its three categories each carry their own owner gate, on the same terms as every other category, which is what makes the count exactly **as many gates as there are categories** rather than three fewer. The shell is also where gating matters most: a defective header is not one bad design, it is a defect on every page of every site, sitting above every other design in the library.
  4. **Then the gated categories, in inventory order, beginning with Heroes.**

- **The category owner gate (normative).** After each category is built there is a **blocking owner gate**. The next category does not begin until it passes. There are **as many gates as there are categories** — the shell block's three included (build order 3 above), so the count has no exceptions in it — and the owner is the bottleneck **by design** — this is a deliberate quality mechanism, not an accident of resourcing.

  **Automated, producing a single pass/fail sheet:** (1) the category deployed to a real Ghost target and rendered live; (2) NFR-6(c3)'s canvas-vs-real-Ghost comparison green for **every** design in the category, under its stated exclusions; (3) the NFR-5 accessibility scan green on every design; (4) compile CI and the FR-J17 emitted-theme quality gate green; (5) the NFR-6(a) render matrix green across device widths, colour modes and Style Packs; (6) FR-D17's preservation check green across every design in the category — cycle the full design ring with `[` and `]` and confirm no content prop **and no control value** is lost.

  **Then the owner, by hand, as a user:** compose a page from the category's designs, deploy it with the product's own deploy button, view it on the live Ghost site, exercise the controls and confirm the rendered result changes as designed, and approve. **The automated sheet is a precondition for the owner's review, never a substitute for it** — and the owner's review is not a substitute for the sheet.

  Two things follow deliberately. The product's real connect → design → deploy → verify path is exercised once per category rather than once before launch. And a defect in a shared primitive is found at the gate that introduced it, not after the whole library has inherited it.

- **Post-launch topology (Test vs Live):** at go-live, a fresh infrastructure set is provisioned as **Live** (new Supabase project, Vercel production environment, live Dodo) and takes over the production domains; the existing pre-launch stack — including Ghost targets T1–T4 — becomes the permanent **Test** environment on test domains. From the first customer onward, all testing (E2E, render matrix, the NFR-6(c) fidelity layers) runs exclusively against Test; real-user data exists only in Live. Test is a permanent, costed line item, not a pre-launch expense that ends at GA (Appendix F).
- **Cutover migration.** Three record sets are exported from Test and imported into Live at cutover: **suggestions**, their **votes** (FR-M2), and the **dogfood project** that builds Inflozo's own Ghost theme (FR-N5). Everything else starts empty in Live. Without this the public suggestions board — which has been collecting real submissions from real visitors on the real marketing site — resets to zero on launch day.

---

## 5. Functional Requirements

Requirements are numbered `FR-<domain><n>`. "Must" is the default force. Free/Pro gating is defined in Appendix F. Sections are ordered by dependency, not alphabetically — FR-Q (Theme Settings) sits between FR-I and FR-J because routing and theme settings both feed the compiler.

### FR-A · Authentication & Accounts

- **FR-A1** Sign-up and sign-in via **magic link** (Supabase Auth email OTP link) with branded email template. No passwords exist anywhere in the product.
- **FR-A2** **Passkey** support via Supabase Auth's native passkey (WebAuthn) capability. Because Supabase requires a signed-in session to register a passkey and the API is in Beta (as of May 2026; requires `@supabase/supabase-js` ≥ 2.105.0 with explicit opt-in), the flow is: magic link first → post-onboarding nudge + Account → Security page to register a passkey → subsequent sign-ins offer passkey as the primary, magic link as fallback. Feature-flag the passkey module so it can be disabled without redeploy if the experimental API changes.
- **FR-A3** Account → Security lists registered passkeys (auto-named from authenticator AAGUID) with rename and revoke.
- **FR-A4** Email change with re-verification of the new address; a change to an address already registered to another account is rejected upfront ("already in use") before any verification email is sent.
- **FR-A5** Account deletion: cascades projects, templates, assets, connections, and deploy artifacts; suggestions-board posts are anonymized (author becomes "Deleted user", any uploaded image is removed) and the user's votes and notification rows are deleted; honors a 14-day soft-delete window then hard purge (GDPR). Signing in during the soft-delete window offers a one-click "Restore account" that cancels the purge. **Subscription state across the window is explicit:** soft-delete stops the Dodo subscription's auto-renew immediately, so a deleting user is never charged again while the purge is pending, and entitlements are retained at their current level until the purge; restoring within the window offers one-click resume of auto-renew at the same plan and price, and says plainly when the paid period ended meanwhile — that account restores on Free, under FR-L3's over-limit rules. **Pre-Inflozo snapshots are offered as a download before the purge:** every retained snapshot (FR-J13) is listed with its site and the purge **proceeds on the 14-day deadline regardless** of whether they were downloaded or declined. Gating deletion on an action is gating it on an action a departed user will never take — which turns a GDPR commitment into an indefinite retention policy, the precise opposite of what it promises. Snapshots are **offered** throughout the window because the user's live site may still be running an Inflozo theme, and the offer is made at deletion time and in the confirmation email; after the deadline they are gone. **No further email is sent**, deliberately: FR-P2 permits no nudges, a "your data is about to be deleted" reminder to someone who chose to leave is exactly that, and the deadline was stated when they chose it.
- **FR-A6** Sessions persist 30 days rolling; a sign-out-everywhere action is available.

### FR-B · Projects & Dashboard

- **FR-B1** Dashboard shows projects as cards: **a static placeholder image**, project name, linked-site badge (favicon + name) or "Sample content" badge, last-deploy status chip (Live vX / Never deployed / Failed), updated-at. **Auto-captured canvas thumbnails are deferred out of v1** (Appendix G): capturing one means rasterising a live same-origin iframe with the project's own fonts, images and web fonts resolved, at a moment the canvas happens to be idle — and no path through `html2canvas`-style DOM rasterisation or `foreignObject` SVG reproduces a section runtime faithfully enough to be worth showing. A wrong thumbnail on a dashboard is worse than none. **The placeholder is derived from the project's Style Pack** — its accent and surface tokens — so cards stay visually distinguishable without claiming to be a preview. **The conclusion that mattered survives unchanged: no headless browser and no server-side render of the section runtime exists anywhere in the product**, and Appendix F carries no Chromium line item — that now follows from thumbnails not being rendered at all rather than from rendering them in the browser.
- **FR-B2** Create project from: **Starter template** (Appendix E, with full-page scrollable preview in light and dark before choosing), **Blank canvas**, **Duplicate** of an existing project, or **Redesign proposals** (FR-C7, for connected sites). Style Pack chosen at creation (changeable anytime).
- **FR-B3** Rename, duplicate, delete (type-name-to-confirm) on every project. Deleting a project whose theme is live on a connected site warns that the site's active theme and its rollback history came from this project; the project's deploy artifacts are purged with it, while the per-site pre-Inflozo snapshot survives (FR-J13). Renaming after first deploy changes the display name only (FR-J10).
- **FR-B4** Plan caps enforced at creation time with a contextual upgrade prompt (Free: 1 project; Pro: 25).
- **FR-B5** A project targets at most one connected site at a time (switchable). Unlinked projects render the bundled sample dataset (FR-H3).
- **FR-B6** Dashboard also surfaces: a connected sites strip with health badges, an asset quota meter, and a "What's new" changelog popover.
- **FR-B7** **Notifications center:** a bell in the dashboard and editor top bars opens a per-user notification feed — deploy outcomes, site health changes (reconnect needed), **Ghost-compatibility notices (FR-C5)**, billing events (payment failed / grace), library "updates available" notices (FR-J14), and product announcements. Unread badge, mark-all-read, 90-day retention — **site-health and compatibility notices are exempt from the prune** and are retained until read or resolved, because the population they are written for is precisely the population that deployed once and stopped signing in. In-app only except where FR-P1 names an email for the same event; email policy remains FR-P.

### FR-C · Ghost Site Connections

- **FR-C1** **Connect flow — one credential now, two deferred to first deploy.** The user enters their Ghost URL → guided, screenshotted instructions to create a **Custom Integration** in Ghost Admin → the user pastes the **Content API key** and the **Admin API key** from that one integration → Inflozo validates both and the project is connected, previewable and fully designable. **The Staff Access Token is not requested at connect.** It is requested once, at **first deploy**, where its purpose is visible in context and where the user has already seen the product work.

  **Why the split.** Connect previously asked for three credentials before showing anything, against a G1 target of under ten minutes end to end — three separate mints in Ghost Admin, two of them justified by capabilities the user has not yet reached. Worse, the Custom Integration and the Staff Access Token are minted in different places by different rules, and **the Staff Access Token is a per-user credential carrying that user's role** — "Each user can create and refresh their own token" (docs.ghost.org/admin-api, read 2026-09-03), found on the user's own profile. **An Administrator's token carries everything Inflozo needs** — `theme: all` and `setting: all`, identical to the Owner's for theme download, `routes.yaml` and activation (Ghost `fixtures.json` roles_permissions, read 2026-09-03); an Editor's (`theme: browse, readActive`) does not. **The Owner is not required.** The user who connects a site is often an Editor or Author on it, and cannot become an Administrator in the moment. Inflozo may verify the role with `GET /admin/users/me/?include=roles` on the token. Where this document says "the Owner's Staff Access Token" it means a token of Owner-or-Administrator reach; the executed proofs (VERIFY-AT-BUILD items 9 and 21) used the Owner's. Deferring turns a hard stop at minute zero into a question at the point the answer matters.

  **The graceful no-token path is a designed path, not a degradation notice.** Without the Staff Access Token, Ghost's integration-token allowlist blocks every `GET /themes/*` call, so three things change and **nothing is silently skipped**: there is no **pre-Inflozo snapshot** (FR-J13 offers its designed alternative — the user downloads their current theme from Ghost Admin themselves, and Inflozo says exactly why and exactly how), no **pre-deploy drift check** (FR-J16 fails open, reporting "couldn't verify" rather than blocking), and no **automated `routes.yaml` upload** (FR-I4 falls back to its guided manual card, which is a first-class surface). Deploy itself is unaffected: the Admin API key uploads and activates a theme on its own. **A user may decline the token permanently and still ship**, and the deploy screen states which three protections they are declining, once, without nagging.

  **A project may therefore be partially credentialed, and that is a first-class state** (§7.5): the connection record carries which credentials exist, FR-C8's key management can add the token later at any time, and adding it retroactively enables the snapshot from that point forward — it cannot reconstruct a snapshot of a theme that has already been replaced, and the UI does not pretend otherwise.
- **FR-C2** Validation (server-side): mint the short-lived Admin JWT and call the **authenticated** `GET /admin/config/` endpoint. `GET /admin/site/` must **not** be used to validate a key — it is mounted on Ghost's public admin router, whose middleware chain contains no authentication step, so it returns 200 for a fabricated, malformed or revoked key and validates nothing. `/config/` proves the credential, reports the Ghost `version` (5.x and 6.x accepted; 4.x and older rejected with a "please update Ghost" message), and carries `hostSettings`. The Content API key is verified client-side with a `settings` read. **Plan awareness is probed, not asked:** on a Ghost(Pro) site `hostSettings.limits.customThemes` reports whether custom theme upload is permitted — its absence (self-hosted) means unlimited — so the connect flow marks a site **Preview-only** automatically where the limit forbids custom themes (**Ghost(Pro) Starter**, in Ghost's 2026 Starter/Publisher/Business lineup), and clears the flag automatically when a later probe (FR-C5's daily check) reports otherwise. The shape is undocumented and host-controlled, so the probe carries a fallback: where `hostSettings` is present but unreadable, the flow asks the user which plan the site is on. **The deploy error remains authoritative:** a rejected theme upload fails with a friendly explanation, a "check or upgrade your Ghost(Pro) plan (Publisher or higher)" prompt and a docs link, and sets Preview-only; a successful deploy clears it. There is no user-declared-plan step in the happy path and no plan field in Manage keys (FR-C8). Live-content editing requires the site to be reachable over HTTPS from the browser (Content API fetches are client-side): `http://` URLs are warned at connect, and when client-side Content API reads persistently fail for a validated connection (CORS proxy, mixed content), the editor names the cause and falls back to sample content rather than failing silently. **Code-injection notice:** connect computes one boolean — whether the site's settings carry head or foot code injection — and, when true, surfaces a one-time notice that the live page can legitimately differ from the canvas in ways Inflozo does not control (§1.2). **Connect also reads Ghost's announcement-bar settings** (content, background, visibility — Admin API only) so FR-C4 can offer them as a seed, and **whether Portal's floating subscribe button is enabled**, because the canvas shims it (FR-H5) and shimming a button that is switched off would be its own fidelity defect. **If the setting cannot be read, Inflozo asks** — one question at connect, defaulting to *on*, since Ghost enables the button on essentially every site. The answer is stored on the connection (§7.5) and re-read by the daily health check (FR-C5), so a site owner who turns it off later stops seeing it on the canvas. Only the flag is computed, server-side and discarded; the injected content is never returned to a client, never stored and never enters a render path, so NFR-3's never-read rule over the canvas stands.
- **FR-C3** **Key security, and an honest statement of what is being asked for.** Admin API keys and the Owner Staff Access Token are encrypted at rest with Supabase Vault and **never sent to any client**; all Admin API calls are proxied through server routes that mint the required short-lived JWT per request. The Content API key is browser-safe by Ghost's design and is delivered to the editor for direct client-side content fetches. **No Admin-scoped credential is ever written into a theme, a custom setting or a rendered page** (NFR-3).

  **The Staff Access Token is a full-Administrator credential, and the product says so where it is asked for.** It is not a scoped theme-read grant — Ghost has no such thing, which is the entire reason the token is needed at all — so holding it means Inflozo *could* do anything the site Owner could. What constrains Inflozo is **P8**, which is a product commitment rather than a permission boundary, plus the storage posture above. Stating that plainly at the request point is the only honest version: a user handing over Owner-level access deserves to know it is Owner-level, and a user who declines still gets a product that ships (FR-C1). **Neither §1.4 #5's safe-installs promise nor P8 may be written as though the credential were narrower than it is.**
- **FR-C4** **Auto-branding:** on successful connect, fetch site settings (title, description, logo, icon, cover image, accent color, navigation — and, on the same read, Portal's floating-button state for FR-C2 and the **announcement bar's content, background and visibility**, which are Admin-API-only and absent from both `@site` and the Content API) and present a one-click "Use your brand" card that seeds the active project's Style Pack accent + logo + nav and switches the canvas to live content. **Where the site has an announcement bar set, it is offered as a seed too** (Appendix A §2): its text becomes the message prop of a placed A2 design, `announcement_visibility` maps onto the **show to** control and `announcement_background` onto the Background role, after which it is an ordinary Inflozo section — server-rendered, editable, working with JavaScript off. **Seeding is a copy, never a live binding**: Ghost's announcement text is not readable at render time (it is outside the public settings allowlist and Ghost's own bar fetches it client-side), so a live binding would make the bar's content JS-dependent on an undocumented members endpoint. Once seeded, Inflozo offers a one-click **"turn Ghost's own bar off"** — safe because the copy already lives in Inflozo, and consented rather than silent (P8). Declining leaves both bars stacked, which is a legitimate choice. Skippable; re-runnable later from the Style panel.
- **FR-C5** Multi-site: Free 1 connection, Pro 10. A daily background health check re-validates the stored credentials against `GET /admin/config/`, re-runs version detection (so a Ghost version upgrade lifts stale version-dependent behavior without reconnecting), re-runs FR-C2's `hostSettings.limits.customThemes` probe (so Preview-only sets and clears without any user action), and **re-reads the live `routes.yaml`** to detect routes drift against what Inflozo last offered (FR-I4); failures set a "Reconnect needed" badge. The email notice sends once per healthy→unhealthy transition (at most one reminder), is suppressed while the outage persists, and resets on recovery — subsequent failures log to the notifications center (FR-B7) only. Regardless of transitions, at most **one health email per site per rolling 7 days** (a flapping site logs to the notifications center, never to email). **Compatibility watch — one broadcast per Ghost release, all plans:** on each Ghost release Inflozo re-verifies the shipped library against it using each design's `ghostCompat` (FR-G3) and posts a single notice to every account — normally a confirmation that the library was verified, and where the release does affect shipped designs, a notice naming the affected categories and recommending a redeploy through FR-J14. There is no per-site analysis and no per-release compatibility data to author, and the watch is not Pro-gated because the risk is not plan-dependent. That notice — which names the affected shipped designs — is the one compatibility carve-out FR-P2 allows to reach email (FR-P1). **A compatibility redeploy is a first-class exempt path:** it re-ships a design that already ran, so — exactly like rollback (FR-J9) — it does not require the edit lock (FR-D18), is not gated behind FR-J14's mandatory library-update confirm, and is not blocked by FR-L3's Pro-section exit rule on a lapsed account. A redeploy that would also carry unshipped editor changes is an ordinary deploy and is gated normally. **The exemption is narrow, and its narrowness is what keeps FR-J14's consent promise true: a compatibility redeploy carries *only* the compatibility fix.** It never picks up a pending library advance, a Style Pack change or an unconfirmed design update that happens to be waiting — each of those still requires FR-J14's explicit confirm. A redeploy that silently shipped more than it announced would make "nothing changes without your say-so" simply false, and the reason this path can be exempt at all is that the user gets **exactly** what they were told about. The deploy record distinguishes a compatibility redeploy from a library-update redeploy (§7.5), so the two cannot be conflated after the fact. **Portal's floating-button state and the announcement-bar settings are re-read on the same schedule** (FR-C2), so both canvas shims track the site rather than a connect-time snapshot — a bar the site owner switched off in Ghost admin stops appearing on the canvas without a reconnect (FR-H5).
- **FR-C6** Disconnecting a site never deletes projects; affected projects fall back to sample content. Disconnecting also never deletes the site's pre-Inflozo snapshot: **snapshots are bound to the site record id, not the raw URL** (FR-J13), so a disconnected site's record and its snapshot are retained and re-adopted when that same record is reconnected. Reconnecting a URL for which no record is retained creates a **new** site record with no snapshot association — Inflozo never adopts a snapshot on a URL match alone, because the same URL can serve a different Ghost install after a staging cutover or a rebuild; the new record captures its own snapshot at Inflozo's first upload to it. A snapshot whose site record has been disconnected for **90 days** is an orphan and is purged, after a notice and a download offer — **and, per the FR-A5 rule it inherits, the purge proceeds on its deadline whether or not the offer was taken.**
- **FR-C7** **Redesign proposals:** after connect + auto-branding, offer a one-click "redesign my site" pass that renders 2–3 starter + Style Pack combinations populated with the user's real content; picking one creates the project pre-assembled. Variety is an acceptance criterion: proposals must differ in layout structure, not merely palette. Re-runnable: the same flow is available anytime from the dashboard's "New project" path for any connected site, not just during onboarding.
- **FR-C8** **Key rotation & reconnect:** per-site "Manage keys" flow — re-paste the Custom Integration's Admin and Content keys and the Owner's Staff Access Token, test the connection, save re-encrypts to Vault (FR-C3). **It handles a partially credentialed site as an ordinary state, not an error** (FR-C1): each credential shows present or absent with what it enables, the token can be added at any time or removed again, and removing it degrades the three token-dependent capabilities rather than disconnecting the site. Surfaced from the site card and from every "Reconnect needed" state. **A site's URL is immutable — there is no edit-URL-in-place.** A domain move is a **disconnect + reconnect**: the user connects the new URL as a new site, pastes that site's own keys, and Inflozo re-reads its content (posts, pages, tags, authors, tiers, settings) against the new record; projects are re-pointed with FR-B5's site switcher and the old record follows FR-C6's orphan rule. Editing a URL in place would carry one site's record, snapshot and first-upload flag onto a different live Ghost install — so "restore the original theme" could push one site's archived theme over another's, and the new site would never be snapshotted at all — which is why the affordance does not exist rather than being guarded. There is no plan field in this flow: Preview-only is probed, not declared (FR-C2). A new-site connect whose credentials match an existing record still hints "Moved domains? Reconnect the new URL and re-point your projects — your old site's snapshot is kept for 90 days."

### FR-D · Editor Core

- **FR-D1** Layout: slim top bar; collapsible left **Layers** panel; center **canvas**; right **Controls** sidebar. With nothing hovered/selected the canvas is a pixel-faithful render of the site (P1). Panels may remain visible; the canvas itself carries zero editing chrome. **The editor is a desktop and tablet surface, and the floor is designed rather than discovered** *(owner's ruling **R-76**, 2026-09-03, taken at step 5 because neither this document nor the design export settled it)*. **The floor is a device test, not a width test**, and the distinction is load-bearing: a 1440 px display at 200% browser zoom presents roughly a 720 px CSS viewport, so keying on width alone would evict a low-vision user for zooming — a **WCAG 1.4.4** failure and the opposite of what this ruling is for, which is phones (NFR-5). So: **on a coarse pointer at a small viewport** a project does not open into this layout — it opens onto a designed notice that says the editor needs a wider screen and offers what genuinely works from a phone (the project's deploy history with one-tap rollback — FR-J9 — the sites list and billing); and **on a desktop-class device the editor holds at 200 % browser zoom**, reflowing rather than redirecting. **This is browser zoom, which Inflozo does not own and may not defeat — not the canvas scale FR-D14 refuses**, and the two never meet. **The Dashboard, Sign In, Billing, Suggestions and every marketing page stay fully usable at 390**, which is how the export drew them; the editor and its satellites — Section Picker, Style Packs, Deploy, Routes, Assets, Theme Settings, Editor Cards and the template surfaces — were drawn at 1440 only. This is a decision about Inflozo's own chrome and says nothing about the sites it builds, which are responsive at 1440 / 834 / 390 by archetype (FR-D8, `R Responsive System`).
- **FR-D2** Hover state on a section: 1px outline, floating name tag, and quick actions — **Previous / Next design** ◀ ▶ (FR-D19), Duplicate, Delete, drag handle. Between sections, a hairline "+" insertion affordance appears on hover.
- **FR-D3** Click selects a section (persistent outline + sidebar controls). Esc deselects. Clicking a text element inside a selected section enters inline editing.
- **FR-D4** **Inline editing:** every text prop is editable directly on canvas (line breaks honored; per-prop character limits from the section schema). Selecting text raises a small floating toolbar (Koenig/Medium-style) offering exactly four inline marks — **bold, italic, underline, link** (link via the Link Picker) — available on any text prop, headlines included (exception: a prop bound to a Ghost Admin text setting is plain-text-locked while bound — FR-Q3). **Rich-text model:** a text prop's value is stored as **text plus marks in a JSON structure** — a plain string with an ordered list of `{start, end, mark, href?}` ranges over exactly those four marks — never as an HTML string. Canvas editing is raw `contenteditable` mapped to schema props by data-attributes (§7.3), so no editor library's DOM enters the iframe and the rendered markup stays byte-comparable to shipped markup; the sidebar's Content field edits the same structure, which is why both editors can show marks. The compiler is the single serialization point: it escapes the text and emits the mark ranges as `<strong>/<em>/<u>/<a>`, so unescaped user HTML can never reach the theme (FR-J1's inert-emission rule), and FR-Q3's plain-text lock is a truncation of the mark list rather than a parse. Compiled output is clean `<strong>/<em>/<u>/<a>`; pasted content is stripped to these marks. Every text prop is *also* editable in the Content group of the sidebar (owner requirement). Clicking an image opens the asset picker popover. **A prop's mark allow-list is declared per prop, and so is its inline-token list (ruling R-27, AD-4).** The stored record carries the mark ranges *and* `newTab` / `rel` for a link mark (D9). **Inline binding tokens are per-field and closed** — a prop declares exactly which it accepts, the panel shows them, and every other brace pair is literal.
- **FR-D5** **Layers panel:** ordered section list for the active template; drag to reorder with live canvas follow; per-instance rename; duplicate/delete; visibility toggle (hidden sections are excluded from compilation but retained). Site-wide sections (headers, announcement bars, footers) are single shared instances: they appear in every template's Layers panel as a pinned "Site-wide" group with a globe badge, and editing them changes every template (they compile into `default.hbs`). Site-wide singletons cannot be duplicated; deleting or hiding one asks a confirm noting it affects every template (hiding the footer still triggers FR-J15's Free credit fallback). **A second class of singleton is per-template rather than site-wide: a layout may carry at most one Post Content section** (A25, ruling R-37). The Section Picker **refuses the second placement and says why** — "this layout already prints the article" — rather than accepting it and warning later; two would print the article twice on the live page (FR-I1). **Non-placeable treatments never appear in Layers:** the 28 designs of A32 (Paywall / Content CTA), A33 (Koenig card treatments) and A34 (pagination styles) are chosen elsewhere — the Paywall editor (FR-H6), the **card design module** (FR-Q7), and the designated main feed's Pagination control (FR-H2) — each reachable from Theme Settings when its host is absent (FR-Q9) — apply once per project, and are excluded from placement, drag, reorder, duplicate and delete (`sections-inventory.md` § Placeable sections vs non-placeable treatments). **CTA-bearing sections carry a member-visibility control** — show to Everyone / Logged out / Free members / Paid members — evaluated against `@member` on the live site and previewed via FR-D16; this is how a member ask is placed on a canvas now that the paywall is a template surface rather than a section. **Hiding every section is not emptying:** a fully hidden template is designed and compiles an empty body; only removing every section returns a template to untouched (FR-I1).
- **FR-D6** **Template switcher** in the top bar: Home, Post, Page, Tag archive, Author archive, **Membership (Signup / Signin / Member Home)**, 404, a conditional **Private** canvas, plus any custom templates created in the Routes Manager (FR-I). **The membership canvases are custom page templates, not a members template family:** Ghost 6 has no `members/*.hbs` family and never rendered one (`research-ghost-membership-pages.md` §1, §9), so each compiles to `custom-{name}.hbs` at the theme root and is bound to an ordinary Ghost Page by the user picking it from the **Template** dropdown in Ghost's page editor — **guided by FR-I6's post-deploy flow**, which names the filename and the derived label rather than leaving the user to know — no `routes.yaml`, no Routes Manager visit, and no Ghost Admin step beyond that one choice (FR-I1, FR-I5). The Private canvas follows the same conditional pattern: it appears once a Private Site Gate (Appendix A §31) is called for, opens empty, and compiles `private.hbs` only when designed (FR-I1) — without it that design is a launch deliverable with no surface that can create it. **Synthesis applies to exactly seven templates** — `home.hbs`, `index.hbs`, `post.hbs`, `page.hbs`, `tag.hbs`, `author.hbs`, `error.hbs` (FR-I1 emits `home.hbs`, so it is synthesizable like the rest): untouched, these compile from the normative **Synthesis Defaults** (`sections-inventory.md` § Synthesis Defaults — per-template default section stacks, all [Free] designs, with inheritance rules), so a home-only design still produces a complete, coherent theme: header/footer/style are inherited from the Home canvas by reference, body stacks come from the defaults tables, and archive defaults ship a designated main feed (FR-H2). Every other canvas — the membership pages, Private, and every custom template — is **never synthesized**: it opens empty and emits nothing until designed. Opening one of the six untouched templates renders its default stack as the starting canvas; the first edit materializes that stack into the project doc. **Synthesized templates are visible, and marked (M4).** All six render on the canvas exactly as they will compile, each carrying an **auto-generated** marker in the top bar and in the Layers panel until the first edit materializes the stack. Synthesis is **not** a deploy-time-only mechanism: nothing ships that the user could not have looked at first, which removes a carve-out from §1.2's promise rather than adding one, and it puts the six inside NFR-5's accessibility scan and NFR-6(a)'s render matrix like any other canvas. **The Post and Page canvases are peers.** The Post Content section (Appendix A §25) is placeable, designable and design-pickable on **both**, with the same design ring and the same per-design controls on each — a page's reading experience is designed, not inherited from the post one, and the two canvases hold independent placements even where their synthesized defaults coincide.
- **FR-D7** **Light/Dark authoring:** sun/moon toggle re-renders the canvas in the other mode. Mode-scoped controls (Background role, per-mode image swaps, mode-specific toggles) apply to the active mode only and display a moon badge when a dark override exists; "Clear dark overrides" per section. Project setting: **Light only** or **Light + Dark** (default: **Light + Dark** — every preset ships a hand-paired dark palette, so dark is prepaid). Light-only projects hide the toggle and compile without dark support. **No visitor-facing dark-mode switch is offered on a site whose colour scheme is pinned** (ruling R-34): pinning is the owner's deliberate choice, and a visitor switch that overrides it makes the pin meaningless. Where `color_scheme` is Light or Dark rather than Auto (FR-Q5), the `mode-toggle` control is **not offered and the panel says why** — it is not drawn disabled, because the design that carries it is simply not a design this project can use. Switching a Light + Dark project to Light only keeps existing dark overrides inert — nothing is deleted; they reapply if dark is re-enabled. Switching between Light only and Light + Dark never affects the custom-settings cap — all three built-ins are declared **and referenced** on every project (FR-Q5), so the mode switch changes what the theme *shows*, never what it *declares*.
- **FR-D8** **Device preview:** Desktop / Tablet (834) / Mobile (390) view toggles — display only, and unrelated to a **Preview-only** connection (Appendix I). Sections are auto-responsive; there is no per-breakpoint editing.
- **FR-D9** Undo/redo: 100-step history covering content, controls, ordering, shuffles, and Style Pack changes. **Because control schemas are per design (FR-F7), a shuffle changes the active schema**; undo entries therefore restore the parked value alongside the design, and an entry whose design no longer exists in the library no-ops gracefully rather than half-applying. The undo stack persists locally (per browser) across reloads and sessions — a deleted section is recoverable with all its customizations via undo even after a reload (persistence model in FR-D10; durability is bounded by the browser — the app requests persistent storage via `navigator.storage.persist()`, but an evicting browser can still clear it, so the promise is scoped to "while browser storage survives"). Undo entries whose section schema no longer matches the current library no-op gracefully with a notice (never corrupt state). On a hydrate that **supersedes** the local document — a takeover (FR-D18), or any lock acquisition where the cloud snapshot's revision differs from the local doc's base revision — the local undo journal is **cleared**: its entries reference a superseded document and must never replay (AD1). An ordinary reload that re-acquires the lock against a matching revision keeps its journal, which is what makes undo-after-reload real rather than nominal. The testable claim is scoped to match: **the journal is present and replayable after a reload within the same session**; loss to browser eviction is a stated limitation, not a requirement.
- **FR-D10** **Local-first persistence:** every change is written immediately to local browser storage — writes never block the UI, and editing speed is unaffected (NFR-1). Cloud sync to Supabase runs periodically (default: every 3 minutes), on tab close, on lock release (FR-D18), and before any deploy or export. Manual save (⌘S) is always available. The periodic cloud autosave can be toggled per user; local persistence is always on. Indicator states: Saved locally / Syncing / Synced / Retrying (offline retry queue) — state names; the printed labels are Appendix H's ("Saved on this device" for the first). If local storage is unavailable or a write fails (quota, private mode), the editor falls back to immediate per-change cloud sync and says so in the indicator — never a false "Saved locally". (Fallback mode is a degraded state exempt from NFR-1's no-added-latency clause; the indicator makes the mode visible. AD3's rejection of per-change sync applies to the default path only.) Turning the periodic cloud autosave off shows an explicit data-loss warning (browser storage can be evicted). On lock acquisition (FR-D18) the cloud snapshot is authoritative **whenever its revision differs from the local doc's base revision**: the local doc is replaced before editing resumes, so a stale local copy never overwrites synced work — while a matching revision (the ordinary reload) hydrates in place and keeps the undo journal (FR-D9). (Mechanism: `addendum.md` §AD1.)
- **FR-D11** Keyboard: ⌘K insert section · [ and ] previous / next design · ⌘D duplicate · Del delete · ⌘Z / ⇧⌘Z · ⌘S save now · 1/2/3 device preview · L layers · . (period) dark toggle · Esc deselect. **The states added later carry no shortcut, deliberately:** the paginated preview (FR-D21), the preview subject (FR-D22), the auto-generated marker (FR-D6) and the member-state toggle (FR-D16) are all set-and-forget context, not per-edit actions, and the map above is reserved for what a user does repeatedly inside one session.
- **FR-D12** **Section Picker:** full-screen overlay with left category rail, search, and live previews rendered *in the project's current Style Pack* with the project's content source — the user browses the library already wearing their brand. Free/Pro badges; Free users can add any Pro section — the canvas is open; enforcement happens at the exits (FR-L3). Insertion lands at the invoked position. **The Picker filters on context:** it offers only designs whose `bindingContext` and `compileTarget` (FR-G3) match the template being edited, so a section binding a resource the template does not have — or compiling into a different file — is never presented (FR-H7's prevention rule, applied to placement). The 28 non-placeable treatments (A32/A33/A34) are absent from the rail entirely.
- **FR-D13** **Variant Shuffle:** cycles the selected section through its category's designs in place, **carrying content props and control values under FR-D19's carry / park / default rule** — **restricted to designs sharing the placed instance's `bindingContext` and `compileTarget`** (FR-G3), so a shuffle never lands on a design that binds a resource the current template lacks or that compiles into a different file. Where a category's designs differ on those values (A29 tag vs author, A31 error vs private vs custom page) the ring is **partitioned** and the arrows cycle only the matching partition. **A30 is partitioned on a different axis:** its three membership surfaces — signup · signin · member home — share one `bindingContext` and one `compileTarget` (`custom-{name}.hbs`, FR-I1), so FR-G3's mechanical partition does not separate them; A30 therefore declares an explicit **surface** partition that Shuffle and Site Remix never cross, because those surfaces differ in form markup and content model rather than in render context. The shared per-category content model remaps the user's content; props a design doesn't use are preserved invisibly and restored if shuffled back. The same rule governs list items: a design renders only as many items as its structure fits (8 items shuffled into a 3-card layout shows 3); surplus items are preserved invisibly and reappear on shuffling back, and the sidebar shows the count ("8 items · 3 shown in this design"). Works via hover arrows, sidebar carousel, and [ ] keys.
- **FR-D14** Canvas is site-width, fit-to-viewport with vertical scroll. No zoom in v1. No hard cap on sections per template (a deliberate contrast to competitors' structural caps); the editor must hold NFR-1 performance on the 40-section stress fixture (NFR-1); beyond it, behavior degrades gracefully — slower is acceptable, lockups and state corruption are not. **"Lockup" is bounded as any main-thread block over 5 seconds**, so the clause has a pass/fail condition on the stress fixture.
- **FR-D15** Content-source pill: "Previewing with: {site} / Sample content", switchable when a site is linked. Once a site is linked the pill also names the **preview subject** — which post, page, tag or author the single-resource canvases are rendering (FR-D22) — because "previewing with your site" is not enough information when the choice of subject changes the markup.
- **FR-D16** **Member-state preview, and the `@member` shape.** View canvas as Anonymous / Free member / Paid member — Appendix B's fourth `@member` status, `comped`, **previews as Paid**, differing in billing rather than access; members-aware sections (nav auth links, subscribe CTAs, paywall) re-render accordingly — **and so does Ghost's shimmed announcement strip** (FR-H5), which follows `announcement_visibility`, so a bar set to paid members only is absent while previewing as Anonymous. **The bindable member object is `uuid`, `email`, `name`, `firstname`, `avatar_image`, `subscriptions`, `paid`, `status`** — and is `null` when logged out. `firstname`, `avatar_image` and `subscriptions` are exactly what a member-home design needs, and were missing from the catalog. **`{{#has any="@member"}}` is always false** and must never be emitted as a logged-in test. Gated post bodies are **never** previewed from the connected site: the browser-safe Content API never returns members-only content, so a gated body renders the **style-guide fixture** like every other body (FR-H3, FR-H4) behind a "gated content — shown with sample text" indicator, and the NFR-6(c) fidelity layers exclude gated-body equivalence. This holds with or without a linked site — the fixture is the body on every canvas, so the gated state differs in the *indicator and the surrounding member-aware chrome*, not in the prose.

  **A nudge names the member states you have not looked at.** The failure this prevents is *forgetting*, not switching: the show-to control (FR-D5) multiplies states across a canvas, and a design checked only as Anonymous ships a logged-in state nobody has seen. The editor tracks which member states each canvas has been viewed in and surfaces the unchecked combinations — as a quiet marker beside the toggle, and again in the pre-deploy check (FR-J13) where it is still cheap to act on. It never blocks, and it is what makes the owner's manual member-state pass at each category gate (§4) reliable rather than dependent on memory.
- **FR-D17** **Site Remix:** one action re-rolls the whole canvas — Style Pack and/or every placed section's design — with all content preserved via the shared per-category content model (FR-D13). **Every re-roll obeys FR-D13's context restriction:** a section is only ever replaced by a design sharing its `bindingContext` and `compileTarget`, on every template Remix touches, so a one-click re-roll can never move a section onto a resource its template does not have or into a different compiled file.
