Scoped re-roll (pack only / designs only), single-step undo, and never-lose-content are hard requirements. **"Never lose content" covers control values as well as content props** (FR-D19): a control the outgoing design declares and the incoming one does not is **parked**, not discarded, and returning to that design restores it. The gate is mechanical — **zero loss of content props *and* control values across N shuffles and a full re-roll on the 40-section stress fixture**. Site-wide singletons (header/announcement/footer) are excluded from Remix unless explicitly included, and the non-placeable treatments (A32/A33/A34) are never re-rolled.
- **FR-D18** **Edit lock (single active editor):** one editing context per project across tabs, browsers, and devices. Opening the project elsewhere yields read-only mode with a "Request editing" nudge. On accept, the current holder syncs its changes to the cloud, releases the lock, and becomes read-only; the requester gains edit rights. Locks carry a heartbeat whose payload includes the holder's **unsynced-edit count** — user-perceived edits, never raw operations; `addendum.md` §AD2 states the ops→edits mapping (a shuffle is one edit however many ops it costs), because this number appears verbatim in the string that tells a user what they lost. If a nudge goes unanswered, the requester is notified — "No response; that session has X unsaved edits" — and may take over anyway: the lock transfers, the requester refreshes to the last synced snapshot, and starts editing. A revived former holder becomes read-only and is told concretely what was lost — the message states the count in edits ("That session had 14 unsaved edits; they were not included"); those edits are not recoverable (the device's local journal is cleared on its next hydrate — FR-D9/AD1). **Deploy and ZIP export require the edit lock:** from a read-only session, invoking Ship it or Export first prompts a take-over — surfacing "X unsaved edits exist elsewhere" — so a stale cloud snapshot can never silently ship. (Mechanism: `addendum.md` §AD2.)

- **FR-D19** **Design navigation (the library's primary control):** every placed section exposes its category's full design ring. The user moves through it with **`[` and `]`**, with **Previous/Next design** arrows on the section itself, or by clicking a thumbnail in the sidebar's **Design** picker — which shows position ("Design 7 of 18") so the ring is legible rather than endless. **"Design" is the only term** for what was variously called a layout, a variation or a design; the picker selects among a category's designs and is **not** a sub-arrangement within one design (Appendix C). It writes no `data-*` attribute — it swaps which design renders — which is what separates it from every other control and from FR-D13's Shuffle, whose difference is that Shuffle chooses for you.

  **Controls are per-design (§7.3, FR-F3), so switching must not silently discard settings.** The rule is **carry, park, default**: a control present in both designs **carries** its value; a control present only in the design being left is **parked** — retained against that design so that returning restores it exactly; a control present only in the design being entered takes its **default**. Parking is what makes browsing the ring safe: `]` `]` `]` and back again returns the user to precisely what they had. **FR-D17's preservation gate covers control values as well as content props**, and a lost parked value fails it.

- **FR-D20** **Preview toggle (behaviours are off while designing):** section **behaviours do not run on the canvas** during editing. Layout-affecting CSS — sticky, hover, transitions — is always live, because it changes what the design *is*; JavaScript behaviour is not, because a rotator that swaps content mid-edit, a marquee that slides text from under the cursor or a modal that opens instead of selecting are hostile to editing rather than helpful. A **Preview toggle** hides all editing chrome and runs everything for real, and returns. **Each behaviour module declares itself `edit-safe` or not**, and **the table it is declared in is `research-section-js-libraries.md` §7, one row per module** — this requirement cited a §7.3 table that **did not exist** until the 2026-08-27 merge, which is why the per-design specs each asserted their own value and disagreed (ruling R-21, register item 50). **The sense:** a module is `edit-safe` if it may run inside the editor canvas without interfering with editing. Edit-safe modules — sticky/shrink headers, scroll reveal, tabs, accordions — run always, because designing their open and closed states requires it; the rest are **suppressed on the canvas and their section renders in its resting state** until Preview. `lightbox` is **not** edit-safe: a modal opening over the canvas when the user clicks an image to edit its caption is the defining case. The declaration is part of the module contract, not a per-instance setting.

- **FR-D21** **Paginated preview state:** any canvas showing a paginated feed can be previewed **on page 2 as well as page 1**. Without it the pagination treatments are undesignable in the editor at all — every one of them shows its meaningful state (previous links, page numbers, cursor position) only beyond the first page, and FR-H2's main-feed designation decides which feed paginates. The shim (FR-H5) supplies a page-2 context, and the fixture (FR-H3) carries enough posts for a second page to exist under the project's `posts_per_page` (FR-Q1) — **page 2 is deliberately a middle page**, carrying both a previous and a next link, which is the only state in which a numbered treatment shows its full range. NFR-6(c3)'s comparison exercises a page-2 URL.

- **FR-D22** **Preview subject:** every single-resource canvas — Post, Page, Tag archive, Author archive — renders **one** chosen resource, and which one is a stated choice rather than an accident of query order. **Untouched, and always before a site is linked, the subject is the fixture** (FR-H3): the style-guide post on Post, the style-guide page on Page, and a fixed Orbit Weekly tag and author on the archives. **Once a site is connected the user may override the subject** from the same post/grid picker the Data group uses (FR-H2), and the choice is per canvas, persisted with the project, and named in the content-source pill (FR-D15). It is not cosmetic: FR-H8's media guards mean a subject **with** a feature image and one **without** produce structurally different markup, so an unstated subject makes the canvas non-reproducible and NFR-6(c1)'s snapshot diffs noisy. A subject that disappears from the connected site (deleted, unpublished, or newly gated) falls back to the fixture and says so, rather than emptying the canvas.

### FR-E · Style Packs

- **FR-E1** **Every pack token is either COMPUTED or AUTHORED, and `Appendix D` says which per row** (ruling R-32). Roughly twenty categories need values this list did not define — on-contrast text, dark elevation, scrim strength, tabular figures, pill radius, drop-cap ratio — and most **follow** from colours the pack already declares: the readable text colour on a contrast ground is calculable from that ground, not a taste question. Those are computed by the token system and never asked for. The pack author is asked only for the handful that are **genuine judgement** — scrim strength and pill radius. Two or three real decisions per pack rather than twelve, and twelve packs cannot disagree about a value none of them chose. **A new token enters `Appendix D` marked computed or authored; there is no third state.**

  Token set per pack: colors ×2 modes (background, surface, text, muted text, border, accent, on-accent), heading font + body font (from a curated pool of **30 Google Fonts pairings, enumerated in Appendix D** — self-hosted in generated themes at compile, FR-J3; each pairing declares its two families, the weights shipped, and whether it ships as a variable or a static face, because all three determine `@font-face` emission and bundle size), radius scale (Sharp/Soft/Round), spacing density (Compact/Comfortable/Airy — a pack-level token, distinct from the per-section Vertical spacing scale Compact/Comfortable/Spacious), **site width (Narrow/Normal/Wide) + gutters (Tight/Normal/Loose — its own scale, deliberately not the per-section Vertical spacing labels)** (sections span the site width by default and stay responsive within it), button style (Solid/Soft/Outline/Pill), shadow level (None/Subtle/Lifted), link style (Underline/Accent).
- **FR-E2** **12 curated presets** ship, each with hand-tuned paired light + dark palettes (Appendix D). Switching packs restyles the entire canvas live — this is a designed "wow" moment (≤ 300 ms crossfade).
- **FR-E3** Every token is user-editable per project (color pickers and font pairing list live here and only here — P2). Per-mode palette editing. Custom packs are deliberately per-project — there is no account-level custom pack library in v1 (duplicate a project to carry a look forward). Token edits run a live AA contrast check on token pairings and warn on failures (mirroring FR-Q5's promotion caution) — a warning, never a block.
- **FR-E4** Tokens compile to CSS custom properties in the generated theme: light values on `:root`, dark values under **one** declared selector list, and nothing else in the theme selects on mode at all. **Three inputs decide the mode, and their precedence is normative** — the visitor's explicit choice wins, then the site owner's pinned setting, then the system preference:

  1. **System preference** — `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)`, pure CSS, so it works with JavaScript disabled. This is what `color_scheme: Auto` means; Auto is not a third palette, it is the absence of a pin.
  2. **The owner's pin** — `color_scheme` set to Light or Dark in Ghost Admin. It is **server-rendered as a body class** (`scheme-light` / `scheme-dark`), because that is the one mode signal present in the HTML Ghost sends and it composes with `{{body_class}}` (FR-Q5, FR-J5).
  3. **The visitor's override** — `[data-mode="dark"]` / `[data-mode="light"]`, set by the `mode-toggle` module (FR-G7) when a visitor picks a mode explicitly, and persisted for them.

  **The two markers are layers, not rivals.** A body class is the only thing a server can write; a data attribute is what a runtime override should write, since it must beat the class without racing it in the cascade. Both resolve into the **same token block**, and **sections consume tokens exclusively** — no section stylesheet ever selects on `prefers-color-scheme`, on a scheme class or on `data-mode`. That is what keeps this a decision about one file rather than about every stylesheet in the library, and it is why pack changes and mode changes alike can never break a layout.
- **FR-E5** Auto-branding (FR-C4) seeds accent color and logo into the active pack.

### FR-F · Control System (the disciplined vocabulary)

- **FR-F1** Controls come from a **shared vocabulary of simple, visual, named-value types**. Core set: **Segmented Control**, **Stepper** (e.g. columns 2/3/4), **Toggle**, **Named Select** (word values only), **Swatch Row** (Style Pack roles: Base/Surface/Accent/Contrast/Image — never a color picker), **Image Picker** (opens Asset Library), **Icon Picker** (curated **Tabler** set — MIT, D1, licence text shipped in the theme; **inline SVG, once per use**, never a sprite or an icon font — ruling R-26), **Link Picker**, **Text Field/Area**, **Date Picker** (calendar popover, site timezone — e.g. countdown targets), and **Item List** — *the one that was missing, and it is the largest gap the library had* (register 44). **Item List manages the ITEMS of an authored repeating block: add, remove, reorder.** Without it a user could type into an FAQ's three questions and never add a fourth, which no other control in this vocabulary can express. **It is offered only where the array is AUTHORED** — the user typed the items (A3's link columns and social links, A5, A8–A16). **A Ghost-bound repeat never gets one**: posts, tags, authors and tiers have no items to add, an Add button there would be a lie, and what those need is a *count* (Stepper) plus FR-H2's Data group. **Remove never greys** (ruling R-12): at the minimum it stays visible and active, and clicking it produces the floor and the reason as one sentence under the list. **Reorder is drag, with a keyboard equivalent.** The vocabulary may grow when a section genuinely needs a new type, provided the control stays simple and P2-compliant (visual/named values — never raw CSS, units, or hex). One shared type per need; no per-section one-offs.
- **FR-F2** No units, no hex, no CSS concepts at section level (P2). Spacing = Compact/Comfortable/Spacious. Site width and gutters are Style Pack tokens (FR-E1); sections span the site width by default — width is not a per-section control.
- **FR-F3** Sidebar structure: 3–5 **Quick Controls** pinned on top (the highest-impact decisions **for that design**), then **Content / Arrangement / Style / Data** accordion groups — the second group is named **Arrangement**, not "Layout", because under the one-term rule (Appendix I) *layout* no longer names anything: the design itself is chosen in the **design picker** above the groups (FR-D19), and this group holds the arrangement choices *inside* the chosen design. **Controls are declared per design, not per category** (FR-D19, §7.3): two designs in one category legitimately expose different controls, because a design with no media has nothing to say about media. The hard cap of **≈ 15 visible controls governs a single design**, not the category's union — the union is routinely larger, and it is the union that bounds the park-and-restore domain. The Data group appears only on dynamic designs. **A control another control disables is greyed, with the reason shown** (ruling R-33) — never hidden, and never left accepting a value it will not honour. Same shape as R-12's Remove button and for the same reason: a control that vanishes teaches nothing, and one that silently ignores you is worse. The reason is written as a sentence, not a tooltip — Features' *Icon in accent* spends the accent, so the Action button below the set greys with "not available while the media uses the accent colour". The dependency itself is declared in the control schema (FR-F7), so the sidebar, the validator and the compiler all read one source.

**Three universal controls sit on every section** — **Background role** (Swatch Row), **Vertical spacing** (Compact/Comfortable/Spacious) and **Top divider** (None/Line/Fade). They are declared once in `sections-inventory.md`, not per category, are **exempt from the ≈ 15 cap** (which governs a category's own controls, so the effective ceiling is ≈ 15 + 3), and are never Quick Controls. **A design may offer fewer values of a universal control, and must say why (ruling R-23).** Narrowing is legal; **renaming and inventing are not**. A universal control's **name and vocabulary are identical everywhere** — the Swatch Row's name is **Base** — and only the offered subset varies, with the reason shown at the control: a Contrast Band design offers three of the five background roles and the panel says *"this design is always on a contrast ground."* **An `Inherit` value is refused** anywhere. **"Vertical spacing" keeps one meaning**; per-design re-scoping of it is refused. **A row title must be distinct, but the three words may be shared** (R-13): "Card padding: Compact · Comfortable · Spacious" is legal, because the title says what it affects and an editor should learn one vocabulary rather than one per category.
- **FR-F4** Per-control reset-to-default and whole-section reset. Every control change paints its optimistic UI within one frame; the resulting canvas re-render completes within G6's 100 ms budget on the NFR-1 reference environment (one budget per interaction — no competing numbers).
- **FR-F5** Mode-scoped controls carry a moon badge when a dark override exists (FR-D7).
- **FR-F8** **A link to a page Ghost does not publish is AUTHORED, never assumed** *(owner's ruling, 2026-09-03)*. A26 wanted a link to an "all tags" page. **Ghost publishes no such route** — there is a page per tag and nothing above them — so the destination cannot be hard-coded and the link cannot be dropped either, because a site owner may well have built that page. The answer needs no new mechanism: the row is **a Text Field for the label plus a Link Picker for the destination**, both already in the vocabulary above. **It does not render until a destination is set**, so it can never ship broken, and an owner who has no such page simply leaves it empty. The same shape governs every future link to a page Ghost does not guarantee.
- **FR-F6** **Link Picker** is Ghost-aware: internal pages/posts/tags/authors (live-searched from the connected site, or sample data), **Portal actions** (Sign up, Sign in, Account, Upgrade — compiled to the `data-portal` values Appendix B enumerates; the Upgrade action emits **`account/plans`**, never `upgrade`, which Portal does not parse), external URL, and email. Internal link targets are re-validated against the connected site at compile; dead links (target deleted on Ghost) and links minted against a different site surface as pre-deploy warnings. **The Link Picker gains "Ghost search" as a destination (ruling R-24, the architect's reading confirmed at the merge).** Opening Ghost's search is a single attribute — `data-ghost-search` — not a layout, so **any button or link in any category can open search** without a new design, a new control or a new module. That is what makes deleting A23 a simplification rather than a loss of capability. **`newTab` and `rel` (nofollow / noreferrer / sponsored) are part of the stored mark record**, not editor-only state (D9, carried in the same pass as R-27, AD-4).
- **FR-F7** Controls are schema-driven from the section registry: one schema definition powers the sidebar UI, validation, defaults, dark-override capability flags, and the compiler (P4). **A control may declare that another control disables it, and the declaration carries the reason** (ruling R-33): the dependency is part of the schema, so the sidebar greys the control and prints the reason, the validator refuses the value, and the compiler never emits it — from one source rather than three. **Greyed with the reason shown, never hidden and never silently ignored** (`Appendix C`); a control that vanishes teaches nothing, and one that accepts a value it will not honour is worse. Roughly six categories need it today (A5, A14, A17, A18, A19). **There is one control schema per design**, and one **union schema per category** that is the domain of the park-and-restore rule (FR-D19). The library therefore carries a design-level schema for every design over a union schema for every category — both are generated from the same authored source (§7.3), never maintained twice.

### FR-G · Section Library

- **FR-G1** The library ships with **every category and design inventoried in Appendix A** — and **Appendix A is the count**, generated from the design export by `tools/inventory-gen.py` rather than restated here (standing rule 3; this requirement previously disagreed with itself, offering both "457 + 28" and "456 of the 484"). The inventory is a **GA floor**, not a fixed total: the library grows post-launch per FR-J14. Appendix A is normative — every listed design is a launch deliverable. The **placeable / non-placeable** split is declared there too (the non-placeable treatments are A32 paywall designs, A33 Koenig card treatments and A34 pagination styles, chosen outside the canvas); every requirement quantifying over "every design" — FR-G5's uniqueness bar, FR-G6 and NFR-6(a)'s render matrix, NFR-5's accessibility scan, the FR-N2 gallery — **still covers every non-placeable design**, in the host contexts `sections-inventory.md` names for them.
- **FR-G2** Every category ships **at least 2 Free-tier designs**, and where a category's designs split across `compileTarget`s or `bindingContext`s (FR-G3) **the Free options are distributed across those contexts**, not clustered in one — a Free user who opens a canvas must find something they can put on it. **[Free] is exactly the first two designs of every category, and nothing else** (ruling R-17) — so the total is two per category and is **derived, never restated** (standing rule 3; Appendix A generates it). **Both historical re-tiers are withdrawn:** A31's was redundant, because *every* A31 design draws the private gate, so its #1 and #2 already give `private.hbs` a Free pair; and A29's had no basis in the export, where every design reads whichever object the route provides and no design is author-bound. **A30 needs no re-tier either:** all of its designs share one `bindingContext` and one `compileTarget` (FR-I1), and its first two — **#1 Centred** and **#2 Split Pitch** — cover the two membership surfaces a Free user actually lands on. Every category spec carries the `[Free]` marker on its own roster; the merge does not map it positionally onto a roster that has since lost or gained a design. The floor is guaranteed for every context reachable from the seven synthesizable templates (FR-D6) and for `private.hbs`. It is **not** guaranteed for contexts a user reaches only by opting into an optional surface — custom page templates created in the Routes Manager (A31 #6/#7/#9) and A30's **member-home** surface — where a Free user may find only Pro options; FR-L3's block sheet must therefore offer "remove the section" alongside "swap to a Free design", because there is not always something to swap to — **and a fourth, "revert to the Free design", for the 22 Pro non-placeable treatments, where neither swapping nor removing is even expressible** (FR-L3). Free designs are marked [Free] in Appendix A; all others are Pro.
- **FR-G3** Section registry entry format: `{ id, category, name, tier, bindingContext, compileTarget, contentSchema, controlSchema, quickControls[], html, css, js?, dataBindings?, ghostCompat, darkCapabilities, previewSeed }`. **`contentSchema` is the category's union; `controlSchema` and `quickControls[]` are per design** (FR-F7), so a registry entry describes one design against its category's shared content model. **`bindingContext`** names the Ghost resource a design binds (`none` · `post` · `posts` · `tag` · `tags` · `author` · `authors` · `tiers` · `error` · `private`; `@site` and `@member` are universal and never narrow it). **There is deliberately no `page` value, because a page and a post are the same *resource*.** On `page.hbs` Ghost's root carries `post` and `page` as two keys onto the **same object** — either block scope reaches it, and Inflozo emits `{{#post}}` on both templates so one design compiles identically to both (`appendix-b1-template-contexts.md` §3, §4.2). **What genuinely differs between a post and a page is the product, not the binding**, and that is expressed through **`compileTarget`**: an article carries comments, authors, related posts and a reading TOC, a standing page does not, so A26, A27 and A28 declare `post.hbs` **only** and are structurally unplaceable on a page. Keeping the difference in `compileTarget` rather than inventing a second `bindingContext` is what stops the editor treating two names for one object as two incompatible things. The remaining `page.hbs`-only obligation is `@page.show_title_and_feature_image`, which is a **guard**, not a binding context (FR-I1) and **`compileTarget`** the file it compiles into; both are declared per category, or per design where a category's designs differ, and together they are the filter FR-D12, FR-D13 and FR-D17 obey and the placement half of FR-H7's context rules. **`ghostCompat: { minVersion, helpers[], deprecatedAt? }`** records the helper surface and version floor each design depends on; it is authored *with the design*, never per Ghost release, which is what makes FR-C5's per-release verification a mechanical check rather than a standing editorial job. **`quickControls[]` is a convention, not a second authoring pass:** the first 3–5 entries of a **design's own** control list in `sections-inventory.md` *are* its Quick Controls, in order (FR-F3, FR-F7), so the array is recovered mechanically and the two documents cannot drift. It is read from the design level, never from the category's union `Controls:` line — the union is the storage domain FR-D19 parks against, not a sidebar. Section source is **annotated HTML, not Handlebars** (§7.3, P4) — the same file the canvas renders and the compiler serializes — and its authoring format and directive vocabulary are a **documented deliverable**, since every section is authored by the dev team against it. `css` is **plain CSS consuming Style Pack custom properties only**, authored outside the app's build pipeline and explicitly excluded from any Tailwind processing the Next.js app adopts; utility classes are structurally incompatible with FR-E4's token-only styling. `{{#get}}`-bearing designs may reference **only template-level context** in their filters, never the current render context (FR-H5) — a registry-level constraint on what the library may express, binding on every design authored under E9–E11. The registry is data + files, versioned in the repo, and is the single input to both the editor runtime and the theme compiler. **Every deploy records the variant manifest it shipped** — variant ids plus the library version — on its deploy row, so FR-J14's confirm step and FR-J9's history can name what is actually live without unzipping an artifact. **`contentSchema` declares which inline binding tokens each prop accepts, and nothing else in braces is substituted (ruling R-27).** A subscriber heading accepts `{members}` and only `{members}`, and the panel shows that; anything else between braces is **literal text**. This is AD-36's shape — a value crossing into something that interprets it — so the mechanism is **an allow-list by construction, never a general substitution pass**, and free-form tokens are refused. **`compileTarget` is a refusal, not a hint** (R-7): `any` is withdrawn wherever it was a lie, a design emitting `{{pagination}}` is restricted to paginated targets, and a design performing a `{{#get}}` excludes `error.hbs` and `private.hbs`. The compiler refuses an illegal placement at compile time (FR-J3).
- **FR-G4** Every design must be: responsive 390 → 1440+ with no horizontal overflow — **and the collapse behaviour is *designed*, not specified in prose here.** Its normative source is the **15 structural archetypes** (grid-of-N, split, stack, bar, nav, edge rail, overlay, feed, form, carousel, table, media frame, sticky, article body and the bespoke exception list), each carrying a written collapse rule at three breakpoints, authored in `design/claude-design-prompt-2.md` and applied per design in its specification (§4). Prose in this document cannot specify a collapse ladder for a library this size without either being wrong or being ignored, so it points at the system that can. **The three widths are the archetype breakpoints and the render matrix's** — 1440 / 834 / 390 (NFR-6(a), FR-D8) — and a category cannot begin its build story until its archetypes are designed; WCAG 2.1 AA (contrast via token pairs, focus-visible, semantic landmarks — the AA guarantee is scoped to shipped packs and library defaults; user token edits are warned, never blocked, FR-E3); token-driven only; functional with JS disabled — JS is progressive enhancement, and **every module in FR-G7's registry carries a written no-JS degradation statement that is an acceptance criterion for that module**, restated in every per-design specification that declares it (`research-section-js-libraries.md` §7). The server-rendered state is always the real one: accordions are native `<details>`, carousels are usable `scroll-snap` strips minus their dots and arrows, load-more and infinite scroll fall back to Ghost's own numbered links, share links are real URLs, marquees render as static rows, count-ups render their final value (it is in the HTML before any script runs), and Auto dark mode is the `prefers-color-scheme` media query with no JS at all (FR-E4). Only `toc` renders nothing, and correctly — it is built from `{{content}}`'s headings, which do not exist until Ghost renders the post. **Two waivers exist, and they are named** (FR-G7(3), rulings D2 and R-5): **`nav-transform`**, and **`member-form`**. A subscribe form is **JavaScript-required and says so** — proved by execution against both majors (`MEASUREMENTS.md` §29a): `POST /members/api/send-magic-link/` never parses `application/x-www-form-urlencoded`, Ghost 6 additionally requires an `integrityToken` from a separate GET, and there is no redirect back, so a native `<form>` submit cannot reach the endpoint on either major and would land the visitor on raw JSON. **Every designed sent and error state survives** — Portal's own script applies the `loading` / `success` / `error` classes to the `<form>` — so the CSS work is real and only the promise was false. The library therefore ships a **designed `<noscript>` notice** rather than a form that silently swallows an address, and **"subscribable with JavaScript off" is struck wherever it appears**. Ghost's documentation states none of this; that is a documented docs gap, not a misreading.

  **Motion respects `prefers-reduced-motion`, and it is gated once rather than per module.** Designs that animate indefinitely — marquees, rotators, typewriter, count-ups — and every animating module passes through `core`'s `matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)')` gate before it starts (FR-G7). CSS-only motion carries the same obligation in its own idiom: anything decorative and continuous sits inside `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)`. This is a **generated-theme** requirement and is separate from Appendix H's rule about Inflozo's own confetti moment; a visitor who has asked their operating system to stop moving things is owed that on the sites Inflozo builds, not only in its editor.
- **FR-G5** Uniqueness bar: designs within a category must differ in **layout/structure**, not merely spacing or emphasis. The bar carries a **mechanical proxy** so it is not tested by unaided human judgment across ~3,500 pairwise comparisons: **every design declares a machine-checkable *structural descriptor tuple* in its per-design specification** (§4, Appendix A companion), and **no two designs in a category may share one** — a CI assertion over the inventory. **The tuple is the assertion's subject, not the one-line English descriptor beside the design's name**, because an assertion over free prose tests nothing: two designs can be described in different words and be the same design. The tuple is a fixed set of structural facts — **the archetype, the containment, the ground, the item count class, the media placement, the emphasis mechanism** — of which the first five are **closed vocabularies checked by literal match** (`tools/tuple-check.py`) and only the last is free prose. **`containment` and `ground` replaced an earlier `primary axis` slot**, which was measured to do distinguishing work twice in 186 designs while `ground` covered one of those two: the pair it cost the schema was worth less than the near-duplicate families it could not separate — a design on `surface` and the same design on `contrast` were previously indistinguishable to the assertion. **`containment` is a property of the section, not of the items inside it** — a bare grid of cards is `none`, because those cards are the item's geometry. **The proxy's reach is bounded and the bound is normative:** designs differing only in density, scale, motion or alignment reach identical closed slots and rest on the emphasis phrase, which no machine reads, so the assertion proves that a distinct structural claim exists and never that it is true. It falls out of the design-and-specification session rather than being authored separately (a category whose designs still carry only their one-line descriptor has not been through that session and cannot be asserted over yet). The English line stays, as the human-readable summary of the tuple. A reviewer must still be able to name what is structurally different about any two designs; the proxy makes the failure case findable, not the judgment unnecessary.
- **FR-G6** Section CI (see NFR-6): every design renders in the matrix (3 Style Packs × light/dark × 3 viewports), compiles into a sample theme that passes gscan, and has a stored screenshot baseline for visual regression, **and passes the FR-G8 Baseline checks in both CSS and JS**.

- **FR-G7** **The behaviour-module rulebook (normative).** Every piece of JavaScript a generated theme can run comes from **a fixed registry — `research-section-js-libraries.md` §2 is the list, and the module count is read from it rather than written here** (standing rule 3: this requirement said "31" while the registry had already moved). One of them is the shared `core`; the rest are feature modules, each derived by walking the design descriptors for motion, timing, state, interactivity or a data fetch. Since the step-4b rulings the registry has **gained `nav-transform` (D2), `contact-form` (D28), `group-headings` (R-1) and `header-scroll` (R-3)** and **lost `search-overlay`, `search-expand` and `command-palette`** — the first two to ruling R-24, the third because A23 was its only consumer and **no Inflozo design may bind ⌘K**, which `sodo-search` already takes. **A module declaration may carry the width below which the script runs** (ruling R-38) — "collapses into sections under 768" — and where it does, the module's no-JS line must describe the state on **both** sides of that width, not just one. Three designs need it today (A2-15, A13-15, and A3's footer accordions) and the shape recurs; without the declaration the compiler cannot know that a design's script is inert on a desktop and the acceptance criterion has nothing to check. Five rules bind it.

  **(1) All vanilla, zero third-party runtime dependencies — and the rule is whole again.** No generated theme ships a line of third-party JavaScript. **D3's "scoped, licence-vetted third-party exception" is WITHDRAWN** (ruling R-24): it existed only to vendor MiniSearch inside `search-overlay`, and search is now Ghost's native `sodo-search`, so nothing is vendored and there is **no exception beside `cards.js`**. Twenty-two libraries were measured against the module list and every one was rejected — on size, maintenance, redundancy, or accessibility. The enforcement is stronger than a licence scanner precisely because the answer is zero: **compile CI asserts that `assets/js/` contains only files authored in the Inflozo repository**, one check that cannot drift (`cards.js` is the declared exception — FR-J4). **The licence filter is a hard rule for any future proposal:** MIT, BSD-2/3-Clause, Apache-2.0 or ISC only, because users download, own, redistribute and may **resell** the themes Inflozo generates; GPL, AGPL, LGPL, SSPL, BUSL, non-commercial and licence-key-required terms are rejected outright, ambiguous or missing metadata counts as rejection, and the licence is re-verified **at the pinned version** rather than trusted from the package's reputation. That last clause is not hypothetical: `typed.js` relicensed MIT → **GPL-3.0** at v3.0.0 and the GPL build is now npm `latest`, so the obvious choice for A4 #13's typewriter would have put GPL code inside themes users sell. Pinning the old MIT version was rejected too — dodging a relicence by freezing a dependency forfeits its security fixes.

  **(2) A design may declare more than one module, and a module ships with its section and leaves with it.** **A design declares N modules; the compiler emits the union, and each `research-section-js-libraries.md` §7 line is restated once per declaring design** (ruling R-16) — A1, A4, A14, A15 and A22 each carry designs that declare several, and a per-design specification that names a module without its no-JS statement is not ready to build. JS is authored alongside its design during that category's design-and-specification session (§4), never as a separate module project. **Each design owns its own CSS and its own JS**, and removing a design removes both — the compiler emits neither the stylesheet nor the module of a design nothing places, and it **strips dead CSS** rather than shipping the unreachable branches the data-attribute mechanism would otherwise leave behind (FR-J3). The strip is measured, not asserted: compile CI reports emitted CSS bytes against the bytes reachable from the placed designs' selectors, and a gap is a build warning.

  **(3) Every module declares its no-JS degradation and whether it is edit-safe, and both are acceptance criteria.** **`edit-safe` means the module does not run inside the editor canvas and does not interfere with editing** (ruling R-21) — the sense is fixed once, centrally, because the specs each asserted their own and disagreed (`carousel`: A14 said yes, A15 said no). Anything not edit-safe is **suppressed on the canvas and the section renders in its resting state**; `lightbox` is the defining case and is **edit-safe: no**, since a modal opening over the canvas when you click an image to edit its caption is exactly the interference the rule names. **Every module in the registry carries a value, in `research-section-js-libraries.md` §7** — the per-module table FR-D20 has always cited and which did not exist until this merge. FR-G4 states the guarantee; the per-module statements are normative in `research-section-js-libraries.md` §7 and are restated in each per-design specification. The pattern they follow: the server-rendered state is the real one and JS enhances it — `accordion` is native `<details>`, `carousel` is a `scroll-snap` strip that is fully usable without dots or arrows, `load-more` and `infinite-scroll` fall back to Ghost's own numbered `/page/2/` links, `share` links are real `<a href>` URLs, `filter-strip` is links to Ghost routes and needs JS least of all. **One module renders nothing without JS and that is correct**: `toc` is built from `{{content}}`'s headings client-side because that structure does not exist until Ghost renders the post — the article is unaffected either way. (`confetti` was the other, and it is **deleted** — no design declared it, owner's ruling 2026-08-31.)

  **(4) `core` is platform, not per-section.** It carries the module registry, the `data-i18n-*` reader, one shared `IntersectionObserver` factory, an `AbortController` teardown path, and the **`matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)')` gate every animating module passes through** (FR-G4). It is E4 work and exists before the first category is built.

  **(5) Ten behaviours that look interactive need no module at all**, and not writing them is the largest saving in the runtime: `<details>` for toggle-reveal, `<a href="#top">` with `scroll-behavior` for back-to-top, CSS `columns` for masonry, `:has()` for hover-reveal, `position: sticky` for sticky rails, **server-side `{{#if @member}}` for every member-visibility swap** (client-side member gating would be both slower and a flash-of-wrong-content bug), CSS transitions for the hover and shimmer treatments, `<audio controls>`, and Ghost's native pagination. Each is available only because FR-G8 fixes a floor high enough to rely on it.

- **FR-G8** **Browser baseline (normative, and it governs CSS and JS alike).** Every section stylesheet and every FR-G7 module targets **Baseline "Widely Available", pinned by date** — `browserslist-config-baseline` with `widelyAvailableOnDate: 2026-08-18`, which resolves to a floor of **Chrome/Edge 121, Firefox 122, Safari and iOS Safari 17.2** (computed from `web-features`; see `research-section-js-libraries.md` §6). **The pin is a date, not a version list.** An unpinned Baseline config silently widens what is permitted every month, so the CSS a section may legally use would drift with no commit behind it; pinning makes every widening a reviewable diff, and bumping the date is a deliberate act that **requires a render-matrix re-run** (NFR-6(a)), because widening the vocabulary is exactly what that matrix exists to catch. **The Tier-2 allowlist carries `<details name>` explicitly (ruling R-15), and 2026-08-31 measured why it has to** (`MEASUREMENTS.md` §31c): `details-name` is Baseline **Newly**, `baseline_low_date` 2024-09-03, reaching **Widely on 2027-03-03** — beyond this pin, so the entry is **required** and not belt-and-braces. **The entry expires when the pin passes that date; recompute rather than assume.** One-at-a-time accordions keep the no-JavaScript `<details name>` grouping; an older browser may show more than one panel open, that is **acceptable degradation, and nothing is built for it** (D19 stands) — and the affected population is now named rather than open-ended: support begins at Firefox **130** against this pin's Firefox **122**, so it is **Firefox 122–129 and nothing else inside the floor**. Naming it in the allowlist is what stops the collision recurring the next time the linter is pointed at a new pin date. **`mask-image` is Tier-2 too** — A32's paywall fade is a fixed-height gradient from the bottom, never a line count (R-3) — as is **`fetchpriority`**; **`scrollbar-gutter` is confirmed Tier 3**.

  **Three tiers, because one hard line would be unusable.** **Tier 1 — anything at Baseline Widely on the pinned date: unrestricted**, load-bearing, anywhere. **Tier 2 — a short version-controlled allowlist of Baseline *Newly* features**, usable **only where the fallback is the design's own unstyled state and the design is complete without them**; nothing on the list may carry layout, contrast or interaction. It opens at `backdrop-filter` (behind an opaque or gradient scrim), `text-wrap: balance`, `text-wrap: pretty`, `scrollbar-width` / `scrollbar-color` and `@starting-style`. **Tier 3 — everything else is forbidden and fails the build.** Two consequences are already fixed by the floor: CSS scroll-driven animations are **not** Baseline (no Firefox), so `reading-progress` and `reveal` use `IntersectionObserver`; and `popover` is Newly-available only, so modals use native `<dialog>`, whose focus trap and scroll-locking are what let the runtime drop a focus-trap library entirely.

  **Vendor prefixes are a closed list of three, and that is what makes "no autoprefixer" safe** rather than merely fashionable (§7.1). Excerpt truncation uses `display: -webkit-box; -webkit-box-orient: vertical; -webkit-line-clamp: N; overflow: hidden` because `line-clamp`'s standard form is not interoperable; the site-wide reset uses `-webkit-text-size-adjust: 100%` or iOS Safari inflates text and breaks the type scale; non-selectable chrome uses `-webkit-user-select: none` alongside `user-select: none`. **Any other `-webkit-` / `-moz-` / `-ms-` declaration is a CI failure.** CSS Nesting is Baseline Widely and therefore technically permitted, but §7.1 forbids it for a different reason — flat, hand-editable, Casper-grade output (FR-J1) — and that prohibition stands. `overscroll-behavior` is not Baseline, so nothing may depend on it for scroll containment.

  **Enforced by three tools that need no build step**, which is why this policy fits §7.1's flat-CSS mandate rather than fighting it: **`stylelint-plugin-use-baseline`** over the flat stylesheets at `available: "widely"` plus the Tier-2 allowlist, **`eslint-plugin-compat`** over the modules against the pinned browserslist, and **`size-limit`** over the compiled `assets/js/main.js` (NFR-2). All three are build-time only and never enter a generated theme.

### FR-H · Data Binding & Content

- **FR-H1** **[Two different lists, deliberately.]** Appendix B catalogs the **Content API resources**, which include **Settings** and **Newsletters**; the `{{#get}}` helper accepts a *narrower* set. Conflating them would delete a real capability, so each is named where it applies. The Ghost data surface available for binding is cataloged in Appendix B. It reflects the Ghost Content API resources — **Posts, Pages, Tags, Authors, Tiers, Settings** — plus Handlebars template context (`@site`, `@member`, pagination) available at render time on the live site.
- **FR-H2** **[Count is capped at 100.]** Ghost 6 caps `{{#get}}` server-side at 100 by default (operator-overridable via `optimization:maxLimit` / `allowLimitAll`); Ghost 5 applies no such cap unless the operator set one. gscan **v6 only** warns on both `limit="all"` and any limit over 100, so the Count control caps at **100** and **the compiler never emits `limit="all"`** — that is what keeps a 0-warning v6 scan reachable on a claim NFR-7 makes for both majors. Dynamic sections expose a **Data** control group: Source (Latest / Featured / By tag / By author / Hand-picked posts), Count, Order (Newest/Oldest), and show/hide toggles for meta (date, author, excerpt, reading time, tag chip). On every template Ghost natively paginates — **index, custom collections, channels, and the tag and author archive templates** (Ghost serves archives and channels the same paginated `posts` context and `/page/N/` URLs as collections; a `{{#get}}`-driven feed with a fixed Count renders identical content on every page of an archive) — exactly **one section is designated the main feed**: it is bound to the native paginated `posts` context (never `{{#get}}`), sized by the global `posts_per_page` (FR-Q1) instead of a Count — **or by the route's own `limit:` where the template is reached through a `routes.yaml` collection that sets one, which overrides the global for that route only** (FR-I2). The canvas reads whichever value actually applies to the template being previewed, because a feed previewed at 12 and served at 6 is a WYSIWYG defect the user cannot see coming, and it alone exposes **Pagination style: Numbered (`/page/2/`) / Load More / Infinite scroll** (designs in Appendix A §34). All other feed sections are `{{#get}}`-driven: fixed Count, never paginated. The editor marks the main feed visibly. **The main feed owns the template's empty state** (FR-H4): a tag with no posts, an author who has published none, a filtered collection that matches nothing — each renders the designated feed design's declared empty state, which is why that state is a required field in every feed design's specification and not an optional flourish. A paginated template with **zero** feed sections is allowed, and what happens next depends on the template — the two cases were previously conflated under one workaround. **The home case is solved structurally and its workaround is retired:** a feed-less marketing homepage lives in `home.hbs`, Ghost serves `/page/N/` from `index.hbs`, and the two are different files with different content, so no duplicate arises and there is nothing to guard (FR-I1). **The archive case survives and keeps the guard:** `tag.hbs` and `author.hbs` have no second file to fall through to, so a feed-less archive still generates `/page/N/` URLs that duplicate page 1 — pre-deploy checks warn, and the compiler emits the SEO guard on **those** templates (`noindex` beyond page 1 plus a canonical link to page 1). Deleting the guard outright because `home.hbs` exists would have deleted more than `home.hbs` replaces. Designation has a defined lifecycle: the first feed section placed on a paginated template auto-designates as the main feed (tag and author templates start with a designated main feed from the Synthesis Defaults, and a channel template created in the Routes Manager is given one the same way at creation); deleting or hiding it transfers designation to the next feed section (or the zero-feed rules apply); the user can reassign it anytime. **Hand-picked order costs one `{{#get}}` per picked item. There is no hard cap; the panel warns past 25** (ruling R-20, as the owner re-ruled it on 2026-08-27 once §30 showed the premise was wrong). `filter="id:[…]"` **discards the requested order** and returns `published_at desc` on both majors (`MEASUREMENTS.md` §29d), so "picked order is drawn order" compiles to N single-id gets rather than one query. **The stored order IS the drawn order, and that is a contract on the schema rather than an implementation detail** *(owner's ruling, 2026-09-02)*: picked references are held in the order the user dragged them and handed to the template in that order, never re-sorted by `published_at` on the way. Stated because the failure is **silent** — **A18-11 Numbered draws an ordinal over whatever list it is given**, so a re-sort renumbers the list and nothing looks broken, it is simply wrong. The measurement above is also why the contract is cheap: the order already costs one query per item, so preserving it adds nothing. The cap is **stated in the panel**. It is **not** a platform limit: Ghost races each `{{#get}}` against a 5000 ms timeout of its own and imposes **no per-template budget** — 150 gets on one template resolved in full on both majors (§30, register item 47). At roughly **10 ms per pick** (8 ms on Ghost 5) this is a **latency** budget, not a platform one, so **the owner ruled no hard cap: the field warns past 25 and lets the user proceed.** The warning states the cost in plain terms, and it is worded for the case that actually bites — **the budget is per page, not per section**: three hand-picked sections at 25 each is 75 gets, ~0.75 s added to every visitor's render. The panel warns per section; the pre-deploy check warns per template. **A design may cap its own Count below the global 100 and the panel states the reason** (R-18). **The Data group's `fallback` field selects how the designed empty state renders and never what else to show instead** (ruling R-36) — back-filling with the newest posts is removed from its value set entirely, because a visitor must never be shown posts nobody chose for that spot and the fallback query it would need has never been proven to work in Ghost. And an item count is always a number picker — **except where the count selects between drawn layouts, which is a named set (ruling R-30, `Appendix C`)** — never a fixed enum**. **Load-more designs are main-feed-only** (R-7): a `{{#get}}` feed never paginates, so there is no `/page/2/` to fall back to.
- **FR-H3** **Sample dataset "Orbit Weekly", and the fixture set:** a bundled fictional publication — **32 feed posts** with feature images/excerpts/reading times, 6 tags, 3 authors with portraits and bios, 2 tiers (Free + "Supporter" with monthly/yearly prices and benefits), nav, and brand assets — plus three **fixtures** that belong to the dataset and are excluded from every feed. All imagery is internally produced (license-clean). Every section's `previewSeed` maps into this dataset so any section drops in looking finished. **The canvas never renders real post or page body HTML (NFR-3), so the fixture is the contract** — everything below is what makes A25's reading designs, A33's card treatments, A28's threads and A34's pagination designable at all rather than stipulated.

  **32 feed posts, and the size is derived from what has to be renderable.** `posts_per_page` defaults to **12** (FR-Q1), so a 12-post dataset paginates to exactly **one** page and A34's ten pagination designs, FR-D21's page-2 state and FR-H2's main-feed designation would between them have no renderable state at all. **32 = 12 + 12 + 8** yields three pages: a first, a **true middle page** — the only state carrying both a previous and a next link, and therefore the only one in which a numbered treatment shows its full range — and a partial last, which is where a design's row-alignment breaks if it is going to. **Two rules are normative and the numbers are their current values:** the feed dataset must exceed `posts_per_page` by enough to render a middle page *and* a partial page, and the two must never be equal.

  **Every Data-group Source must return a usable set, not merely a non-empty one** (FR-H2). The dataset is composed to guarantee it: **8 of the 32 are `featured`**, so A19's featured-bound designs fill their largest layouts and a Source of **Featured** is never a one-card row; the 6 tags each carry **at least 3** posts, so **By tag** returns a real set on any tag; and the 3 authors each carry **at least 6**, so **By author** does the same. Featured posts are spread across tags and authors rather than clustered, so a *combined* filter — featured **and** by tag — also returns something. A Source option that renders empty in the editor is indistinguishable from a broken binding, which is the confusion this composition exists to prevent. The twenty posts past the first twelve exist to fill pages 2 and 3 and to make those filters work: they carry the same field shape drawn from the same image pool rather than adding twenty further hero images.

  **(1) The style-guide post — the `{{content}}` fixture.** A post whose body is **every Ghost editor (Koenig) card**, in every class-affecting variant. It is what `{{content}}` resolves to everywhere on the canvas (FR-H4, FR-H5), what A25's twelve designs wrap, and what FR-Q7's card panels preview against. It is **generated from Ghost's own renderers, never hand-written** — the output of each `render*Node` is snapshotted into a fixture file and checked in — and it covers: image ×3 widths ×caption; gallery 1–5 images ×caption; video ×3 widths ×loop; callout ×9 colours ×emoji; button ×2 alignments; product ×rating ×button ×image; `header_v2` ×4 layouts ×swapped; signup ×4 layouts; cta ×9 backgrounds ×2 layouts ×dividers ×sponsor ×image; file ×title/caption combinations; toggle open **and** closed; code ×caption; bookmark ×thumbnail ×caption (`research-ghost-koenig-cards.md` §6.6 — that enumeration **is** the design surface, and FR-Q7's panel set must cover exactly it). It renders **inside the same wrapper the shipped theme uses** (`<main><article class="gh-content">…`), because `.kg-width-*` behaviour and every scoped selector depend on it; it loads **the theme stylesheet, then a simulated `cards.min.css` carrying only the chunks *not* in FR-Q7's exclude list, in that order** — the same order the browser will use (§7.4) — rebuilt whenever the exclude list changes; and it loads the **four vendored behaviour scripts** (FR-Q7) so toggles toggle and galleries proportion correctly. A canvas loading only Inflozo's CSS would look right while the shipped site looked wrong, which is the precise failure this document exists to prevent. **Regenerated whenever the Ghost target bumps** — a renderer-derived fixture turns a changed card into an obvious diff instead of silent drift (NFR-6(c1), §7.6).

  **(2) The comments fixture — with a stated count.** `{{comments}}` on the canvas renders **14 comments across 9 threads** (five threads carry one reply), one with a deliberately long body for wrapping and one rendered in the signed-out state. The count is stated because A28's **count header** control has nothing to render without one, and two digits are what the header's layouts actually have to hold. The fixture stands for Ghost's native thread output; **what a theme may style *inside* that output is a verify-at-build item** (§7.6), and A28's ten designs are scoped to the chrome around the thread — panel, count header, width, signed-out note — until it is answered.

  **(3) The style-guide page.** The same body on a `page.hbs` fixture, so A25 is designable on its second compile target (Appendix A §25) and A24's `@page.show_title_and_feature_image` guard has both of its states to show.

  **Fixtures are Inflozo-authored content, not user content.** NFR-3's never-read rule is about the *user's* body HTML and is untouched: the fixture is generated, checked in, and trusted by construction — which is exactly why it can be rendered when real body HTML cannot (§7.3).
- **FR-H4** With a linked site, the editor fetches real content **client-side** from the site's Content API (browser-safe, cacheable by design) with SWR-style caching (60 s) and silent fallback to Orbit Weekly on network failure (with a subtle indicator). **Reads are batched and de-duplicated per resource** across the canvas, Section Picker previews and Link Picker search, under a per-session request ceiling; on HTTP 429 or repeated failure the editor falls back to Orbit Weekly and **names the cause**, because Ghost rate-limits Content API keys and Ghost(Pro) sits behind an edge Inflozo does not model. The canvas reads list and metadata fields only and **never post or page body HTML** (NFR-3): `{{content}}` resolves to the **style-guide fixture** (FR-H3) in every Post Content design, on `post.hbs` and `page.hbs` alike. That is the whole of what "dummy content" means here — a generated, checked-in body carrying every Ghost editor card, not unspecified placeholder prose, because A25's designs and A33's card treatments are judged against what it renders and an undefined placeholder makes both undesignable. When the site has fewer or zero items than a section requests, the canvas renders what exists plus an editor-only indicator ("this site has 2 posts; section shows up to 6"); compiled sections render gracefully with fewer items.

  **The empty-state rule governs FEEDS, and a section whose items the user typed is not one** *(owner's ruling, 2026-09-02, closing A13's contradiction)*. The test is where the items come from, and it is a test rather than a list so it cannot go stale: **items pulled from Ghost** — posts, tags, authors, tiers — render the designed empty state, because an empty tag archive that renders nothing is a broken page a visitor arrived at deliberately. **Items the user AUTHORS** — a Process list, a Features set, an FAQ — render **nothing at all at zero**, because there is no such page to break: an editor who has typed no steps is mid-build, not looking at an error. A13 Process has said exactly this since it was drawn and is right; what needed narrowing was the rule, not the category.

  **Zero items has three distinct behaviours (owner ruling), and conflating them is what produced a design nobody could place.** **(1) The designated main feed** (FR-H2) renders its **empty state** — a real, designed state of that feed design, not a separate design and not a blank region, because the main feed *is* the page and an empty tag archive that renders nothing is a broken page. **It is never back-filled** (ruling R-36): a visitor is not shown posts nobody chose for that spot, and the fallback query that would take has never been proven to work in Ghost. The designed "nothing here yet" state is the only answer. Every feed design's specification declares this state (Appendix A companion, the per-design **Empty states** field), and its copy comes from `archive.empty_heading` / `archive.empty_body` in the string catalog. **(2) A secondary `{{#get}}` feed** — related posts, a featured row, a topic strip — renders **nothing at all**, heading and container together, because an empty "More like this" panel is worse than its absence; this is the same reasoning that puts A28 #2 rather than #1 in the `post.hbs` Synthesis Defaults. **(3) A bound prop that is not a feed** follows FR-H8 unchanged: text falls back to its static value, media hides the element it occupies. **This retired a design.** A31 #8 "Empty Tag State" was an inventory entry for behaviour (1) — but it declared no file to compile into and no surface could place it, so it was a design in the library that no user could reach. It is a state of the feed now, and A31's ids are non-contiguous as a result.
- **FR-H5** **Canvas rendering & helper shim:** the canvas renders sections directly from their annotated-HTML source (§7.3), the same source the compiler serializes, so canvas and compiled markup agree by construction rather than by comparison. Handlebars is never parsed or executed in the browser. Ghost-sourced values are resolved by a helper shim implementing the subset the library uses: `{{#foreach}}`, `{{#get}}` (mapped to Content API queries with NQL filters), `{{img_url}}` (emitting Ghost-shaped sized URLs and `srcset`, not pass-through — NFR-6), `{{date}}`, `{{reading_time}}`, `{{#match}}`, `{{#if @member}}`, `{{navigation}}`, `{{asset}}`, `{{content}}`, `{{title}}`, `{{excerpt}}`, `{{custom_excerpt}}`, `{{url}}`, `{{tags}}`, `{{authors}}`, `{{t}}` (FR-Q6), pagination context, the four **core helpers** `{{total_members}}` / `{{total_paid_members}}` / `{{content_api_key}}` / `{{content_api_url}}` (Appendix B), and truthy helpers — the full Appendix B surface. **The two count helpers render a sample value on an unlinked project and the real rounded string on a linked one, always as a string.** **`{{content_api_key}}` never renders a real key on the canvas** — the shim emits an inert placeholder, because a key belongs in a deployed theme on the user's own domain and has no business in a screenshot, a snapshot diff or a support session. All Content-API values render as text nodes (NFR-3). **`{{content}}` resolves to the style-guide fixture and `{{comments}}` to the comments fixture with its stated count** (FR-H3) — neither is ever the connected site's real output, and both are shim outputs like any other, held to the same contract tests. **Two of Ghost's own injected surfaces are shimmed on the canvas, and neither shim is a section.** Both render **inside** the canvas iframe (§7.3's chrome partition) where Ghost puts them, and both are **non-interactive, non-selectable, absent from Layers, excluded from Shuffle and Remix, and never compiled into anything**. They exist so a design is authored knowing what will actually share the page with it, which is why these two are shimmed rather than scoped out like the remaining injected surfaces (§1.2). Both are drawn from the connection's stored settings snapshot (FR-C2, §7.5), refreshed daily (FR-C5), and both are NFR-6(c3) exclusion regions — Ghost's real markup is Ghost's to render, and diffing against it would fail on markup Inflozo did not write.

  **(1) Portal's floating subscribe button** — `position: fixed`, bottom corner, shown only when the connection says the button is enabled. It lands exactly where edge-rail and corner designs put their own furniture.

  **(2) Ghost's announcement bar — a strip pinned to the very top of the canvas, above everything the theme renders**, because that is where `{{ghost_head}}` puts it. It shows the site's real announcement text in its real background (`announcement_background`), it **occupies real vertical space and pushes the design down** rather than overlaying it, and it honours the canvas's member-state toggle: a bar set to paid members only does not render while previewing as Anonymous (FR-D16), matching `announcement_visibility`. **This is what makes the stacking visible while designing rather than discovered after deploy** — an Inflozo announcement bar, a transparent-overlay header or a sticky header all sit *below* Ghost's strip on the real site, and designing them without it on screen is designing against a page that does not exist. It disappears the moment the user clears Ghost's bar (Appendix A §2), which is the point at which the two-bar stack resolves into one. Shim fidelity is enforced by contract tests asserting each shimmed helper's output matches recorded real-Ghost output for a fixed input set (NFR-6). `{{#get}}` filters may reference only template-level context, never the current render context — this constrains the section library and is binding on FR-G3's registry contract.
- **FR-H6** The `{{#get "tiers"}}`-bound surfaces (Pricing sections, the paywall, membership pages) show live tier names, prices, currency, and benefits from the connected site; sample tiers otherwise. **Tier presence never implies tier purchasability (normative).** Ghost seeds an active `$5/mo · $50/yr` "Default Product" tier at install, so `{{#get "tiers"}}` returns a paid tier carrying real prices and a real id on a site that has never connected Stripe (`research-ghost-membership-pages.md` §6). Every emitted paid CTA — tier cards, checkout buttons, `data-portal="signup/{tierId}/monthly"` — therefore compiles inside `{{#if @site.paid_members_enabled}}`, never inside a tier-count check, and every tier query filters `type:paid+visibility:public` rather than trusting the API to hide hidden tiers (§11.1, §7.6). This is the single most likely way a generated membership page ships broken. **The paywall is a template surface, not a placeable section:** its 12 designs (Appendix A §32) are selected in a standalone **Paywall editor** with its own canvas, one design active per project, and compile to `partials/content-cta.hbs` — the override point Ghost renders at the members-only cutoff the user placed in the Ghost editor. It is therefore absent from the Section Picker, from Layers and from Shuffle (FR-D5, FR-D12, FR-D13). A member ask on an ordinary canvas is an existing CTA section carrying FR-D5's member-visibility control, not a paywall. Connect (and the daily health check, FR-C5) records whether membership and paid tiers are enabled on the site; placing portal/signup sections or designing the paywall on a site with members disabled warns in the editor, and a pre-deploy check repeats it — a theme never ships dead signup forms unannounced. **The check never fires on synthesized instances**, which is moot for the paywall (the Synthesis Defaults synthesize none) and is the rule for every other case, so an untouched project is never warned about a section its user did not place. **Gate every member ask on the site's own capability flag** (ruling R-4): a free ask wraps in `{{#if @site.allow_self_signup}}`, a paid ask in `{{#if @site.paid_members_enabled}}`, and both are warned at connect and again pre-deploy. **`@member` binds only its proven field set** — `uuid · email · name · firstname · avatar_image · subscriptions · paid · status` (`update-local-template-options.js:27-40`). **"Member since" is deleted**: `created_at` does not exist on `@member`. **The newsletter list is deleted** and becomes a stateless Portal hand-off to `account/newsletters`, and **newsletter cadence is deleted** — `schema.js` carries no such field. A signed-in badge reads **"Signed in"**, never "You are subscribed": `@member` cannot know which letter. A tier badge tests `{{#match visibility "paid"}}`, never `!== 'public'`. **And a member's own details are never server-rendered into markup** (ruling R-28, AD-38): no `@member.email`, name or billing detail reaches the page — anything identifying is a hand-off to Ghost's own account panel, which is the safe shape whatever `cacheMembersContent` is set to on a server Inflozo neither controls nor can re-check.
- **FR-H7** **Context-aware binding (prevention, not warning):** the editor offers only bindings valid in the current template's render context, per the normative matrix in `appendix-b1-template-contexts.md`. Invalid bindings are never presented; there is no path by which a user can construct one. The matrix is keyed on three axes: **template** (single-resource templates such as `post.hbs`/`page.hbs` expose their resource only inside a block scope, never at top level — on `page.hbs` the root carries `post` and `page` as **two keys onto one object**, so `{{#post}}` and `{{#page}}` both reach it, and **Inflozo emits `{{#post}}` on both templates** so one design compiles identically to both. `{{#page}}` is the *only* form on a `routes.yaml` custom route declaring `data: page.x`; list templates expose `posts` and `pagination` at the root), **scope** (top level vs inside `{{#foreach}}`), and **connected Ghost version** (fields introduced after a target's version — e.g. the seven social fields added in 6.36.0 — are not offered for that site). `@site.*` and `@member` are available in every template. Moving or duplicating a section onto another template re-validates every binding on it; bindings unavailable in the destination are surfaced at the moment of the move and must be re-pointed or reverted to static before the move completes. Ghost compiles themes without strict mode: an out-of-context binding renders an empty string with no error at build, deploy or runtime, so the editor is the only place this class of defect can be caught.
- **FR-H8** **Empty-value handling:** every bound prop compiles inside a guard; the unguarded state is unreachable. **This governs props, not feeds** — a feed with zero items is FR-H4's rule, not this one, and the two must not be collapsed: a guard hides an element, an empty state renders one. Two behaviors, chosen per binding, defaulting by kind: **text** bindings fall back to the static value the prop held before it was bound — `{{#if @site.description}}{{@site.description}}{{else}}<static>{{/if}}` — and **media** bindings hide the element they occupy, guarding the element and never the attribute. This mirrors Ghost's own themes, which substitute where a sensible alternative exists and hide only where none does. **The guard is derived from the bound field, never from a helper argument.** This is stated because the spike got it wrong in a way nothing could see: `wrapGuard` derived the guard identifier from a date helper's *format* argument and emitted `{{#if YYYY}}` — a guard on an identifier that does not exist, so the block never renders and the content is **silently and permanently lost**. A garbage guard is *present*, which is why a check that only asks "is there a guard?" passes while the page is empty. Guards use `{{#if}}` exclusively — `{{#has}}` is not a null check and reports false for numeric and boolean values. Media guards must enclose any `srcset`: an unguarded `srcset` renders a malformed attribute the browser resolves as a relative URL, producing live 404s. Every `@site` key is always defined (`null` when unset), so `{{#if @site.x}}` is sufficient in all cases.

  **The avatar is the one designed substitute, and it has two forms (ruling R-2).** An avatar with no photograph does not hide — hiding a face leaves a hole in a row of faces — and it does not fall back to a generic silhouette. It shows **initials**, and which initials depends on where the name came from, because **Ghost cannot split a name in a template**: `{{split}}` arrived in Ghost 6.5 and is a **gscan error below it**, so a two-initial form is unreachable for Ghost-sourced data on the majors Inflozo supports. Therefore: a list the **user authors** carries **two initials baked at compile** ("Jane Doe" → `JD`), where Inflozo owns the string and can split it; an **author pulled from Ghost** shows **one letter**, produced in **pure CSS** from the first character — no helper, no script, no partial, and correct on both majors. A design must not mix the two forms in one component.

### FR-I · Templates & Routing (routes.yaml)

- **FR-I1** **[`home.hbs` is emitted.]** Ghost resolves `home.hbs` for the site root ahead of `index.hbs`, and Ghost's own Source theme uses it. Inflozo emits it whenever the Home canvas differs from the generic post feed, which is the ordinary case — this is why FR-H2 needs **no** special-case SEO workaround **for the root** — that half is retired rather than reworded, while the archive half of the guard survives, because `tag.hbs` and `author.hbs` have no second file to fall through to. **A post layout may carry exactly one Post Content section, and the editor prevents a second** (ruling R-37) — two would print the article twice on a live page. Refused **at placement, with the reason**, not warned about after the fact: there is no reason to want it, so nothing is lost by making it impossible (FR-D5's singleton rule is the mechanism). Standard template set always compiled: `default.hbs` (synthesized shell: head, fonts, tokens, header/footer partials, `{{ghost_head}}`/`{{ghost_foot}}`, `{{body_class}}`), `index.hbs`, `post.hbs`, `page.hbs`, `tag.hbs`, `author.hbs`, `error.hbs` — and **where one of these has no project doc its content is synthesized per `sections-inventory.md` § Synthesis Defaults before assembly** (FR-J1), so a standard file is never emitted empty. `page.hbs` gates its title and feature-image markup on `{{#if @page.show_title_and_feature_image}}` — the A24 designs compiled into it carry the guard, and nothing else does (Appendix A §24). Ghost's own page-builder settings drive the flag, and honouring it is a **product** obligation before it is a gscan one: unguarded, the user's toggle in Ghost Admin does nothing. gscan's `GS110-NO-MISSING-PAGE-BUILDER-USAGE` is only a **warning** (identical on the v5 and v6 specs, so it never blocks a deploy), and it fires when the property appears in **no** non-partial `.hbs` template nor in any partial reachable from one — an **orphan** partial does not satisfy it, and the `ref: page.hbs` it reports is cosmetic, emitted unconditionally even where no `page.hbs` exists. Its sibling `GS110-NO-UNKNOWN-PAGE-BUILDER-USAGE` (also warning) fires on any other `@page.*`, so `show_title_and_feature_image` is the **only** property from that namespace the compiler may ever emit (FR-J6). **There is no members template family.** `members/signup.hbs`, `members/signin.hbs` and `members/account.hbs` do not exist in Ghost 6 and never render; that path is a fossil of one archived 2021 theme whose own `routes.yaml` pointed at it (`research-ghost-membership-pages.md` §1, §9, §12). A designed membership page compiles to **`custom-{name}.hbs` at the theme root** — the same file class as FR-I3's custom templates — and the user binds it to an ordinary Ghost Page by picking it from the **Template** dropdown in Ghost's page editor. **No route is emitted and none is needed:** designing a membership page adds no `routes.yaml`, and therefore removes a manual Ghost Admin step rather than adding one (FR-I4, FR-I5). Ghost stores the chosen filename on the page's own row, so the binding survives a retitle, a slug change and a theme swap. Inflozo therefore **never emits `page-{slug}.hbs`**: that form is matched against the live slug at render time, detaches silently the moment the user retitles the page, and outranks the user's explicit dropdown choice — Ghost Admin disables the dropdown outright when a slug template matches. Membership templates compile **only when the user has designed one**; untouched membership flows are served by Ghost's native Portal — no dead files ship. Removing every section from a designed membership template returns it to untouched: its file is not emitted on the next compile, and a Ghost page still pointing at that filename falls back to `page.hbs` rather than erroring. **That fallback is silent on Ghost's side, so Inflozo is not silent on its own:** emptying a custom template warns before it takes effect, naming the consequence, because "the page still loads, wearing a different design" is exactly the failure a user discovers weeks later. `private.hbs` follows the same conditional pattern: it compiles only when a Private Site Gate section (Appendix A §31) is designed, and so does `partials/content-cta.hbs`, which is emitted only when the paywall has been designed (FR-H6). **The emptying rule is general:** removing every section from any template returns it to untouched, and the consequence differs by class — the seven synthesizable templates (`home.hbs`, `index.hbs`, `post.hbs`, `page.hbs`, `tag.hbs`, `author.hbs`, `error.hbs`) re-synthesize from the defaults on the next compile, while every conditional template stops emitting entirely. **One class does not stop:** a Routes Manager custom template that has a `routes.yaml` route pointing at it keeps emitting even when emptied, because a route whose template is missing raises `IncorrectUsageError` and the URL returns 500 (`research-ghost-membership-pages.md` §1) — removing that surface is a Routes Manager deletion (FR-I3), not an emptying. **Hiding every section is not emptying** (FR-D5): a fully hidden template is designed and emits an empty body.
- **FR-I2** **Routes Manager** (dedicated surface reachable from the editor): a visual builder for `routes.yaml` — define **collections** (URL prefix, filter, assigned template — **page size per collection**, defaulting to the global `posts_per_page` (FR-Q1) — ⚠ this document previously said Ghost has no per-collection page size, and that is **false**: a `routes.yaml` route carrying an explicit `limit:` **overwrites `@config.posts_per_page` at render time for that route** (`services/routing/controllers/collection.js`, `research-ghost-binding-contexts.md` §2; `appendix-b1-template-contexts.md` §2 already carried the fact). It is the **fourth** reviewer assertion about Ghost in this project to enter the document as normative text and be verified false, and the failure class has its own risk row in §7.6. Setting a per-collection limit is therefore offered; leaving it unset emits no `limit:` and inherits the global) with a rich filter builder (All/Any condition groups over tag, author, primary tag/author, featured, visibility and has-feature-image) and drag-to-reorder collection precedence; **channels** (filtered post streams with their own path and RSS feed); **custom routes** (static path → custom template); and taxonomy prefixes; plus a live YAML preview pane and validation (path collisions, unknown templates, filter syntax, **custom-template filename collisions, and any attempt to rename a filename this project has already deployed**) before save. All of this ships in v1. **The filter builder's field→NQL mappings are normative**: tag → `tag:{slug}` · author → `author:{slug}` · primary tag → `primary_tag:{slug}` · primary author → `primary_author:{slug}` · featured → `featured:true` / `featured:false` · visibility → `visibility:{public\|members\|paid}` · has-feature-image → `feature_image:-null`; All/Any groups compile to `+` and `,` with parenthesized nesting. **The builder never emits redundant or nested parentheses, and flattens single-child groups** — NQL parses `((tag:news))` into a garbage key **silently, with no error and no exception**, so a doubly-wrapped group produces a filter that quietly matches the wrong posts. Sibling groups are safe; only nesting corrupts. This is a rule on the emitter, and validation asserts it on the emitted string. **Published date IS offered, with relative syntax.** ⚠ This document previously dropped the field, asserting that NQL has no relative-date syntax and that a fixed date would silently freeze. **Both halves are false and were verified false against `@tryghost/nql`:** `published_at:>now-30d` parses to `{"published_at":{"$gt":"…"}}`, and Ghost re-parses the `routes.yaml` `filter` string **per request**, so a relative filter tracks the clock rather than freezing at compile. The builder therefore maps published date to `published_at` with the operators `>` / `<` / `>=` / `<=` over either an absolute ISO date or a relative `now-{n}{d|w|M|y}` term, and offers the relative form first, because "the last 30 days" is what a collection usually means. **Relative dates are a `routes.yaml` capability and not a Data-group one:** FR-H2's `{{#get}}` Source list is unchanged, since a fixed-Count feed with a relative filter would render a different set on every request while the design was built against a fixed one — and no design in the library asks for that. The builder emits NQL and never parses it, so validation is a round-trip over what the builder produced and no third-party NQL parser is a dependency. **The round trip asserts the parsed result *is the intended filter*, not merely that parsing succeeded** — a success-only check is blind to exactly the failure that motivates the parenthesis rule, since `((tag:news))` parses cleanly into something else entirely.
