- **FR-I3** Custom templates created in the Routes Manager (compiled as `custom-{name}.hbs` — **§7.4's naming and collision scheme is enforced here, at the naming step**: the user types a display name, sees the derived filename and the Ghost dropdown label it will produce, and is refused a name that collides with another template in the project or with one this project has already deployed. **Renaming a deployed template is not offered at all**, and the UI says why: Ghost stores the filename on every page that selected it and never repairs it) appear in the template switcher as designable canvases and are also assignable to individual Ghost pages from the Ghost editor (documented). The switcher's "+ New template" is a shortcut that deep-links into this Routes Manager flow — one creation flow, two entry points. Deleting a custom template (or its route) in the Routes Manager warns when the template has designed content — the canvas is deleted with it (recoverable via undo, FR-D9) — and the switcher drops the removed template; an editor viewing it switches to Home with a notice.
- **FR-I4** **`routes.yaml` uploads automatically, and the guided step is the fallback.** The route is **`POST /settings/routes/yaml`**, `multipart/form-data`, file under the field name `routes` (`GET` on the same path downloads). The earlier claim that no automated path exists on any version or host was **false, and it deleted a working capability**: the integration allowlist that blocks `setting: edit` binds **integration** tokens only — a **staff** token carries a `user_id`, skips the allowlist entirely, and an Administrator or Owner holds `setting: all`. Inflozo already mandates the site Owner's Staff Access Token (FR-C1) and already relies on this same path for `GET /themes/*` in FR-J13 and FR-J16, so the mechanism was proven in one requirement while being denied in another. **Automated upload is therefore the primary path**, performed with the same credential and immediately verified by reading the file back byte-for-byte (unchanged from the previous specification, and now the confirmation step of the automated path rather than of a manual one). **The guided Labs card remains as the designed fallback** for the cases that genuinely cannot use it: no Staff Access Token supplied, a revoked or rotated token, or a non-Owner connection. It is a designed surface, not a warning toast (UX mandate, header note). **FR-I5's product claim is restated accordingly** — the value is that Inflozo manages routing rather than that it guides you through Ghost's manual step, and §7.6's open item on this is closed with the source citation rather than left pending.
- **FR-I6** **Template-binding flow (in-product, and post-deploy where it belongs).** A designed membership page or custom template compiles to `custom-{name}.hbs` and emits **no route** (FR-I1, FR-I5) — which removes a Ghost Admin step but leaves exactly one: someone must open the Ghost page editor and pick the template from the **Template** dropdown. Until now this document specified the mechanism and shipped no flow and no docs for it, so the last step of a designed membership page was a thing the user had to already know.

  **The flow fires from the deploy success state**, because it is a post-deploy step and nothing earlier can complete it: a deploy that emitted one or more `custom-*.hbs` files ends on a "one step left" card naming **each** emitted template, its exact filename and the exact dropdown label Ghost will derive from it (§7.4's transform is fixed, so the label can be shown rather than guessed), with the click-path in Ghost Admin and a deep link to the page editor of the connected site. It is reachable afterwards from the template switcher (FR-D6) and from the site card, and it is **not** a modal that must be dismissed to reach the confetti — the deploy succeeded.

  **It is a checklist, not a notification.** Each emitted template is listed with a done-state the user marks themselves; Inflozo cannot verify a binding, because the assignment lives on Ghost's page row and the Content API does not expose which template a page selected. **Saying "we can't see whether you did this" is the honest version**, and the flow says it rather than implying verification it does not have. A project whose deploy emitted no custom template never sees the flow — which is every starter's first deploy (FR-O1), so the confetti stays clean. FR-B7 raises one notification when the flow is unopened a day after such a deploy, once, never again; FR-N4 carries the docs page.

- **FR-I5** Projects that never open the Routes Manager compile with Ghost's default routing (no routes.yaml surprises). **There is no exception.** Designed membership pages emit no route — they compile to `custom-{name}.hbs` and are bound from Ghost's page-editor Template dropdown (FR-I1) — so a project can ship a fully designed signup, signin and member-home page and still deploy with default routing and no `routes.yaml` at all, which is one manual Ghost Admin step fewer than a routed surface would have cost (FR-I4). **Two routes are emitted on demand, and one never is (rulings R-24, R-25).** Ghost serves **no all-tags route and no all-authors route** natively, so each needs a Routes Manager custom route rendering a template with a `{{#get}}`. Inflozo **emits them, and only when a design that links to them is actually placed** — a site is never given a page it does not use. **`/search/` is never emitted by anyone.** Ghost's search is `sodo-search`, opened by `data-ghost-search`, by a `#/search` fragment on the current page or by ⌘K; `#/search` is a fragment, not a route, and a route that rendered no results would be a fake results page by another name (`MEASUREMENTS.md` §29c). **`{{ghost_head exclude="search"}}` is never emitted**, and **no Inflozo design may bind ⌘K**, which sodo already takes — a lint rule on the emitter, not a probe.

### FR-Q · Theme Settings (global config & Ghost custom settings)

- **FR-Q1** A dedicated **Theme Settings** surface (reachable from the editor, sibling to the Routes Manager) configures global theme values compiled into `package.json`: `posts_per_page` (default 12) and any future config Inflozo manages. This replaces per-section derivation — feed sections' Count controls govern only their own `{{#get}}` queries. **Every surface that mentions "posts per page" links HERE, never into Ghost Admin (ruling R-10 #13)** — `posts_per_page` is a key in the theme's own `package.json` (`theme-engine`'s `allowedKeys`, `research-section-js-libraries.md` §2.2) and **Ghost Admin has no such setting to link to**. A34's pagination designs are the ones that ask, and sending a user to Ghost to look for a control that does not exist is worse than not offering the link.
- **FR-Q2** **[Groups and colour defaults.]** Ghost recognises exactly two `group` values — `homepage` and `post`. Any other string is **bucketed into "Site wide" in Ghost Admin and rendered there**, which is where a site-wide setting belongs, so the setting reaches the user exactly as intended; the only consequence is a gscan **recommendation**, not a misplacement and not an error. Colour-type defaults must be **6-digit hex** (`#RRGGBB`) — three-digit shorthand, named colours and a missing default each trip an **error** and block the deploy. **Custom settings builder:** the user defines Ghost `@custom` settings for their theme — name, type (select / boolean / color / image / text — Ghost's exact five), options, default, Ghost Admin group (Site wide / Homepage / Post), optional visibility condition on another setting — compiled into `package.json` `config.custom` and thereafter editable by the site owner in **Ghost Admin → Design settings** without redeploying. Inflozo enforces Ghost's 20-setting cap with a visible meter — **3 slots and their keys are reserved for the dark-mode built-ins (FR-Q5) in every project, Light-only included**, so user-defined settings cap at 17 and toggling dark mode on or off can never overflow the cap or collide with a built-in key; a Light-only project **compiles all three and references all three** (FR-Q5), so the reservation is not a slot held empty — it is a slot in use, and the uniform cap is a description of what every theme actually emits rather than a bookkeeping convenience. Inflozo generates lowercase snake_case keys, validates select defaults against options, and disallows defaults on image settings (Ghost rule). **Setting keys are immutable once deployed or exported** (export mirrors deploy, FR-J12 — a manually installed exported theme has stored values to protect too) — renaming a key on a later deploy is a breaking change that would erase the site owner's stored value, so the builder treats keys as permanent identifiers (rename changes the label only). Keys are unique per project; deleting a setting warns that re-creating the same key later would resurrect the site owner's stored value.
- **FR-Q3** A custom setting takes effect by **binding**, and its Ghost type follows from what is promoted — all five types are available: section controls flagged "expose to Ghost Admin" (**Toggle → boolean** · **Segmented / Named Select → select** · **Image Picker → image** · **text prop → text** — Ghost Admin text settings are plain strings, so binding a text prop shows an upfront confirm: "This text won't support formatting while it's editable in Ghost Admin — bold, italic, underline, and links will be removed and disabled." On confirm, any existing inline marks are stripped and inline formatting is disabled for that prop for as long as the binding exists; removing the binding re-enables formatting) and the Style Pack accent token (**accent → color**; color promotion is accent-only in v1, FR-Q5). The compiled theme reads `{{@custom.*}}` at render time instead of the baked value for every bound control or token. **Shuffling away is not deleting.** Under FR-D19's park-and-restore rule, moving to a design that does not declare a bound control **parks the binding along with its value** — the setting is not emitted while that design is inactive, and returning restores both. The warning says so ("this Ghost setting won't appear while this design is in use") rather than threatening loss, because breaking a promotion the user made simply because they browsed the design ring would be hostile. **Deleting or hiding** the section is different and does warn about the binding; compile validation rejects dangling bindings (the setting must be re-bound or removed), so a theme never ships dead Ghost Admin settings or unread `{{@custom.*}}` values. **Promotion is visible and warned at pack switch:** a promoted value compiles as the Ghost Admin *default* and Ghost keeps whatever the site owner stored, so switching Style Packs (FR-E2) restyles the canvas while the live site keeps the owner's old accent. The Style panel shows which tokens are promoted, and switching packs while the accent is promoted warns before the switch lands. **Ghost's comment accent colour is linked to, never written (ruling R-22, reversing D17).** Inflozo states plainly that comments take their colour from Ghost's accent setting and gives a direct link to it. **AD-10's write allowlist stays at four** — no fifth write, no new consent screen, no new audit surface. The credential is all-or-nothing (an Admin key mints a JWT Ghost verifies by signature, not by intent), so every added write widens a blast radius that cannot be narrowed at the credential. **Also declined and recorded so they are not re-proposed:** A30's page creation, and `portal_button` writes — what actually needs re-pointing there is the site's own member links, which is an editor choice (FR-F6, P0·4), not a write.
- **FR-Q4** The canvas previews custom settings at their defaults — **including the three dark built-ins' fallback chains** (FR-Q5): an unset dark logo previews as the light logo or the wordmark, an unset dark accent previews as the light accent. Previewing the *declared* default rather than the *resolved* one would show the user a state their visitors never see. Compile validation + gscan cover the generated custom config.
- **FR-Q5** **Dark-mode built-ins — always declared, and always *referenced*.** **Every** project compiles the same three built-in custom settings, Light-only included: `color_scheme` (Auto/Light/Dark — Auto follows the visitor's system preference in pure CSS, FR-E4; Light/Dark pin a mode as a body class), **Dark accent color** (color; default = the pack's hand-paired dark accent), and **Dark logo** (image) — leaving 17 slots for user-defined settings. **`color_scheme` is the single source of the site's mode, and nothing offers a second selector** (ruling R-34): `{{comments mode=…}}` **derives** from this value rather than exposing its own control (FR-Q3), and when the value is Light or Dark rather than Auto, FR-D7's visitor `mode-toggle` is not offered at all.

  **The template always reads all three, and that is the whole mechanism.** gscan's `GS100` fires on a `@custom` key that is **declared and never referenced**, so the earlier design — declare them only on Light+Dark projects — put a gscan collision inside the one setting a user is most likely to toggle. Referencing them unconditionally removes the collision at its root instead of gating the declaration around it: because `default.hbs` names each key on every compile, **`GS100` can never fire** (FR-J6), and there is **no compiler special case** — one mechanism in the theme, the same on every project.

  **What "referenced" means is real fallback logic, not a token mention.** Three chains, baked into the theme and inert rather than absent when the values are unset: **`dark_logo` falls back to the light logo, and to the wordmark when there is no logo at all**; **the dark accent falls back to the light accent**; and **`color_scheme` drives the body class** FR-E4 resolves against. A Light-only project therefore ships three settings whose logic is present and quiet — and the moment its owner fills one in, in Ghost Admin, it works, with no redeploy and no visit to Inflozo. That is a better outcome than the setting not existing, which is what the conditional design would have shipped.

  Color promotion is **accent-only in v1** (FR-Q3): promoting the accent creates the *light* setting, and its dark counterpart — the Dark accent built-in — **already exists in every project**, so both modes stay owner-controllable as a pair whether or not the project was authored with dark in mind. Other pack tokens have no dark built-in counterpart and are not promotable (deferred: Appendix G). Promoting the accent shows a one-time caution ("once this lives in Ghost Admin, contrast is in your hands" — AA cannot be re-validated post-deploy). Promoted colors compile as an inline token block in `default.hbs` (runtime values cannot live in static CSS).
- **FR-Q6** **Translations module:** every compiler-generated, visitor-facing **chrome string** — labels that are not user-editable text props: pagination ("Older posts", "Newer posts", "Page {page} of {pages}"), load-more/infinite-scroll labels, member form feedback (success/error/confirmation), error-page copy, search trigger and placeholder text, skip-link, lightbox/gallery labels, "Read more"-type card labels — lives in one **standard string catalog**, authored normatively in `appendix-h1-string-catalog.md` with a stable key and an English default per string. **Keys are dotted `namespace.name`, never the English string itself**, so improving a default never renames a key and never orphans a user's override. `.hbs` output consumes catalog strings **exclusively via Ghost's `{{t}}`** with `{placeholder}` hash params — never hard-coded literals — and compile validation enforces it. **Strings written by bundled JS are out of `{{t}}`'s reach**, because Ghost never runs `assets/js/main.js` through Handlebars: those keys are resolved at compile time (defaults plus overrides) and emitted as **`data-i18n-*` attributes on their module's mount element** (FR-J4), which validation asserts instead; the `{{t}}`-only rule is scoped to `.hbs` output explicitly. **`credit.*` is a locked namespace:** the "Built with Inflozo" strings live in the catalog so they compile through the same path, but they are **not listed in the Translations surface and are not overridable on any plan** — whether the credit *appears* is the plan question (FR-J15); what it *says* is not a question, and an override reaching the compiler for a `credit.*` key fails the build rather than being silently dropped. A dedicated **Translations** surface (sibling to Theme Settings, FR-Q1) lists the overridable catalog with English defaults and lets the user override any label, in any language. **The theme always ships `en.json`, plus the project-language file when the project language is not English.** Ghost's i18n falls back to `en` for any key the active locale does not resolve, and it falls back to the **file** — so a theme shipping only `de.json` renders **raw translation keys** wherever Ghost cannot resolve one, which is exactly what "exactly one locale file" would have shipped to every non-English project. **The two files cannot disagree**, and that is a property of how they are produced rather than a hope: the compiler emits both from the **one** catalog in the same pass — `en.json` carrying the English defaults, the project file carrying the same key set with that project's overrides applied — so the key sets are equal by construction and only the values differ. When the project language is English there is one file and it *is* `en.json`. **Language is stored on the project**, defaulted from the connected site's `@site.locale` at first link and always user-editable; switching the project to a site whose locale differs warns before the switch, because the overrides were written in the old language. **Catalog keys sit inside FR-J14's backward-compatibility contract:** append-only, never reworded in place, and a superseded key ships a migration map that carries the user's override forward. **RTL is not supported in v1** (Appendix G): selecting an RTL language code — or linking a site whose `@site.locale` is one — requires a blocking acknowledgement that generated themes are LTR-only, recorded and repeated as a pre-deploy warning. **Scope limit, stated plainly:** strings inside Ghost's own surfaces — Portal, native comments, and the Sodo search overlay — belong to Ghost's i18n namespaces and cannot be reached by a theme catalog; the catalog covers the theme-rendered search *trigger* and placeholder only. The canvas renders the user's overridden strings via the `{{t}}` shim (FR-H5); ZIP export includes the locale file.

- **FR-Q7** **Ghost card design module:** the user designs **every Ghost editor (Koenig) card** — callout, bookmark, toggle, button, gallery, image, audio, video, file, product, header, embed, code, table, blockquote and the rest — from a dedicated surface that lists the card types, shows which have been customised, and offers **reset to Ghost's default** per card. Each card's panel keeps to the same 4–7 control discipline as a section, with a live preview beside it rendered against the FR-H3 style-guide fixture. **Three cards are excluded from colour controls and from a Contrast Band's inversion (ruling R-6):** the signup, CTA and header cards carry the author's own inline colours, so a band's plane may run behind them but their surfaces stay the author's. **Gallery width is Ghost's, not the treatment's** — it is hard-coded `kg-width-wide` — and embed cards carry no width class at all, so "bleed applies to every media card" is false and is struck. **The HTML card emits no wrapper element**, so it cannot be a target of the Rules or Contrast Band values. **The public-preview cut is an HTML comment** (`<!--members-only-->`), not a component: nothing in A33 owns that edge, and the paywall partial follows it. Icons inside a treatment follow R-26 — **inline SVG from the curated Tabler set, once per use**. **Card corners come from the pack's radius token, and are not offered per card** *(architect's ruling, 2026-09-03, closing A33's finding 3)*. The controls-audit notes proposed a per-card corner matrix; A33 answered with the pack token instead and was right. A radius is a **Style Pack** decision (`Appendix D`, AD-30), so a card opting out of it would be the one element on the page disagreeing with everything around it — and per-card corners would be twenty controls whose only job is to disagree with the pack, which is the shape FR-F3's control ceiling exists to refuse. Recorded as declined so it is not proposed again.

  **The emission mechanism is normative, and it is not `!important`.** For every card the user has designed, the theme's `package.json` declares `card_assets: { exclude: [...] }` naming that card, so **Ghost emits nothing for it** — no competing stylesheet to out-specify and no specificity contest to lose. Inflozo's CSS is then the only CSS. Three consequences bind the implementation. **(1)** `card_assets: false` is **forbidden**: a card Ghost ships in a future release is not in the exclude list and must keep Ghost's defaults rather than arriving unstyled on every user's site. **(2)** Excluding a card drops **both** its stylesheet **and** its JavaScript, so the four cards whose behaviour depends on Ghost's JS — **audio, video, gallery and toggle** — ship Inflozo's own vendored equivalent (Ghost's implementation is MIT) whenever they are designed; without it the players are inert, gallery proportions collapse and toggles never open. **(3)** `header` and `header_v2` are **separate exclude names** and both must be listed. **Colour controls are not offered for `header_v2`, `signup` and `cta`**, whose colours the post author sets inline per post from Ghost's editor: a control that silently loses to an inline style is worse than no control, and the author has already chosen. Excluding a card also **restores gscan's Koenig rules for it**, so checking scales with what Inflozo actually wrote (FR-J6).

- **FR-Q8** **Translation override validation:** overrides entered in the Translations surface (FR-Q6) are **validated on entry** — a syntactically invalid override cannot be saved, and the error names the problem in the user's own string rather than in catalog terms. Compile re-validates as a backstop and fails the build rather than emitting an unparseable catalog. This is not defensive tidiness: Ghost's translation layer parses each string with an ICU MessageFormat constructor that throws **outside** its own error handling, so a single unbalanced brace in one override escapes into the page renderer and returns a **whole-page 500 across the entire site** — not a fallback string, not a missing label. The one user-typed value that reaches this path therefore carries the same validation obligation as any other untrusted input (NFR-3). **Validation REFUSES; it does not repair — and the reason is executed, not assumed.** Braces in an override are reserved for ICU placeholders (`{count}`), and there is **no way for a user to type a literal brace at all**: against Ghost 6.58.0's bundled `intl-messageformat` 5.4.3, the standard ICU escape `'{'` **throws** just as an unbalanced brace does, and the numeric-entity form `&#123;` — which the compiler uses for user text under the brace-safe emission rule — **parses but does not render**, because Ghost's `{{t}}` helper HTML-escapes its output and emits `&amp;#123;`, so the visitor reads a literal `&#123;` on the page. Both routes were tried and both fail, so the validator's only correct behaviour is to refuse, name the offending character in the user's own string, and say that braces mark a placeholder. **A consequence worth keeping:** because `{{t}}` escapes, a translation override cannot inject markup — `<script>` in an override renders as visible text — so this input's risk is the 500, not injection.

- **FR-Q9** **Treatment selection fallback:** the non-placeable treatments — Koenig card styling, pagination styling — are selected from **Theme Settings** whenever the section that would otherwise host the choice is absent. A project with no post-content section still styles its cards; a template with no feed still carries a pagination treatment for when one is added. Emission (FR-J3) never depends on a host section existing, and the selection survives that section's removal.

- **FR-Q10** **Share destinations are one ordered, site-wide list, and it is Inflozo's** *(ruling R-29 — this is the FR R11 called for)*. A project declares **one** ordered list of share destinations in Theme Settings (FR-Q1's surface); every design that offers sharing renders **that** list, in **that** order, so a reader meets the same set on a post footer, a share rail and a share row. **It is stored as one row on the project doc** (AD-27) rather than per section — a per-section list is how two share rows on one site end up disagreeing. **The destinations are emitted as real `<a href>` URLs** built at compile from the post's own canonical URL: no script, no SDK, no third-party button, and the whole set works with JavaScript off (FR-G7's zero-dependency rule is not bent for this). Copy-link is the one destination needing a script and it is the `share` module's only job. **Ghost's own Portal share page is NOT used, and the reason is a Ghost fact, not a preference:** it does not exist on Ghost 5 — where the link silently opens the **sign-in** modal instead — and where it does exist it is a shadow-DOM iframe a theme cannot style, with a fixed order and no Mastodon (Appendix B, `MEASUREMENTS.md` §29b/c). The default list ships as an ordered set the owner may reorder or trim; **an empty list renders no share affordance at all** rather than an empty container.

### FR-J · Theme Compiler & Deploy

#### Compile

- **FR-J1** Compiler input: project doc (per-template ordered section instances with content values, control values, dark overrides) + Style Pack + routes config + referenced assets. **Templates with no doc are synthesized per `sections-inventory.md` § Synthesis Defaults before assembly** (FR-I1) — an untouched template has no doc at all, so without this step the compiler emits nothing for it and the Synthesis Defaults are specification-only. **Mechanism (mandated; empirically validated by `spike-compiler/`):** sections are authored as annotated HTML; the compiler walks the DOM, substitutes content and control values, injects Handlebars block helpers as HTML comment markers, serializes with `outerHTML`, and resolves markers and expression tokens in a final pass. Handlebars is never parsed, evaluated or printed anywhere in the product — there is no partial evaluator and no AST printer. Two mechanics are binding on the implementation, both discovered in the spike: Handlebars expressions are carried through serialization as **opaque ASCII tokens** swapped back afterwards, so expressions containing quotes survive intact inside attributes; and **comment-wrapped markers are unwrapped before token substitution**, because substituting first makes a marker Inflozo inserted indistinguishable from a comment a section author wrote. **User content is emitted inert:** any Handlebars syntax a user types into a text prop (`{{title}}`, `{{{x}}}`) is escaped so it renders as literal characters and is never interpreted by Ghost. **Output quality is a requirement, not a by-product** — the generated theme must be hand-editable and production grade, at Casper/Source quality: repeating markup whose content comes from **Ghost** (post cards, pagination, bylines, subscribe forms) is extracted into real partials invoked with **no parameters**, because `{{#foreach}}` supplies the context, while markup whose content comes from the **design** is inlined and baked; class names derive from section names; one organized stylesheet carries the tokens at its top; section boundaries carry comments; `package.json` is complete; **the compiler emits consistently indented output directly** — Prettier is not used and cannot be, since it throws on Ghost partial syntax and silently deletes the doctype (FR-J17 lints what it produces). **That makes formatting a real implementation requirement rather than a claim**, because `outerHTML` does not indent: the serializer (§7.3) produces the formatting, to a stated contract — **two-space indentation**, one attribute per line once a tag exceeds the line budget, a blank line between section boundaries and none inside them, and **`{{!-- {Layer name} · {Category} · {Design} --}}`** as the boundary comment, bound to the same layer-name source §7.4 binds the filename to so a rename moves both together. The contract is normative because it is deterministic output that NFR-6(c1)'s committed snapshots diff against — ragged formatting would make every snapshot diff unreadable and every real change invisible inside it. **The spike does not yet meet this** (its output is visibly ragged) and is labelled accordingly rather than treated as the reference for formatting; and no builder fingerprints appear anywhere in the output. Output: a complete Ghost 5.x/6.x-compatible theme matching the structure in §7.4, emitting **no `engines.ghost-api` key** — gscan carries `GS010-PJ-GHOST-API-PRESENT` as a standing *warning* on both the v5 and v6 specs, so emitting it would guarantee a warning on every compile against FR-J6's 0/0 target; compatibility comes from the helper surface the library uses, not from a `package.json` declaration (NFR-7).
- **FR-J2** `package.json`: project-derived name/description/version; **`engines.ghost: ">=5.0.0"`** — the floor Ghost checks at upload, and `>=6.0.0` would contradict NFR-7's public 5.x support with nothing to catch it; note that `engines.ghost` and `engines.ghost-api` are **different keys** and only the latter is withheld (FR-J1). **`card_assets` per FR-Q7** — `{ exclude: [...] }` naming every card the user designed, never a blanket `true` and never `false`; **`engines.ghost: ">=5.0.0"`** for as long as NFR-7 claims 5.x support, and **never `engines.ghost-api`**; the **normative `image_sizes` map below**; `posts_per_page` from Theme Settings (FR-Q1) — emitted as a **JSON number ≥ 1**, never a string: `"12"` from a form field trips `GS010-PJ-CONF-PPP-INT` at **error** level and blocks the deploy, while omitting it is only a recommendation, so the compiler **coerces to integer**. Also required, all at error level unless noted: `name` **lowercase** and matching `^([a-z0-9]+-)*[a-z0-9]+$`; `version` valid semver; `author.email` present and RFC-valid; `keywords` containing `"ghost-theme"` (warning). Levels are identical on the v5 and v6 specs. `custom` = the dark-mode built-ins (FR-Q5 — **all three, on every project**) plus any user-defined custom settings from FR-Q2 (cap-enforced, P3).

  **`image_sizes` (normative).** `xs 150` · `s 400` · `m 750` · `l 1200` · `xl 2000`. Ghost generates a rendition per declared key and `{{img_url size="m"}}` resolves against **this** map — with no map, every sized URL silently returns the original, so a 4000px hero serves behind a 300px card and nothing reports it (gscan does not validate `image_sizes` at all). **Compile asserts that every `size=` argument in emitted `.hbs` exists as a key here.** Three ceilings exist and are deliberately different: these five keys size *Ghost-hosted content images*; FR-J3's `400 / 800 / 1600 + original` rendition set covers *theme-bundled assets*, which Ghost never resizes; and FR-K2 caps *uploads* at 2400px on the long edge. A `srcset` built from the wrong set is a live defect, so the three are never conflated. NFR-6(c2)'s shim recordings are made against an **Inflozo-emitted** `package.json`, because Ghost's resize behaviour follows the theme's own map.
- **FR-J3** Assets: only assets actually referenced by the project are copied into `assets/images/` with content-hashed filenames, **each emitted as a fixed rendition set — 400 / 800 / 1600 px wide plus the original** — so FR-J5's `srcset` has real files behind it rather than a promise. Fonts are **self-hosted**: the compiler bundles woff2 subsets of the project's font pairing (from the curated ~30-pairing pool, FR-E1) into `assets/fonts/` with `@font-face` and `font-display: swap`. **Subsetting is static, by script range, never content-driven** — the theme renders posts written after deploy, which the compiler cannot see: latin + latin-ext by default, and a project whose language (FR-Q6) falls outside that range ships the **full face** for the covering script, with the size consequence surfaced in the budget below rather than silently absorbed. Generated themes make no third-party font requests (Google Fonts hotlinking is a documented GDPR liability for EU site owners, and a runtime dependency P5 forbids). **Section CSS and JS are emitted per *design*, not per category, and both leave when the design does** (FR-G7): a placed design contributes its stylesheet and its module, an unplaced one contributes neither, and the compiler **strips the CSS the placed designs cannot reach** rather than shipping the dead branches the data-attribute mechanism would otherwise leave behind. **The strip is sound only because a design never becomes another design at render (AD-37, ruling R-8), and compile CI asserts exactly that** (register 49): no emitted stylesheet may contain a rule reachable only under another design's root attribute. If a design could swap at render the strip would remove CSS the page later needs, so this assertion is what makes the optimisation safe rather than merely usually-right — it is the check, not the rule, that register 49 was holding open. Global CSS = token block + base/reset + shared primitives + `cards.css` (the designed Koenig cards, FR-Q7). **A theme-size budget is enforced and surfaced pre-deploy** — total zip, per-entry and uncompressed total — because Ghost rejects oversized uploads with `COMPRESSED_TOO_LARGE`, `ENTRY_TOO_LARGE` and `TOTAL_TOO_LARGE` at host-configured limits (Ghost(Pro) Starter is separately documented at 5 MB). The real limits are probed on the §4 targets and the budget is set below the lowest observed; a project over budget is told which assets are responsible **before** it attempts a deploy.
- **FR-J4** **JS bundling — two inputs, and only one of them is Inflozo's.** `assets/js/main.js` carries the **behaviour modules from FR-G7's registry**, bundled **only if used**: a module is emitted when a design that declares it is placed, and it leaves when that design does (FR-G7, FR-J3). Vanilla JS, no framework, `defer`red. Every module carries its FR-Q6 strings as `data-i18n-*` attributes on its mount element; no module contains a visitor-facing literal. **`assets/js/cards.js` is a separate file with a separate origin** — Ghost's own MIT card behaviour, vendored because FR-Q7's `card_assets: { exclude: [...] }` drops Ghost's JS along with its CSS, and emitted only for designed audio/video/gallery/toggle cards. Keeping the two apart is what makes FR-G7's **zero third-party runtime dependencies** claim literally true rather than approximately true: `main.js` is repo-authored code and nothing else, which compile CI asserts by inspection of `assets/js/` (FR-G7); `cards.js` is Ghost's code shipped back to a Ghost site, and it is declared as such in the theme README.
- **FR-J5** Generated markup follows Ghost theme requirements: `{{ghost_head}}`/`{{ghost_foot}}`, `{{body_class}}`, and **`{{post_class}}` on the wrapping `<article>` — never on `<body>`**. `{{post_class}}` carries `post`, `tag-{slug}`, `featured`, `no-image` and `page`; it does **not** carry members-visibility classes, so the theme emits those itself (`{{#unless access}} post-access-{{visibility}}{{/unless}}`) or every members site silently loses its gated-card styling. `no-image` is the class FR-H8's guards most often pair with. no deprecated helpers, `{{img_url}}` **plus a theme-authored `srcset`** — the helper returns a single URL string and emits no `srcset` at all, so the theme composes one from FR-J2's `image_sizes` keys (Ghost content) or FR-J3's rendition set (bundled assets) — with WebP and lazy loading below the fold for Ghost-hosted content images (theme-bundled assets ship pre-generated responsive sizes from compile), portal `data-portal` attributes, paywall via the `content-cta.hbs` partial Ghost renders at the members-only cutoff (FR-H6); all chrome strings emit via `{{t}}` against the shipped `locales/` files (FR-Q6) — no hard-coded visitor-facing literals.
- **FR-J6** **gscan gate:** every compile runs gscan server-side. Errors block deploy with human-readable mapping; warnings are surfaced but deployable. **What gscan certifies is narrow, and FR-J17 exists because of it** — a theme with no `lang`, no viewport, no `alt` text, an inline `onclick`, scrambled heading order, 1.5:1 contrast and four missing templates scores 0/0 on both specs. **One gscan failure mode must not be passed through verbatim:** a malformed `visibility` string cascades into ~19 errors including a bogus JSON-parse failure reported against a perfectly valid `package.json`. Inflozo detects that signature and replaces it with its own explanation naming the real cause, because the raw output sends the user to a file that is not the problem. Target for all library output: **0 errors, 0 warnings** — reachable, and confirmed empirically by `spike-compiler/` against both the v5 and v6 specs, once FR-J1 omits `engines.ghost-api` and FR-I1 gates `page.hbs` on `@page.show_title_and_feature_image`. **Version policy:** Inflozo pins one gscan version, at or above the newest version bundled by any supported Ghost target, so a theme passing Inflozo's gate is not rejected by the target's own gate on upload; a gscan upgrade lands only behind a library release with a full re-run of the whole inventory, never silently, because a bump can turn the shipped library red and block every user's deploy at once. **The human-readable mapping is scoped to the reachable shortlist** — the rules Inflozo's own output can actually trip, not gscan's full ~100 — and any unmapped rule falls back to a stated verbatim format (rule code, gscan's own message, the file, and a docs link), so no compile ever surfaces a raw stack trace. **`GS100` is on the reachable shortlist and can never fire**, and the reason is worth stating because three keys in every theme depend on it: it triggers on a `@custom` key declared and not referenced, and FR-Q5's fallback logic references all three on every compile, in every project. If a future gscan bump changes that trigger, three settings in every shipped theme go red at once — which is why §7.6 asks for GS100's exact trigger to be re-proved on a fixture rather than assumed.
- **FR-J7** Artifacts: each successful compile stores the zip in a `deploy-artifacts` bucket (excluded from the user's asset quota). Retention: last 10 per project (Pro) / last 3 (Free), **and pinning is part of the retention rule rather than a separate feature** *(owner decision 2026-08-21, `architecture-.../BACKUP-GATE.md` § Rollback retention; written here 2026-09-03 during step 5, which found the decision had never reached this document — a story built from the PRD alone would have shipped a history with no pin control. Ruling R-79)*. **A version can be pinned to survive pruning, and pinned versions count against the same total.** Three rules bind the surface (FR-J9): **(1) the limit is stated, not implied** — a list that silently drops its oldest entry reads as complete when it is not, so the count is printed on both plans, alongside the reason it is a real bound: these artifacts are **not regenerable** (AD-29, decision D10), because rebuilding an old design against today's library produces a different theme, which is why rollback replays a stored file rather than recompiling. **(2) The history never shows a version it cannot restore** — a pruned artifact is absent from the list, never a greyed row with a dead Restore button. **(3) At most N−1 versions may be pinned** — 9 of 10 on Pro, 2 of 3 on Free — **enforced in the database**, so that deploying can never be blocked by pinning; the pin control therefore needs a **visible refusal with its reason** rather than a silent failure, and **unpinning is always allowed**, by design, so nobody can trap themselves. **The pre-Inflozo snapshot is outside this count entirely** (FR-J13 exempts it from pruning) and is listed as the site's original theme rather than as a version.

#### Deploy & rollback

- **FR-J8** Deploy: server-side upload to the site's Admin API (themes upload endpoint) with optional **Activate** (owner requirement: "deploy only" vs "deploy and activate" are distinct buttons). Progress UI: Compiling → Checking (gscan) → Uploading → (Activating) → Live, with friendly error surfacing (auth failed → reconnect CTA; version mismatch; Starter-plan block per FR-C2). **Compiling and Checking are the Pre-flight step and run before "Ship it"; Ship it uploads the artefact they produced** — one compile per deploy, on one `deploy_jobs` row (AD-20; a stale artefact re-runs Compiling first). Cancel is available during Compiling/Checking; once Uploading starts, the deploy runs to completion (idempotent; rollback covers regret).

  **Upload succeeded, activation failed — the one true behaviour, because four requirements used to disagree about it.** This is a **partial success**, not a failure, and it is recorded as one: the deploy row's `status` is **`uploaded`** and its `activated` flag is **false** (§7.5). Six consequences follow, and none of them is left to inference. **(1) The artifact is retained** like any successful compile (FR-J7) and appears in deploy history **without an active badge** (FR-J9), so a retry is a one-click re-activation rather than a recompile. **(2) The theme name freezes**, because the name is claimed on the site the moment a theme lands there — matching FR-J13's snapshot trigger, which fires on first **upload** including a deploy-only one, so the two rules agree by design rather than by coincidence (FR-J10, FR-J13). **(3) The drift baseline does *not* move**: FR-J16 compares against what Inflozo last put **live**, and an uploaded-but-inactive theme is not what visitors are served. **(4) FR-J11's "never leaves a partially active theme" is unaffected and means what it says** — the site's active theme is still the previous one, untouched; nothing is half-applied. **(5) The user is told plainly** — "Your theme is on your site but isn't live yet" — with a re-activate action, because "failed" would be wrong and silence would be worse. **(6) It sends no deploy-failure email** (FR-P1 email 5): the theme uploaded, the user is in the product looking at the result, and the state is recoverable in one click.
- **FR-J9** **Rollback:** deploy history per project×site lists version, timestamp, gscan summary, active badge; any retained artifact redeploys in one click, **and a pin control per version, whose three rules are FR-J7's** *(R-79)*: the retention limit is printed on both plans, a pruned artifact is absent rather than greyed, and pinning the last unpinned version refuses visibly with its reason. The list also carries two rows that are not ordinary versions: **the pre-Inflozo snapshot**, above the versions, outside the count, actioned as *restore original* (FR-J13); and **an `uploaded`-but-not-`activated` row**, shown without an active badge and actioned as *re-activate* rather than as a recompile (FR-J8). Rollback is exempt from FR-L3's Pro exit gating — restoring an artifact that already ran is always allowed, on any plan. Rollback and snapshot restore (FR-J13) redeploy stored artifacts, not the working document, so they never require the edit lock (FR-D18).
- **FR-J10** Theme naming: `inflozo-{project-slug}`, auto-incremented semver per deploy; version shown in Ghost Admin and the history list. **The theme name freezes per site, at the project's first deploy to that site** — each site gets its own frozen name, and deploying the project to a different site later freezes a fresh name there. Renaming the project afterwards changes its display name in Inflozo only, so renames never orphan old themes in Ghost Admin and rollback history stays coherent per site. **Uniqueness is scoped to what Inflozo can actually know.** Ghost blocks `GET /themes/` for Custom Integration tokens, so Inflozo cannot enumerate a site's installed themes; whether the Owner's Staff Access Token lifts that block is a §7.6 verify-at-build item, and until it is confirmed the frozen name is tracked in Inflozo's own per-project×site binding record — which is exempt from FR-J7's artifact pruning, so pruning can never lose it and re-derive a fresh name. A collision between two Inflozo projects on one site auto-resolves with a slug suffix (e.g. `inflozo-blog-2`), surfaced to the user, and never deadlocks on the frozen name. A collision with a theme Inflozo did not deploy cannot be detected in advance and Ghost would overwrite rather than error; the `inflozo-` prefix makes it vanishingly unlikely, and the first-deploy confirmation names the exact theme that will be created so the user can catch it.
- **FR-J11** Deploys are rate-limited (10/hour per site) and idempotent; **the limit counts the EXPORT path too, and there is a per-account compile budget above it** *(register 35, the owner's direction)*. Both halves matter and the first is a real hole: the limit derives from `deploys.created_at` scoped by site, while FR-J12's export runs **the same compile pipeline** and writes `exports` with a null `site_id` — so a per-site count misses it entirely and an account could compile without limit by exporting. The counting method is therefore **per-account over compiles**, whatever they are called, with the per-site deploy limit sitting inside it. **The budget is set against a measured number, not an imagined worst case:** one deploy costs **$0.000427** (3.9 s compile + 8.8 s upload and activate), so a Pro account at 25 sites deploying flat out reaches **$76.84/month** and **0.88 concurrent invocations** (`MEASUREMENTS.md`). This is billing hygiene, not capacity — the budget exists to make a runaway visible and stoppable, and it is sized to be invisible to every honest user. A failed upload never leaves a partially active theme; a failed upload never leaves a partially active theme — **and an upload that succeeds while activation fails leaves the site's previous theme active and untouched**, which is a recorded partial-success state rather than a partial application (FR-J8). **Deploy + activate holds a per-site advisory lock**, so two projects targeting the same site serialize rather than interleave — theme activation is globally stateful and a rate limit is not a mutex. **The §4 test targets are exempt from the rate limit:** a single DoD run is 30 serialized deploys, which the limit alone would stretch past three hours before compile time, and every re-run would pay it again.

#### Lifecycle

- **FR-J12** **Theme ZIP export (all plans):** any project can download its compiled theme zip, Free included — **including a project that is read-only because the account is over its Free project cap** (FR-L3): read-only means not editable, never locked in. Projects containing Pro sections on a Free account are blocked per FR-L3's exit rule. Export gating mirrors deploy gating exactly. **Every export is recorded** (project, version, timestamp), because FR-Q2 freezes custom-setting keys "once deployed **or exported**" and that rule needs a fact to test against.
- **FR-J13** **Pre-Inflozo snapshot ("safe installs"):** at Inflozo's first theme upload to a site (deploy-only included — manual activation in Ghost Admin must not bypass the safety net), download and archive the currently active theme as a restorable artifact (excluded from asset quota, exempt from history-retention pruning). **Capture is real, not best-effort.** Ghost's `tokenPermissionCheck` allowlists only `themes: ['POST','PUT']` for Custom Integration tokens, so every `GET /themes/*` call made with an Admin API key returns 403 — on every Ghost version and every host, self-hosted 5.x, 6.x and Ghost(Pro) alike. Inflozo therefore reads the live theme with the site **Owner's Staff Access Token** (FR-C1), which that allowlist does not bind. A **pre-deploy gate** before the first deploy to a site (`BACKUP-GATE.md`) asks the user to confirm they hold their own copy of the current theme and `routes.yaml`. **It offers the two as downloads only when the Staff Access Token is present** — the token lifts `GET /themes/` (VERIFY-AT-BUILD item 9), and the gate never promises a file it cannot fetch — **and otherwise names the Ghost Admin path** for each, described and linked for the connected version rather than hard-coded *(owner, 2026-09-03, F-077 — the sentence previously offered both unconditionally, which the token-less path cannot honour)*. The snapshot is Inflozo's safety net, not the user's only one. Where the Staff token is absent or revoked the degraded path is a **designed flow, not a warning toast** — before activation the user is told no snapshot could be captured, and that Ghost retains the previous theme under Settings → Design, with guidance to reactivate it there. **Restore scope is signature-gated on the `package.json` marker, not the theme name.** Every Inflozo-built theme carries an `inflozo-*` name **and** a `package.json` marker, and the marker alone governs the rule: a user can export a theme, rename its package and install it manually (FR-J12), so keying on the name would let Inflozo capture its own theme as that site's "original" and turn restore-to-original into restore-to-Inflozo. A theme carrying the marker is **never** captured as a snapshot. Rollback and automated restore are guaranteed for Inflozo-signed themes (rebuilt from Inflozo's own stored artifacts, no download endpoint needed); a third-party active theme is archived and otherwise never touched. Restoring a snapshot is a one-click redeploy that skips *Inflozo's* gscan gate — but **Ghost validates every upload with its own gscan**, so an old Casper fork or a marketplace theme installed years ago on a since-upgraded site can be rejected on the way back in. That case has a designed fallback too: Inflozo offers the snapshot zip as a download and points the user at Settings → Design, where Ghost still holds the previous theme. Snapshots are stored per **site record id** (FR-C6), survive project deletion and disconnect/reconnect, and are offered as a download before account purge (FR-A5).
- **FR-J14** **Library updates & redeploy:** the section library and Style Packs are versioned with Ghost compatibility in mind. When the library advances beyond what a project's last deploy used — **each deploy records its library version and the variant manifest it shipped (FR-G3)**, which is what makes "advanced beyond" a fact rather than a guess — the project shows an "Updates available" notice with a human-readable summary of what changed, grouped by category and naming the project's affected placed sections. That summary is generated from a **machine-readable library changelog** shipped with every release: one entry per change carrying its kind (new design · superseded design · schema migration · catalog key · Style Pack), the ids it touches, and one line of prose. Compiles always use the current library version (a single live library, no per-project pinning — chosen for implementation simplicity); consequently **any redeploy includes all library changes since the project's last deploy** — there is no way to redeploy without them — and the canvas always renders the current library. Because the update is inseparable from the redeploy, the deploy flow makes it consensual: whenever the library has advanced since the project's last deploy, a **mandatory confirm step** lists the changes ("This deploy includes library updates: … — continue?") before compile proceeds. A live site never changes except through a user-initiated redeploy plus this confirmation; the one exemption is FR-C5's compatibility redeploy, which re-ships a design that already ran. Library releases are bound by a **backward-compatibility contract**: designs are never deleted, only superseded (hidden from the Section Picker; placed instances keep rendering); section content/control schema changes are append-only or ship a migration map applied to existing project docs; and **the string catalog is inside the contract too** — catalog keys are append-only, never reworded in place, and a superseded key ships a migration map carrying the user's override forward (FR-Q6, `appendix-h1-string-catalog.md`). Because designs are never deleted, a placed section can fail to resolve **only when a schema migration fails**: that case, and only that case, degrades to the nearest current design — defined mechanically as the design in the same category sharing the instance's `bindingContext` and `compileTarget` with the greatest content-schema overlap — with a visible notice. Never a silent re-render, never a failed load, and no deletion path to build. **Post-launch cadence (retention commitment):** the library keeps growing after GA on a regular cadence — target: monthly drops of new designs and/or Style Packs — delivered through this update flow; the Evergreen differentiator (§1.4) is an ongoing commitment, not a launch artifact. Because the count moves every month, user-facing copy about the library is **count-agnostic** ("hundreds of gorgeous sections"); the inventory is a GA floor stated in FR-G1, never a number quoted in product microcopy (Appendix H).
- **FR-J15** **Credits & white-label:** generated themes include a "Built with Inflozo" credit in both README.md and the theme footer by default. Pro accounts can disable both per project; Free deploys and exports always retain credits. The A3 footer sections' credit toggle is this same per-project setting surfaced in context — Pro-gated, locked on for Free. If a Free design has no visible footer section, the compiler appends a minimal credit line to `default.hbs`'s footer region.
- **FR-J16** **Pre-deploy drift detection:** before every deploy after the first to a given site, Inflozo reads the live theme with the Owner's Staff Access Token (FR-J13) and compares a **content manifest** — relative path → SHA-256 of file contents, line endings normalized — against the manifest of what Inflozo last deployed there. **Zip bytes are never compared:** Ghost re-zips on download, so timestamps, entry ordering and compression differ every time and a byte comparison would report drift on every single deploy. The comparison targets the **frozen theme name for this project×site** (FR-J10), not merely whatever theme happens to be active, so a user who activated something else is not misread as having hand-edited Inflozo's. On any difference the deploy **stops** and shows exactly which files changed, were added or were removed — named by the user's own layer names wherever a file is a section partial, never by raw paths — and offers to download the live theme before overwriting it. **The check fails open:** a revoked token, a network failure, a missing theme or a 403 reports "couldn't verify" and the deploy proceeds; drift is never asserted without two manifests in hand. It is skipped on the first deploy to a site, where there is nothing of Inflozo's to compare against. The requirement is **zero false positives**, which binds the compiler as much as the check: a rename in Layers is a real change and is reported as one, and conversely **a compiled filename must never change when the user changed nothing** — an unstable name would report drift the user did not cause and void the guarantee.

  **Identity is content, not path.** Every Inflozo-generated file carries a **fingerprint**, and drift asks "did this content change from what Inflozo wrote", so a **layer rename produces no drift signal at all** — nothing on the live site changed, only a filename Inflozo chose. A manifest keyed on path alone could not say that: the path moves, and a path-keyed comparison reads a rename as a deletion plus an addition. Fingerprints keep both promises that were previously in tension — layer-named files stay navigable for a hand-editor (§7.4), and FR-J16's zero-false-positive guarantee holds. **Frozen section-partial filenames were rejected as the alternative** because they decay: a name frozen at first deploy stops describing the layer it came from the first time the user renames anything.

  **Determinism survives, serving a different purpose.** §7.4's rule that the same project doc compiles to the same filenames on every machine and every run is no longer what drift rests on — it is what **NFR-6(c1)'s committed snapshots** rest on, and it is still required for that reason. And the comparison target is unchanged and load-bearing: **what Inflozo last deployed**, never what it is about to deploy. Comparing against the pending build would make every rename read as drift regardless of any naming scheme. **The converse is not drift's job:** a *custom-template* rename is a user change and a forbidden one, prevented at the naming step (§7.4, FR-I3), because by the time drift could report it the pages pointing at the old filename have already detached.

- **FR-J17** **Emitted-theme quality gate:** every compiled theme passes an automated quality gate **in addition to** gscan, before it can deploy. The gate asserts, at minimum: valid HTML; a `lang` attribute and viewport meta; correct heading order; an accessible name for every link and control; `alt` handling that satisfies NFR-5 on both the image-only-link and figure cases; AA contrast on emitted text against the Style Pack's own tokens; the presence of every template §7.4 requires; no inline event handlers; and CSS that lints clean against the Baseline browser floor (§7.3). **The gate exists because gscan does not measure what FR-J1 claims.** Demonstrated empirically: a theme with no `lang`, no viewport, no `alt` text, an inline `onclick`, `<h4>`→`<h1>`→`<h6>` heading order, 1.5:1 body contrast and four missing templates scores **0 errors and 0 warnings on both the v5 and v6 specs**, while Ghost's own Casper and Source each *fail* a rule. gscan validates that a theme is a Ghost theme; FR-J17 validates that it is a good one, and the "marketplace-quality" claim in FR-J1 rests on this gate rather than on gscan's zero. **The gate stops at the edge of `{{content}}` (ruling R-6).** Its heading-order assertion and NFR-5's accessible-name assertions **do not inspect the inside of a post body**. Ghost emits its own markup there — a toggle card is `div.kg-toggle-card[data-kg-toggle-state] > h4 + button`, not a `<details>` — so a gate that walked into `{{content}}` would fail every post containing one: our own quality apparatus failing on Ghost's markup, on a customer's content. A theme owns `cards.css` and nothing else inside the body, so it is assessed on what it owns.

### FR-K · Asset Library

- **FR-K1** **Global per-account** library (used across all projects), grid view with search, type filter (Image/SVG/Logo), and sort (Newest/Name/Size). **There is no Video asset type, and the amendment that would have added one is cancelled rather than deferred** (ruling R-9, half-reversing D27). A15's *Upload a video* source is cut: Ghost's Content API has no media resource, AD-10's write allowlist has no media upload, and Inflozo does not become a video host. **A15 is embeds only** — every video reaches a section as an oEmbed URL. **And nothing fetches from the provider** (ruling R-35, chosen more strictly than the room proposed): neither Inflozo's server nor the editor's browser contacts YouTube, Vimeo or any other provider, at any point. The embed card's **title, description and poster are authored fields the customer fills in**, which A15 already declares — they are required, not fetched. Two consequences follow and are recorded rather than left to be rediscovered: AD-10 and AD-23 need no oEmbed provision, and **no CORS question remains to test**, so the oEmbed half of probe family 36 is **cancelled**. The consequence D27 carried with it goes too: *Ambient loop (muted)* had uploaded files as its only source, so it is cut. **D27's second half stands** — an embed still refuses autoplay (D39). FR-J3 therefore creates no `assets/media` directory.
- **FR-K2** Upload via drag-drop/multi-select. **Client-side optimization before upload:** raster images convert to WebP (q≈82), max long edge 2400 px, through the browser's **native `canvas.toBlob('image/webp')`** — supported by every browser inside NFR-7's floor, Safari included — Safari gained it in 16.4, comfortably below the 17.2 the FR-G8 pin now sets, so the claim has margin rather than sitting on the line. A bundled encoder or server-side transcode exists **only as a runtime fallback** for a browser that reports no WebP encode support; shipping the encoder unconditionally would add ~300 KB of wasm that no supported browser needs. Input cap 10 MB/file; SVGs sanitized (script/foreignObject stripped). Original filename preserved as display name. Optimization is destructive by design — originals are not retained; the upload UI states the trade-off ("Images are optimized (WebP, max 2400 px); originals aren't stored"). No browser silently uploads an unoptimized original (acceptance criterion).
- **FR-K3** Quota metering with an always-visible meter on the library page (Free 100 MB / Pro 5 GB) and upload-time enforcement with upgrade prompt.
- **FR-K4** Per-asset usage: "Used in 3 projects" chip; deleting a used asset warns and lists affected projects (deleting replaces with a placeholder in canvases; compile blocks until resolved). **Usage is tracked in a server-side index recomputed when a project doc is upserted**, never derived by scanning every doc at render time — FR-K1's grid shows a chip per asset, and N document scans per page load does not survive contact with a real library. The index is therefore **stale by up to one cloud-sync interval** (FR-D10): a doc can reference an asset for minutes before the server learns of it. Delete accordingly **re-verifies against the client's live document** before proceeding, so the warning is never wrong in the direction that loses a user's image.
- **FR-K5** Editor delivery via Supabase Storage CDN (cached egress); at deploy, referenced assets are bundled into the zip (P5).
- **FR-K6** Asset detail actions: **Replace** (swaps the file behind the asset; every usage across all projects updates), **Copy URL**, **Download**.

### FR-L · Plans & Billing (Dodo Payments)

- **FR-L1** Plans per Appendix F. Checkout via Dodo hosted checkout (monthly $15 / yearly $150), Dodo as merchant of record (taxes handled by Dodo). Checkout pre-selects the **yearly** plan (two months free), with monthly one click away — both prices always shown, never a dark pattern. **The stated plan mix is 60% yearly / 40% monthly**, and Appendix F's blended net per Pro customer and its break-even are computed from that mix rather than from the monthly price alone: the same fee schedule nets materially less per month on $150 than on $15, so a yearly default that is not modeled overstates revenue. No time-boxed Pro trial: the open canvas (FR-L3) is the trial. **Checkout collects explicit consent to immediate delivery of the digital service and an explicit waiver of the statutory withdrawal right**, recorded with the order — without it the no-refund policy (FR-L2) is not lawfully enforceable in EU/UK markets, and the public statement of those terms is the policy page in FR-N1.
- **FR-L2** Webhooks drive an `entitlements` state machine mapped to **Dodo's actual event vocabulary** — `subscription.active`, `subscription.renewed`, `subscription.on_hold`, `subscription.failed`, `subscription.updated`, plus `payment.succeeded` / `payment.failed`. **There is no documented `subscription.cancelled`:** cancellation is detected from `subscription.updated` plus a direct read of the subscription, never inferred from an event name that does not exist. Transitions: `free → pro_active` (activation) · `pro_active → pro_past_due` (payment failed; banner) · `pro_past_due → pro_active` (late payment recovers) · `pro_past_due → free` (grace expires) · `pro_active → free` **at period end** when auto-renew has been stopped · `pro_active → free` **immediately on a dispute being opened**, because a chargeback happens regardless of refund policy and must never leave an account `pro_active` with a full term remaining — **and the reverse edge exists, because disputes are not all fraud.** A dispute a customer opened by mistake, or one resolved in the merchant's favour, has a defined path back: `free → pro_active` **restored by manual support action**, recorded with the reason and the resolving Dodo event, and restoring the **remainder of the original paid term** rather than starting a new one. The mechanism is deliberately manual — dispute resolution arrives on Dodo's timeline in a shape Inflozo does not control, and auto-restoring on an event Inflozo has not verified is how an account flips twice. Restoration lifts every downgrade consequence (FR-L3) at once and sends **no** email, per FR-P2: the user asked for it and is present when it happens. Appendix F.6's churn should not treat a dispute as permanent attrition. A plan switch (monthly ↔ yearly) arrives as `subscription.updated` and changes price and period only, never entitlement. **The 7-day grace is Inflozo's window**, reconciled against Dodo's `on_hold` state rather than assumed equal to it — Dodo's dunning timing is Dodo's. **Policy (owner): no refunds, and no mid-cycle cancellation.** Cancelling stops auto-renew; access continues to the end of the paid period and then downgrades to Free, entering FR-L3's block-until-resolved flow. Inflozo never auto-deletes anything, on any path. Webhook handlers are idempotent and signature-verified. On return from Dodo checkout, the server verifies the subscription state directly and grants `pro_active` without waiting for the webhook — a paying user is never blocked at an exit gate by webhook lag.
- **FR-L3** **Free-tier gating — open canvas, gated exits:** Free users can place, shuffle, and edit any Pro section on canvas (marked with a subtle ✦ badge). Enforcement happens only at the exits: deploy, ZIP export, and any surface exposing compiled theme code all block for a project containing Pro sections, with an itemized "Pro sections in this design" sheet offering, per item, a remediation that **actually exists for that item** — which the original three-option list did not, for 22 of the things it gates.

  **Placeable sections** get the familiar three: **upgrade · swap to a Free design via Shuffle · remove it**. The third is not decorative, because some binding contexts have no Free design to swap to (FR-G2).

  **The 28 non-placeable treatments get a fourth option, because none of the first three applies to them.** They are *selected*, not placed: Shuffle and Site Remix never touch them (FR-D13, FR-D17), and there is no canvas instance to remove. **22 of the 28 are Pro** — 10 of A32's 12 paywall designs, 4 of A33's 6 card treatments, 8 of A34's 10 pagination styles — and for every one of them the remediation is **revert to the Free design of that treatment**, which always exists because FR-G2's first-two rule guarantees it: A32 #1–#2, A33 #1–#2 and A34 #1–#2 are all `[Free]`. Each reverts on its own selection surface — the Paywall editor (FR-H6), the card design module (FR-Q7), the main feed's Pagination control (FR-H2) — or from Theme Settings where the host section is absent (FR-Q9).

  **The count is derived, not asserted:** (12 − 2) + (6 − 2) + (10 − 2) = **22**, and it moves if any category's Free floor moves. **`routes.yaml` is exempt** from the code-surface clause: it contains no section code, FR-I4's download is the only way to complete a routed deploy, and Appendix F grants the Routes Manager to Free unconditionally. **Downgrade rules (explicit):** existing deployed themes are never touched; on the Free plan in an over-limit state, all but one project become read-only — the user chooses which stays editable (default: most recently updated); read-only projects stay viewable, **stay exportable (FR-J12)** and become editable again on Pro. The same block-until-resolved pattern governs every over-limit resource — Inflozo never auto-deletes: while connections exceed the Free cap, deploys are blocked until the user disconnects down to it (each site's pre-Inflozo snapshot survives, FR-J13); an over-quota asset library goes read-only (existing files stay, uploads blocked) until the user deletes below the Free cap; retained artifacts above Free's 3 are kept but no new ones are retained. An itemized sheet lists exactly what is over and by how much. There is no dormant or frozen connection state — a connection either counts against the cap or is disconnected. Safety operations — rollback (FR-J9), snapshot restore (FR-J13) and the FR-C5 compatibility redeploy — remain available throughout **for connected sites**; they execute via the site's Admin API, so a disconnected site has neither until reconnected (a reconnection performed to run a restore counts against the connection cap like any other). Hidden sections are excluded from compilation and therefore never trigger exit gates.
- **FR-L4** Billing page: current plan, renewal date, invoices (Dodo customer portal link), upgrade/cancel/resume, plan-limit meters.
- **FR-L5** Contextual upgrade prompts at exactly four moments: 2nd project, 2nd site, quota exceeded, Pro-section deploy/export block. Never a blocking interstitial elsewhere — and never while playing with Pro sections on canvas.

### FR-M · Suggestions Board

- **FR-M1** Custom-built on Supabase (no third-party). Categories: Section idea / Feature / Integration. Statuses: Open / Planned / Building / Shipped.
- **FR-M2** Signed-in users submit (title, description, optional image) and upvote (one per user per suggestion). No comments in v1. Abuse controls: per-user rate limits on submissions and votes, title/body length caps, harder throttling for new accounts; images go through the FR-K2 upload pipeline (type/size limits, sanitization).
- **FR-M3** Public read-only board on the marketing site; the identical board lives in-app. Sort by Top / New / Status. Suggestion text appears immediately; an attached image renders on the public board only after admin approval (until then it is visible in-app to its author and to the admin).
- **FR-M4** Admin claim: edit status, merge duplicates, hide spam, approve suggestion images for the public board (FR-M3). **The first admin is seeded from the owner's own account at setup**, not granted by a flow — a board that ships with no admin has no one who can grant the first one, and "an admin exists" is not a feature to be built later. The claim lives on the user row (§7.5) and **survives the Test → Live cutover** (§4), which migrates suggestions and votes: an admin claim that did not migrate would leave the live board unmoderated on day one. Further admins are granted by an existing admin.

### FR-N · Marketing Website

- **FR-N1** Pages: **Home, Features, How It Works, Sections Gallery, Pricing, Suggestions, Docs, Contact, Changelog**, plus auth entry points and legal (Terms, Privacy, and a **Refund & Cancellation policy** stating FR-L2's no-refund and cancel-stops-auto-renew terms — required by Dodo, and the public statement the checkout consent in FR-L1 refers to). Statically generated on the same Next.js app (the suggestions board fetches live data client-side — FR-M3). The Contact page includes a simple form relaying to the owner via Resend (FR-P3).
- **FR-N2** **Sections Gallery** (the flagship, in the spirit of Flowbite Blocks / Tailwind Plus): SSG page per category; each design shown as a live render (same section runtime, Orbit Weekly data, **in a same-origin iframe — the same hosting model as the editor canvas (§7.3), because it is the same runtime and there is no second security posture to maintain: the gallery renders no untrusted HTML either (NFR-3), so a `sandbox` attribute would buy nothing and fork the component**) with a per-preview light/dark toggle, Free/Pro badge, design name, and deep link; "Open in Inflozo" CTA. Category pages are the SEO engine ("Ghost hero sections", "Ghost pricing sections", …) with per-category OG images and sitemap coverage.
- **FR-N3** Home: product narrative with an animated editor mock (design-shuffle moment as the hero demo), feature grid, how-it-works, gallery teaser, Style Pack strip, pricing teaser, FAQ, final CTA.
- **FR-N4** Docs (MDX): Getting started · **Assigning a designed template to a Ghost page** (FR-I6 — what the filename is, what the dropdown label will say, and why Inflozo cannot confirm it for you); Connecting your Ghost site (custom integration walkthrough with screenshots; **the Staff Access Token as a separate, later, optional step** — what it unlocks, that it is Owner-level, and exactly what a user gives up by declining it — FR-C1, FR-C3) · Deploying & rollback · **Hosting requirements** (self-hosted and Ghost(Pro) tiers; Starter limitation) — **and this page does not ship claiming verified Ghost(Pro) behaviour until §4's gate clears**, because it currently describes a Starter limitation inferred from an unobserved payload · routes.yaml upload step · Theme Settings & custom settings · Style Packs · Asset Library · Plans & billing · Troubleshooting · FAQ. Every docs page carries a "Suggest an edit" affordance relaying to the owner via Resend (FR-P3).
- **FR-N5** The blog/changelog runs on **Ghost with an Inflozo-built theme** (dogfooding; linked from the footer as proof).

### FR-O · Starter Templates

- **FR-O1** **10 starters** ship (Appendix E), each a complete pre-wired project: all standard templates designed, Style Pack chosen, sample content mapped. **Starters ship no membership template at all** — not even an undesigned one: there is no members template family for one to sit in, and a membership page is an opt-in `custom-{name}.hbs` (FR-I1). So Ghost's native Portal serves those flows, no `routes.yaml` is emitted (FR-I5), a starter's first deploy never requires the manual routes step (FR-I4), and the user is never sent into Ghost's page editor to pick a template for a page they did not ask for — the confetti moment stays uninterrupted. Users add designed membership pages later.
- **FR-O2** Starter chooser shows full-page scrollable previews with light/dark toggle before creation.
- **FR-O3** Starters are seeds — after creation they are ordinary projects with no linkage back.
- **FR-O4** At least two starters — **Quiet** and **Ledger** — are composed exclusively of [Free] designs end-to-end, so a Free account can ship a starter with zero swaps. The other eight are Pro-flavored by design.

### FR-P · Transactional Email

- **FR-P1** Exactly five user-facing transactional emails exist in v1 (plus the two owner-relay flows in FR-P3), sent via **Resend**: (1) magic-link sign-in and (2) email-change verification (Supabase Auth templates, branded, routed through Resend SMTP), (3) site "Reconnect needed" health alert (FR-C5), (4) payment failed / grace-period notice (FR-L2), (5) deploy failure notice. **A conditional sixth — the annual renewal reminder:** advance notice before an annual auto-renewal charge is statutory in several target markets (California's ARL, EU/UK consumer rules) and Dodo is merchant of record, so §7.6 verifies whether Dodo already sends it. If Dodo does, Inflozo sends nothing; if it does not, the reminder becomes email #6, carved out of FR-P2. **The "Reconnect needed" channel also carries the one compatibility notice that names affected shipped designs** (FR-C5) — the users at risk are precisely those who deployed once and stopped signing in, so an in-app-only notice would never reach them.
- **FR-P2** No marketing, digest, or nudge emails in v1. "Theme updates available" (FR-J14) is surfaced in-app only — never by email. **Two carve-outs, both non-discretionary and neither a nudge:** the Ghost-compatibility notice that names affected shipped designs (FR-C5), because it is the only signal Inflozo has that a customer's live site may be breaking; and the annual renewal reminder, if and only if Dodo does not send it as merchant of record (FR-P1). The routine per-release confirmation that the library was verified stays in-app.
- **FR-P3** **Owner-relay emails:** the marketing contact form (FR-N1) and the docs "Suggest an edit" affordance (FR-N4) relay submissions to the owner's address via Resend — platform as sender, submitter as reply-to, honeypot + rate limiting against spam.

## 6. Non-Functional Requirements

- **NFR-1 Performance (app):** editor time-to-interactive < 3 s (p75 of 10 timed runs, **warm**); all canvas interactions hold **60 fps** — the criterion is p95 frame time ≤ 16.7 ms with **no long task > 50 ms** across a 3-second trace of drag, reorder, Variant Shuffle and control changes; control-change render < 100 ms; persistence is local-first and asynchronous — saving must never add perceptible input latency (FR-D10's no-local-storage fallback is exempt, honestly labeled); Section Picker preview lazy rendering; dashboard LCP < 2 s. **Warm** means the app shell and section-library bundle are already in the browser HTTP cache and the Supabase session is live; a cold first visit is measured but not gated. **Reference environment:** a mid-tier laptop with 4× CPU throttle (Chrome DevTools); **stress fixture:** a 40-section template. All NFR-1 and G6 targets are pass/fail on this environment and fixture, and the gate is **manual-only** — run against the reference laptop before each release, never on CI. A shared CI runner is not that machine and 4× throttle on it is not reproducible, so a CI number would be noise presented as a gate. Beyond the fixture, graceful degradation (FR-D14).
- **NFR-2 Performance (output):** **Lighthouse is not measured.** A generated theme runs on *the user's* Ghost host, where TTFB alone can move the score 20 points on identical markup, so a host-determined number has no truth value and naming a host would gate Inflozo on infrastructure it does not own. Instead the compiler asserts, in CI and without a Ghost host, the properties Inflozo actually controls — on every compiled theme: (1) every image carries a **correct `srcset` and `sizes`** matching the rendition set it was generated with (FR-J5); (2) every shipped font is **`woff2`-only and preloaded**, and **subset for latin-script projects** — a non-latin project ships the **full face** instead, because subsetting a script whose glyph coverage the user's own copy determines would drop characters their posts actually use (Appendix D.b, which governs the reconciliation); (3) **no render-blocking JS** — every theme script is `defer`red or `type="module"`; (4) a **CSS budget of ≤ 50 KB gzipped, measured over the subset of `screen.css` a given template actually reaches** — tokens + shared primitives + that template's placed designs' styles. The subject needs stating because `screen.css` is one file for the whole theme, so "per rendered template" has no artifact on disk: the budget is computed from the compiler's own record of which design stylesheets each template pulls in, which it already holds in order to strip dead CSS (FR-G7). The whole-file size is reported alongside it and is not the gate; (5) **no layout shift from unguarded elements** — every media binding compiles inside its FR-H8 guard, asserted by proving no unguarded `src`/`srcset` survives compilation **and that every emitted guard resolves to a real bound field**.
