Presence alone is not the assertion: a guard on an identifier that does not exist passes a presence check and renders nothing (FR-H8). **JS budget: < 40 KB gzipped for a *maximal design*** — maximal means **every FR-G7 module on one page** **plus `cards.js`** where designed cards require it; a design using fewer modules ships proportionally less. **40 KB is the only JS number in this document** — the 50 KB and 100 KB thresholds considered mid-session are retired, so nothing here carries two budgets. **Measured, not assumed:** a realistic 15-section marketing home came to **≈ 10.3 KB** and the maximal page to **≈ 17.5 KB**, both against a 40 KB ceiling (`research-section-js-libraries.md` §4, §A2 for the method); `cards.js` is added on top and re-measured once the vendored set is built. **The re-measurement ruling R-24 would have forced is cancelled, because the registry got smaller rather than larger** — `search-overlay` was the largest single module at 1.8 KB and it is **deleted**, along with `search-expand` and `command-palette`. Search is Ghost's own `sodo-search`, which the theme neither ships nor pays for. Both measured figures are therefore ceilings, and the module count they were taken over is read from the registry rather than restated here (standing rule 3).

  **The JS gate is a developer-facing warning, not a build failure, and that is deliberate.** It is enforced by **`size-limit`** over the compiled `assets/js/main.js` in compile CI, and it never appears in the editor — the user has no lever to pull in response to it, so showing them a number would only be alarming. It does not block a build because nothing realistic comes close: the measured maximal page uses 44% of the ceiling, and a threshold that cannot plausibly be hit is worth more as a drift signal than as a gate. If a future module set does approach it, that is a design conversation, not a red build. **Read the number in proportion:** Ghost's own bundled `sodo-search` measures **86.5 KB gzipped** — five times Inflozo's entire maximal section runtime, injected by Ghost through `{{ghost_head}}`, and not Inflozo's to remove. Inflozo's sections are not the weight problem on a Ghost site.

  Zero console errors on every template of every compiled fixture theme.
- **NFR-3 Security:** RLS on every table keyed to `auth.uid()`; Admin API keys and the Owner Staff Access Token in Supabase Vault, server-only, decrypted per request — **and the token's full-Administrator scope is disclosed where it is requested** (FR-C3), never implied to be narrower; short-lived Admin JWTs minted per call; deploy + upload rate limits; Dodo webhook signature verification; SVG sanitization on upload (FR-K2). **No Admin API key ever reaches a Ghost theme (normative, and it was proposed).** An Admin API key is **never** stored in a Ghost theme custom setting, **never** printed into a page, and **never** requested beyond FR-C1's connect flow — where it lives server-side in Vault and is used only to mint short-lived JWTs. The proposal to spend one of the 20 custom-setting slots on an Admin key was researched rather than dismissed, and it failed on three independent grounds, each sufficient alone: the Admin Integration role carries `member:all`, `member_signin_url:read` and `theme:all`, so a leaked key is **site takeover**, not a scoped read; Handlebars has **no server-side fetch**, so a theme could only *use* such a key by printing it into the rendered page; and Ghost's Admin CORS allowlist includes the site's **own hostname**, so a key printed into the page is directly exploitable from that page. It was also **unnecessary** — every capability the proposal was meant to buy is served by the four core helpers in Appendix B at **zero setting cost** (`research-contested-variants.md`). **`{{content_api_key}}` is a different thing and is permitted:** the Content API key is browser-safe by Ghost's own design, read-only over published content, and Ghost ships the helper precisely so themes can use it. The distinction is the key's scope, not the act of printing.

**The canvas renders no untrusted HTML.** The FR-H3 fixtures are not an exception to this and never weaken it: they are Inflozo-authored, generated from Ghost's renderers, checked into the repo and reviewed like any other source — trusted by construction. It is precisely *because* the user's own body HTML is never read that a generated body has to exist at all. It reads list and metadata fields only — posts, pages, tags, authors, `@site` — and **never post or page body HTML**, which renders as dummy placeholder content instead. Every Content-API value therefore enters the DOM as a **text node, never through `innerHTML`**: there is no sanitizer library on the canvas path and none is needed, because there is no HTML to sanitize. Three carve-outs complete the rule: (1) `codeinjection_head` and `codeinjection_foot` are **never read into the canvas, rendered, stored, or logged** — FR-C2's connect-time check tests only whether they are non-empty and discards the result, which is what lets §1.2 surface its notice without the injected code ever entering Inflozo; (2) every URL-valued field is **scheme-validated (`http`/`https` only)** before it reaches an `href`, `src` or `srcset`; (3) **excerpts render text-only and are sanitized**, being the one field Ghost may return carrying markup. The canvas iframe is same-origin (§7.3). **CSP** (app and marketing): `default-src 'self'`; `script-src 'self'` — **no `'unsafe-eval'`**, which nothing needs because Handlebars is never parsed or executed in the browser (FR-H5); `style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`; `img-src 'self' https: data: blob:`; `connect-src 'self'` plus the Supabase project origin and the connected Ghost site's origin (client-side Content API reads — P5). **`connect-src` is therefore composed per session, not shipped as a static header string**: the Ghost origin varies by project and a user may hold several connections, so the header is built from the active session's connection set in Next.js middleware (§7.1) and a static list would either be too permissive or break the editor; `frame-ancestors 'self'` — **`'none'` would forbid the same-origin editing iframe §7.3 mandates**, which is the one real objection to that boundary and is resolved here rather than left implicit; `base-uri 'none'`; `object-src 'none'`; `form-action 'self'`.
- **NFR-4 Reliability:** autosave offline queue with retry/backoff; deploys idempotent with immutable artifacts; background site health checks; Supabase spend-cap alarms and usage dashboards; Supabase Postgres point-in-time recovery (PITR) enabled for user work-product, with a restore drill exercised before launch.
- **NFR-5 Accessibility:** tool **axe-core**, ruleset **WCAG 2.1 AA**, threshold **zero violations** — pass/fail, no score. Scope: **every shipped design and the Inflozo app itself.** Design scanning runs on the NFR-6(a) render matrix, against the same renders, at near-zero marginal cost — no second matrix exists. Generated themes carry an alt attribute on **every** image (user-supplied, falling back to the bound Ghost field) and a correct heading order per template. **An image that is the sole content of a link must carry a *non-empty* `alt`:** `alt=""` there leaves the link with no accessible name, an axe-core `link-name` violation and therefore a WCAG 2.1 AA failure this threshold sets to zero — feature images fall back through `feature_image_alt`, then the post title. (An `alt="{{title}}"` beneath the same page's `<h1>` is *not* a defect — it is what Casper ships — so the rule is about links, not duplication.) **Scope includes the fixture renders and the six synthesized templates** (FR-H3, FR-D6): a card treatment or a default stack that ships an accessibility failure is no better for having been generated. The app is keyboard-complete: reorder via keyboard, focus management in overlays and **across the canvas iframe boundary** (§7.3), reduced-motion support. User Style Pack token edits can still break contrast — FR-E3's color picker runs a **live contrast check** and warns before the edit lands, and responsibility transfers to the user at that point (a warning, never a block). **The scan stops at the edge of `{{content}}` (ruling R-6).** Its accessible-name assertions **do not inspect the inside of a post body** — Ghost emits its own markup there, and a scan that walked into it would report violations on a customer's content that no theme can fix. A theme owns `cards.css` inside the body and nothing else, so it is assessed on what it owns; FR-J17's heading-order gate is scoped the same way. **The combobox + `aria-activedescendant` pattern is gone from the library entirely** — it was the registry's single largest AA failure source and it lived in A23, which R-24 deletes.
- **NFR-6 Quality automation:**
  - **(a) Render matrix** — every section design × 3 reference Style Packs × light/dark × 3 viewports → Playwright screenshot baselines with diff gates (designs × 3 packs × 2 modes × 3 viewports; the number is derived from Appendix A and moves with the inventory, never restated independently of it). **Threshold:** a design fails above **1% differing pixels** at a per-pixel tolerance of 0.1. **Pinned renderer:** one fixed Playwright/Chromium version in one fixed container image with fonts installed in the image, animations and caret disabled — the runner is part of the baseline. **Baseline regeneration:** a change to a Style Pack, a shared primitive or the pinned renderer invalidates the whole set at once, so a mass rebaseline requires the owner's approval on a sampled visual review (one design per category, both modes), lands as its own commit touching baselines only, and names the change that caused it. The NFR-5 axe-core scan runs on these same renders. **The matrix pins its fixtures** (FR-H3): A32 renders on a gated `post.hbs`, A33 on the style-guide post, A34 on the 32-post feed at all three of its pages, A25 on the style-guide post *and* the style-guide page, and the six synthesized stacks render as themselves (FR-D6), and **an empty tag** is pinned as a fixture so the main feed's empty state (FR-H4) is a render like any other rather than a state nothing exercises. A fixture change is a baseline change and follows the regeneration rule above.
  - **(b) Compile CI** — nightly assembly of synthetic themes covering 100% of designs → gscan 0/0.
  - **(c) Fidelity, in three layers.** There is no DOM-normalized golden diff: it could only pass by normalizing away the image URLs FR-J5 depends on, which would blind the one test that underwrites the WYSIWYG promise. **(c1) Compiled-output snapshots** — the `.hbs` each design compiles to is committed and diffed **per-commit**, so any change to emitted theme code is visible in review. **The fixtures are committed and diffed the same way**: because the style-guide post is generated from Ghost's own renderers, a Ghost target bump surfaces as a visible fixture diff rather than as silent canvas drift (FR-H3, §7.6). **(c2) Shim contract tests** — every shimmed Ghost helper (FR-H5, Appendix B) is asserted against **recorded real-Ghost output** for a recorded input set, per-commit and offline; image URLs are compared, not normalized away, because the canvas shim emits Ghost-shaped sized URLs and `srcset` rather than passing paths through. **(c3) Nightly perceptual diff** — the canvas rendered against the same page deployed to a real Ghost target (§4), at (a)'s threshold and on (a)'s pinned renderer, over the risk-weighted rotating batch §4 defines. **Its exclusion regions are §1.2's carve-outs, named here because this is the test that enforces them:** the post and page body (the canvas shows the fixture, the live page shows the author's own HTML), Portal's floating button **and Ghost's announcement strip** (both shimmed on canvas — FR-H5 — so both are Inflozo-drawn approximations of Ghost's own markup and would fail a pixel diff against the real thing), Ghost's native comments and search overlay, and any region the site's own code injection touches. Animations are frozen for the comparison, and an intentional change is approved in one click rather than re-baselined by hand. **The card treatments are one of its named weights**, and they are compared through the style-guide fixture — the one surface on which Ghost's `cards.min.css` and Inflozo's `cards.css` can actually be caught disagreeing (FR-Q7).
  - **(d) E2E** — Playwright against the running stack (pre-launch: production, per §4's mandate; post-launch: the Test environment, §4) covering: auth, connect, build, shuffle, dark authoring, deploy to all Ghost deploy targets (§4), rollback, billing (Dodo test mode), quotas — **and one keyboard-only journey: sign in, connect, place a section, cycle the ring with `]` and `[`, deploy — run with no pointer events, verifying the keyboard rows of `EXPERIENCE.md` § Accessibility Floor** *(amended 2026-09-03, F-097; the amendment `review-accessibility.md` §4 flagged and did not make)*.

  Only **(c3)** and **(d)** touch a real Ghost.
- **NFR-7 Compatibility:** the visitor-browser floor is **FR-G8's pinned Baseline** — Chrome/Edge 121, Firefox 122, Safari and iOS Safari 17.2 at the `widelyAvailableOnDate: 2026-08-18` pin — and it moves only when that date is bumped deliberately, never by drift. Generated themes support Ghost 5.x **and** 6.x — compatibility comes from the **helper surface the library uses** (Appendix B), verified by gscan against the v6 spec and by the NFR-6(c) fidelity layers — **`engines.ghost: ">=5.0.0"` declares the floor Ghost enforces at upload, and declaring it is not what makes 5.x work**; the helper surface is. The withheld key is the *other* one, `engines.ghost-api`, which gscan carries as a standing warning on both specs (FR-J1): no `engines.ghost-api` key is emitted (FR-J1), because gscan flags it as a standing warning on both specs. **Ghost 5.x reached end of life in January 2026:** 5.x *connections* stay supported for lagging sites, but 5.x parity is a compatibility courtesy rather than a permanent commitment — **but T3 is permanent, not the first thing to drop.** Now that the Ghost fleet is self-hosted, a second droplet is the entire cost of testing a claim this document makes publicly, and several gscan rules genuinely differ between the v5 and v6 specs (AMP, for one, was removed in Ghost **6.0**, so it still exists on 5.x). Dropping T3 would turn NFR-7's 5.x support from a tested claim into an asserted one, which is the trade §4's governing constraint refuses. Ghost 4.x and older are rejected at connect with a friendly "please update Ghost" message.
- **NFR-8 Privacy/compliance:** **GDPR export** is discharged by **Supabase's own per-user data export tooling**, run by the owner on request and delivered as a JSON + assets archive within the statutory 30 days — no in-app export surface ships in v1, and no FR specifies one. **Delete** is FR-A5. **Analytics: marketing site only** — privacy-respecting analytics may run on inflozo.com; the authenticated app ships **no analytics and no telemetry** in v1 (this is what makes G1/G2/G8–G10 manual or moderated measurements), and no third-party trackers run anywhere. Dodo handles tax as merchant of record.
- **NFR-9 Observability:** Sentry (app + server), structured deploy logs (per-stage timing, gscan output), Supabase usage alerts at 60/80/95% of included quotas.

---

## 7. Technical Assumptions & Architecture Direction (for the BMAD Architect)

### 7.1 Stack

- **Frontend/app:** Next.js (App Router, TypeScript) on **Vercel** — one codebase serving marketing (SSG) at **inflozo.com** and the authenticated product at **app.inflozo.com** (two domains, one deployment).
- **Backend:** **Supabase** — Postgres (+ RLS), Auth (magic link + experimental passkeys, `supabase-js` ≥ 2.105.0 pinned), Storage (buckets: `assets/{userId}/…`, `deploy-artifacts/{projectId}/…`, `site-snapshots/{userId}/{siteId}/…`) — **the `thumbnails` bucket is not provisioned in v1**, since nothing writes to it (FR-B1), Vault for the Admin API keys **and the Owner Staff Access Token** (FR-C3).
- **Server compute:** Vercel Node.js functions (not Edge) for: compile + gscan + zip, Admin API proxy (JWT mint), Dodo webhooks, health checks and notification retention (Vercel Cron). **Thumbnails are not rendered at all in v1** — FR-B1 ships static Style-Pack-derived placeholders and defers capture (Appendix G), so no headless browser runs on Vercel and Appendix F carries no Chromium line item.
- **Compile job posture:** the compile function assembles a file tree, subsets fonts, pulls referenced assets from Storage, runs gscan and zips. Budget it as a **long-running Node function**, sized against a measured worst case — the 40-section stress fixture across every emitted template with a full asset set — not against the platform default. Set duration and memory from that measurement and state both in the architecture; if the worst case exceeds the plan's function ceiling, split the job at the **gscan boundary**, not inside deploy, so the upload stage never grows a second timeout mode. Progress and cancellation are backed by the `deploy_jobs` row (§7.5), never by function-local state — FR-J8's five-stage UI must have something to poll when the compile has already failed.
- **Payments:** Dodo Payments hosted checkout + webhooks (fee model noted in Appendix F).
- **Email:** Resend for all transactional email (FR-P); Supabase Auth SMTP routed through Resend for consistent branding.
- **Section CSS authoring format:** **plain, flat CSS** — no preprocessor, no nesting, no build step — one stylesheet per design, authored against Style Pack custom properties (`var(--…)`) and the control-driven data-attribute selectors of §7.3, with class names derived from the section name. **Section CSS is excluded from the app's Tailwind pipeline**: it is never scanned, never purged, never touched by the app build. The two are incompatible, not merely separate — a utility class encodes its value at authoring time, so it cannot follow a Style Pack switch (FR-E2) or a per-mode token (FR-E4), and a utility-class soup is precisely what FR-J1's hand-editable, Casper/Source-grade output requirement forbids. Tailwind styles the Inflozo app; the section stylesheets are library data that the compiler concatenates and prunes into `assets/css/screen.css`. They never meet.
- **Baseline enforcement, and why it needs no build step:** `stylelint-plugin-use-baseline` runs **directly on the flat stylesheets** — no bundler, no preprocessor, nothing to configure away — which is the reason FR-G8's policy fits this stack instead of fighting it; `eslint-plugin-compat` covers the modules and `size-limit` the compiled bundle. All three are dev dependencies of the Inflozo repo and never enter a generated theme. **The three sanctioned vendor prefixes are FR-G8's closed list**; any other is a CI failure, which is what makes "no autoprefixer" a safe choice rather than a fashionable one.
- **Bundled-code licence filter (hard rule):** anything that could end up inside a generated theme must be **MIT, BSD-2/3-Clause, Apache-2.0 or ISC** — users own, redistribute and may resell what Inflozo generates. GPL/AGPL/LGPL/SSPL/BUSL, non-commercial terms and Commons Clause riders are disqualifying, with no case-by-case exception, and missing or ambiguous metadata counts as a rejection. The rule is enforced rather than assumed because it has already nearly bitten: `typed.js` relicensed MIT → GPL-3.0 at v3.0.0 (FR-G7). Today the check is trivial — FR-G7's answer is zero runtime dependencies, so CI simply asserts `assets/js/` holds only repo-authored files.
- **Rendering:** **there is no Handlebars runtime in the browser.** Sections are annotated HTML with two renderers over one source — canvas DOM and `.hbs` text (§7.3, FR-H5) — so the app ships no template compiler, nothing calls `new Function`, and NFR-3's CSP needs no `'unsafe-eval'`. The Ghost-helpers shim (FR-H5) is a first-class package; its fidelity is held by the NFR-6(c2) shim contract tests, not by a golden DOM diff.

### 7.2 Content & data flow

- Editor → user's Ghost **Content API** directly from the browser (browser-safe key; API is read-only, cacheable; resources: posts, pages, tags, authors, tiers, settings; NQL filters, `include` params, `Accept-Version` pinned).
- Editor → Inflozo server → user's Ghost **Admin API** only for: connection validation, theme upload/activate, **writing and reading `routes.yaml`** (FR-I4 — automated upload is the primary path, with the guided Labs card as the designed fallback). Never from the client.
- Deployed sites: zero runtime dependence on Inflozo (P5).

### 7.3 Single-source section runtime (mandated)

**One source, two renderers.** A section design is authored as **annotated HTML** — plain, valid HTML carrying `data-*` directives (`data-prop`, `data-prop-attr`, `data-bind`, `data-bind-attr`, `data-empty`, `data-repeat`, `data-repeat-limit`, `data-partial`) — plus **its own** plain CSS file (§7.1), **its own** optional behaviour module from FR-G7's registry, and its content/control schemas (FR-G3) — all four owned **per design**, so removing a design removes its markup, its stylesheet and its module together (FR-G7, FR-J3). That single source feeds **two renderers**: one produces canvas DOM, the other produces `.hbs` text. Canvas and shipped markup therefore agree **by construction**, not by comparison — byte-comparable markup is a property of the design, not something a test has to earn expression by expression. **Handlebars is never parsed, evaluated or printed anywhere in the product** — not in the browser, not on the server. There is no partial evaluator and no AST printer, and the multi-week component those would have required does not exist. `spike-compiler/` validates the **core** of this path empirically: 16/16 renderer assertions, and a generated theme scoring **0 errors / 0 warnings** on gscan 6.4.2 against **both** the v5 and v6 specs.

**Compiler mechanics (binding on the implementation).** The theme renderer walks the DOM, substitutes content and control values, injects Handlebars constructs, and serializes with `outerHTML` — HTML's own parser and serializer stand in for the Handlebars printer Ghost does not ship. Four mechanics are binding — three proven in the spike, and the fourth added 2026-08-19 after it was proven by execution against Handlebars:

1. **Handlebars expressions are carried through serialization as opaque ASCII tokens** placed in text nodes and attribute values, then swapped for the real expression *after* serialization. Without this the serializer escapes the quotes inside an attribute-borne expression, and `src="{{img_url feature_image size="m"}}"` — the single riskiest case in the design — comes out corrupt.
2. **Block helpers inject as HTML comments carrying tokens.** `{{#foreach}}` wrappers and FR-H8's `{{#if}}` guards are placed as comment siblings around the element they enclose, then unwrapped in the final pass. Guards wrap the **element**, never the attribute — and guard on the **bound field**, never on a helper argument (FR-H8).
3. **A mustache may never abut a closing brace, and the compiled theme carries no `{{{` or `}}}` at all.** *(Added 2026-08-19, verified by execution.)* Handlebars reads `}}}` as a triple-stash close, so `:root{--a:{{@custom.x}}}` is a **parse error** — and that is exactly the shape FR-Q5's inline token block in `default.hbs` emits for a promoted colour. gscan reports it only as "Templates must contain valid Handlebars", which points at nothing. The serializer therefore terminates every CSS declaration (`;}`, a space or a newline all parse), and compile CI asserts **exactly one permitted `{{{body}}}`, in `default.hbs`, and zero `{{{` or `}}}` in every other emitted file**.

  **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-08-20 (stress test Round 3, decision D3).** This read *"compile CI asserts the emitted theme contains zero `{{{` and zero `}}}` sequences. Inflozo emits no triple-stash by design … so the assertion is exact."* The premise is false. Inflozo emits **one**, unavoidably: `{{{body}}}` is how a Ghost layout injects the rendered template, and there is no alternative — read from the shipped Ghost 5.130.6 image, `casper/default.hbs:77` and `source/default.hbs:56` both use it, and `express-hbs/lib/hbs.js:527` assigns `locals.body` as a plain string, so `{{body}}` would HTML-escape the entire page. `spike-compiler/build.js` has emitted it since the spike was written. As stated the assertion fails on **every** build, which means it gets deleted by whoever hits it first — taking with it the guard against `:root{--a:{{@custom.x}}}`, the actual defect it exists for. Scoping it to the one permitted occurrence keeps the guard and lets it pass.

4. **Comment-wrapped markers MUST be unwrapped BEFORE token substitution.** Substituting first makes a marker Inflozo inserted indistinguishable from a comment the section author wrote, and the compiler then rewrites author comments as template code. This is an ordering constraint on the design, not an implementation detail; it belongs in the compiler's own test suite and must not be left for the implementer to rediscover.

**The directive vocabulary is incomplete, and the complete gap list is stated here rather than discovered per category** *(Round 3, decision D5 — closes Round 1 decision 8, deferred through two rounds)*. The eight directives above (`data-prop`, `data-prop-attr`, `data-bind`, `data-bind-attr`, `data-empty`, `data-repeat`, `data-repeat-limit`, `data-partial`) were derived from a two-section spike. Walking every category of `sections-inventory.md` against them yields **13** constructs the library needs and the vocabulary cannot express. This list is normative for E4's authoring-format deliverable and **gates it**: the vocabulary is settled once, before the first category is authored, because these directives are read by *both* renderers and a late addition re-authors every design that predates it.

| # | Missing construct | Where it is needed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Repeat over a content-prop array** — `items[]`, `logos[]`, `steps[]`, `images[]`, `team[]`, `values[]`, `socials[]`, `locations[]`. `data-repeat` names a **Ghost** source and emits `{{#foreach}}`; a content array is *user* data and must be baked at compile as N static blocks, with per-item `data-prop` and per-item `data-empty`. **This is the largest single gap in the library and is named nowhere else.** | A5, A8, A9, A10, A11, A12, A13, A14, A15, A16, A22 — **11 categories, ~166 designs** |
| 2 | **`{{#get}}` with `filter` / `limit` / `order`** — the whole FR-H2 Data group (Source · Count · Order), plus A7's mandatory `filter="visibility:public"`, FR-H2's ban on `limit="all"`, and **hand-picked order as N single-id gets, uncapped with a warning past 25** (R-20). A `{{#get}}` is **refused on `error.hbs` and `private.hbs`** (R-7) | A7, A15's playlist designs, A17, A18, A19, A20, A21, A22 #7, A27 |
| 3 | **Two-armed conditional (`{{else}}`)** — `data-empty` emits a one-armed `{{#if}}` and there is no way to express the other arm | A1's `@member` swap (Sign in ↔ Account), A22's signed-in swap, **A19's quote design when the post carries no `custom_excerpt`**, **A30's signed-out branch on the account template** — Ghost does not redirect and there is no protected URL, so the `{{else}}` arm of `{{#if @member}}` has to be designed (R-4), A27's "by primary tag **with fallback to latest**", and **A29's zero-post archive** — an empty tag, a new author, a month with nothing in it, each of which is reachable and each of which needs the other arm |
| 4 | **Member-state conditional over four closed values** — Everyone / Logged out / Free members / Paid members. The paid-vs-free test is not a plain path | A2, A6, A22, A26, plus A30 (13) and A32 (12) |
| 5 | **Positional helpers** — `@first`, `@last`, `@index`, `@number`, `@even` / `@odd` | A17 and A18's spanning and ranked designs, A13's numbering, A19's alternating designs. **Positional is all there is** (ruling R-1): "the first *featured* item spans" needs cross-iteration state Handlebars has not got, so **`@first` spans and "featured" reaches a grid through `Source: Featured only`** instead. **A18's numbering restarts at 01 on every page** and the specs say so — numbering that continues across pages is not expressible. |
| 6 | **Pagination** — `{{pagination}}`, `{{#if pagination.next}}`, `{{page_url}}` | A34's 10 designs, FR-D21's page-2 state |
| 7 | **Nested repeats**, with tokens resolved in **reverse insertion order** (AD-5b(a)) | A17 and A18's per-card tag chips, A20's per-tag post rows, A21's per-author post rows, A9's grouped designs |
| 8 | ~~**Group-by / change detection**~~ — **struck from this list (ruling R-1).** A header on a key change is **not a compiler construct**; it is a **behaviour module, `group-headings`**, whose no-JS state is a flat ruled list (P0·8 rule 4). It reaches A18's grouped and timeline designs, A20 and A21's index designs. The row is kept struck rather than deleted, because "add a group-by directive" is a proposal that will otherwise be made again. |
| 9 | **Bare-helper binding** — a helper with no bound path. `data-bind` requires one | `{{content}}` (A25), `{{comments}}` (A28), `{{total_members}}` (A7's members design), `{{navigation}}`, `{{statusCode}}` / `{{message}}` (A31), `{{content_api_key}}` (**A29's filter design** — the one remaining client-side Content API consumer now that A23 is deleted) |
| 10 | **Compile-target-conditional wrapper** — the same design emitting different markup per target: `{{#if @page.show_title_and_feature_image}}` around title and feature image on `page.hbs` **only**, never on `post.hbs` | A24 — normative for all 16 designs |
| 11 | **Mixed literal-and-bound attribute value** — `data-portal="signup/{tier}"` where the tier comes from a bound row | A22, A30, A32 |
| 14 | **Adjacency and placement as a declared compile-time INPUT** *(register 48, ruling R-19, AD-37)* — a design states what it needs to know about the page it sits on (is there a section above me · does the section above carry an image · am I the last block before the footer · has this route already emitted a share block · does a duplicate of this post appear elsewhere on the page) and **the compiler answers it at build from the placement list**, baking the answer into the emitted markup. There is no runtime lookup and no `{{#if}}` for it, because Ghost's render cannot see the page the editor saw. `sections-inventory.md`'s "sections never know about each other" is amended, not deleted: not at *runtime*; the compiler knows about all of them | The overlay-header precondition, stacked-padding collapse, duplicate-post suppression, one-share-block-per-route, footer adjacency and margin occupancy — **≥ 12 categories**. This is the input row AD-37 says register 48 carries |
| 12 | **Bound value into an inline custom property** — A20's tag-accent tint needs the tag's own `accent_color` on the element. **This collides with AD-3's "no inline styles" and the collision must be ruled on, not worked around** | A20 |
| 13 | **Text-node interpolation mixing static and bound text** — `data-bind` replaces the whole `textContent` | A10 #7 ("stats embedded within a flowing sentence"), A20 #14 |

Items 5, 6 and 7 were already known (§7.3's original list and Round 1). Items 1, 2, 3, 4, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12 and 13 are new, and item 1 alone covers roughly a third of the library.

**Escaping.** User content is data, never code. Any Handlebars syntax a user types into a text prop is emitted **inert**. **The naive rule is not sufficient and must not be shipped:** escaping only `{{` leaves a user-typed backslash immediately before it producing `\\{{…}}`, which Handlebars reads as an escaped backslash followed by a **live expression** — so the value is evaluated rather than shown, and a block helper typed the same way produces a template that will not compile. This is a functional break of the guarantee, not a cosmetic one, and it is in E0's spike scope.

  **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-08-19 — the remedy this document previously prescribed does not work, and was verified false by execution.** This paragraph used to continue "The backslash is escaped too. The emitted form is `\{{`." Both halves are wrong. **Handlebars' backslash escape is not composable**: executed against `handlebars@4.7.9` — the exact version Ghost `main` declares — exactly **one** backslash escapes a mustache *and is consumed*, while every count of two or more evaluates live (`\\{{t}}` → `\LIVE`, `\\\{{t}}` → `\\LIVE`). There is therefore **no** emission that renders a literal backslash followed by a literal `{{`, so a user who types `C:\{{title}}` cannot be served correctly by any backslash rule at all. The bug this paragraph identifies is real; the fix it named was not.

  **The mechanism is HTML numeric entity encoding.** After HTML-escaping (`&` first), every `{` and `}` the user typed is emitted as `&#123;` / `&#125;`. Handlebars never sees a mustache, so its escaping rules are never engaged; the browser decodes the exact characters back. Every case round-trips with zero parse failures — `{{title}}`, `C:\{{title}}`, `{{{title}}}`, `{{#if x}}…{{/if}}`, `{{!-- c --}}` — and Ghost renders them as literal characters and never interprets them (FR-J1). The architecture carries this as AD-5, with the executed evidence in `../../architecture/architecture-Inflozo-2026-08-19/MEASUREMENTS.md` §3. Because the rich-text serializer is the only place user text becomes markup, this rule has exactly one enforcement site.

**Control → CSS.** Controls are **closed-valued** by construction: every type in Appendix C resolves to one of a declared, finite set of named values — no free text, no units, no hex outside the Style Pack (FR-F1, FR-F2). Closed values are what make **data-attribute selectors** viable, and that is the mandated mechanism: each control writes its value to a `data-{control}` attribute on the section root, and the section stylesheet selects on it (`.hero[data-media-side="right"] { … }`). **A design switch replaces the attribute set**, it does not merge with it: attributes belonging to controls the incoming design does not declare are removed from the section root, while their values persist parked in the project doc (FR-D19). The stylesheet therefore never sees a stale attribute, and nothing in the CSS depends on an attribute a design does not own. Consequences the Architect should hold: a control change is a **single attribute write** on either renderer, which is what makes FR-F4's one-frame optimistic paint affordable; the compiled theme carries the chosen values as static attributes with zero runtime cost; and a control promoted to a Ghost custom setting (FR-Q3) emits `data-media-side="{{@custom.media_side}}"` with no change at all to the stylesheet. No inline styles, no generated class names, no CSS-in-JS.

**Rich text.** The engine is **raw `contenteditable`**, mapped to schema props by the same `data-*` directives — deliberately *not* ProseMirror/Lexical/Tiptap, whose own DOM inside the iframe would break the single-source guarantee outright. Storage is **text plus marks in a JSON structure** (FR-D4): a plain string plus an ordered list of `{start, end, mark, href?}` ranges over exactly the four permitted marks, never an HTML string. Two things follow. The compiler never emits unescaped user HTML through `{{{ }}}`, because there is no user HTML to emit; and FR-Q3's plain-text lock is a **truncation of the mark list**, not an HTML parse. **The compiler is the single sanitization point:** it escapes the text, then emits the mark ranges as `<strong>` / `<em>` / `<u>` / `<a>`. Paste is normalized to the same four marks on entry, which is also why the sidebar's Content field and the canvas can edit the same value.

**Canvas geometry and the chrome partition (binding).** The canvas iframe is **viewport-sized and scrolls internally** — the page scrolls *inside* the frame, the frame does not grow to the page's height. This is the only geometry in which `position: sticky`, `position: fixed`, `100vh` and `IntersectionObserver` behave as they will on the real site, and more than twenty designs across fifteen categories depend on that — including the sticky and sticky-shrink options on the site-wide header, which sit on the default path. It is also what Elementor, Gutenberg and Webflow all do; a full-document-height frame makes sticky never release, pins `fixed` to a twenty-thousand-pixel box and renders `vh` meaningless (`research-canvas-iframe-geometry.md`).

**Chrome is split by kind, not placed wholesale.** Selection outlines, hover states, section badges and insertion indicators render **inside** the iframe as CSS driven by `data-inflozo-*` attributes — the same data-attribute mechanism §7.3 already mandates for controls — and are drawn as `::after` pseudo-elements on the element itself. Two things follow: they add no DOM nodes, so byte-comparable markup is untouched; and they inherit their element's own stacking and transform, so **a sticky header's outline sticks with it** and no rect arithmetic can desynchronise. Only the **floating mark toolbar and its pickers** sit outside the frame, anchored to a virtual reference and tracked while open.

**Ghost's two shimmed surfaces are inside the frame too, but they are not chrome** (FR-H5). Editing chrome decorates a design; these stand in for markup Ghost will inject around it. **Ghost's announcement strip is real content in the layout** — it occupies vertical space at the top of the document and pushes the design down, exactly as `{{ghost_head}}` will — while Portal's button is `position: fixed` and overlays. Both are inert: no `data-inflozo-*` attributes, no selection, no hit-testing, and both are excluded from `outerHTML` serialization, so the byte-comparable-markup guarantee is untouched by either. Per-frame cost is therefore proportional to open popovers — one or two — rather than to the amount of chrome on screen, which is what makes NFR-1's budget affordable during scroll. The alternative was measured and rejected: rect-tracked overlays are what force Shopify to tell theme authors to disable sticky and fixed elements while its inspector is active.

**Device preview resizes the frame in both axes** (FR-D8) to a real device viewport, so media queries fire and `vh` resolves honestly; a separate zoom transform fits an oversized frame on screen **without** changing the CSS pixel viewport, so it never affects which breakpoint applies. Webflow's width-only model is explicitly not the reference here — its users have been unable to check `vh` behaviour without publishing since 2017.

**Focus model across the boundary.** Inline editing happens inside the iframe, in that document's own `contenteditable`; the sidebar and the mark toolbar live outside. A single edit gesture therefore crosses the boundary, and two documents each hold their own focus and selection. NFR-5 requires the editor to be keyboard-complete across this seam. **The focus order is a UX-pass deliverable** and is named in the UX mandate (header note): the canvas must be reachable and escapable by keyboard, selection state must survive chrome taking focus, and the mark toolbar must be operable without destroying the iframe selection it acts on.

**Same-origin, on its real merits.** The canvas iframe is **same-origin** — and not because cross-origin is incapable. A cross-origin frame can measure its own rects and host its own `contenteditable`, and `postMessage` the results out; any claim otherwise is false and must not be used to defend this boundary. The decision stands on cost, not capability: synchronous rect reads and direct DOM writes with no message protocol, no serialization of every hover, drag and control change across a bridge, and one shared style and asset context for canvas and chrome. What makes it **safe** is NFR-3's text-node rule, not the frame boundary. The canvas renders **no untrusted HTML at all** — it reads list and metadata fields only, never post or page body HTML (which renders as dummy placeholder content), and every Content-API value enters the DOM as a text node, never through `innerHTML`. There is nothing to sanitize, therefore nothing the boundary would have contained. FR-N2's marketing gallery uses the same hosting model for the same reason.

**Any proposal to re-implement a section as a React preview component is rejected by this PRD.**

**The directive vocabulary is designed with a proven core — not fully validated.** The spike demonstrates eight directives across two files, and what it proves it proves well: attribute-borne expressions survive serialization, block helpers unwrap cleanly, repeaters extract to parameterless partials, guards wrap elements. That is a genuine working core, and it is roughly enough for the simplest designs in the library. **Five constructs the library depends on are not in it, and E4 does not exit until they are** — each with a worked example, not a description:

1. **`{{#get}}` blocks** — `data-repeat` compiles to `{{#foreach}}` only, while a substantial minority of designs are `{{#get}}`-driven by their own Data declarations.
2. **Member visibility** — `{{#if @member}}` and its inverse, required by the show-to control on every CTA-bearing category.
3. **Chrome strings** — nothing currently emits `{{t "key"}}` from annotated HTML, while FR-J5 forbids hard-coded visitor-facing literals and the catalog carries every one of them.
4. **`srcset` / `sizes`** — NFR-2 asserts correct `srcset` on every image in CI, and the current binding grammar produces exactly one expression per attribute.
5. **The control → `data-{control}` write on the section root** — mandated above as *the* control mechanism, and absent from both the directive list and the spike.

**Three of the five pilot sections need constructs this list is missing**, which is why the pilots are the gate rather than a smoke test.

**The one body of HTML the canvas does render is generated, not read.** NFR-3 keeps the canvas away from every scrap of user body HTML, which would otherwise leave `{{content}}` and `{{comments}}` as holes exactly where two categories and a whole card module have to be designed. The FR-H3 fixtures fill them without weakening the rule: they are Inflozo-authored, snapshotted from Ghost's own renderers, checked into the repo and reviewed like any other source, so they are trusted in the way a section's own annotated HTML is trusted. Nothing here loosens what the canvas may read from a connected site.

**The authoring format is a deliverable.** Every section is authored by the dev team as project work (E9–E11), and a section file opens directly in a browser during authoring — which is most of why annotated HTML won. The `data-*` directive vocabulary is therefore **documented as a shipped internal deliverable**, not left tribal.

### 7.4 Generated theme structure

```
inflozo-{slug}/
  package.json          # NO engines.ghost-api (FR-J1, NFR-7) — but engines.ghost ">=5.0.0" IS emitted (FR-J2); engines.ghost >=5.0.0; card_assets {exclude:[…]} (FR-Q7); image_sizes (normative table, FR-J2); posts_per_page; custom (FR-Q5 built-ins + FR-Q2 user settings)
  routes.yaml           # only if Routes Manager used
  default.hbs           # shell: fonts, token block link, header/footer partials, ghost_head/foot;
                        #   body carries {{body_class}} + the color_scheme class (FR-E4, FR-Q5);
                        #   inline token block for promoted colours (FR-Q3) and the three dark built-ins' fallback chains
  home.hbs              # the site root; Ghost resolves it ahead of index.hbs (FR-I1)
  index.hbs  post.hbs  page.hbs  tag.hbs  author.hbs  error.hbs
  custom-{name}.hbs     # Routes Manager custom templates AND designed membership pages — theme root only (FR-I1, FR-I3)
  private.hbs           # only if a Private Site Gate section is designed (FR-I1, Appendix A §31)
  partials/
    header.hbs  footer.hbs  post-card.hbs  pagination.hbs   # site chrome + Ghost-sourced repeaters
    content-cta.hbs     # Ghost's paywall override point — only if the paywall is designed (FR-H6, FR-I1)
    sections/
      {template}/…      # one partial per placed section, per template (e.g. sections/home/hero.hbs)
      shared/…          # sections placed on more than one template
  locales/en.json           # ALWAYS emitted — Ghost's fallback of record (FR-Q6)
  locales/{language}.json   # plus the project language, when it is not English (FR-Q6)
  assets/fonts/…        # self-hosted woff2 subsets of the project's font pairing (FR-J3)
  assets/css/screen.css # tokens + base + used-section styles
  assets/css/cards.css  # Koenig card styling for every card the user designed (FR-Q7)
  assets/js/cards.js    # vendored Ghost card behaviour, only for designed audio/video/gallery/toggle (FR-Q7)
  assets/js/main.js     # FR-G7 behaviour modules, only those the placed designs declare (repo-authored code only — FR-J4)
  assets/images/…       # hashed, referenced assets only
  README.md             # install + routes.yaml step + credits
```

**Emitted-theme conventions (normative, and labelled by kind).** `<main>` wraps the template's own content region, never the whole document, and never nests — Ghost's own Source theme nests it on the home route, so the benchmark is not a safe guide here (NFR-5 landmark rule). **`robots.txt` and the sitemap are Ghost's**, served regardless of theme; the theme states no position and overrides nothing. **AMP was removed in Ghost 6.0** (not 5.x) and no AMP template is emitted; the v6 spec's AMP rule is a warning matched on content, not on a filename. **`README.md` is a deliverable, not a placeholder:** it states install steps, the FR-J15 credit where the plan requires it, the Style Pack and font pairing in use, and where to re-import. Of the theme-convention rules gathered here, only the accessible-name rule (NFR-5) and the inert-emission rule (FR-J1) are enforced by anything outside Inflozo's own prose — the rest are house style, and a future reader should not mistake them for platform constraints.

**Ghost's custom-font variables (normative, added 2026-08-19).** `assets/css/screen.css` **must** declare `--gh-font-heading` and `--gh-font-body`, in that one file. `GS051-CUSTOM-FONTS` is a **warning on both the v5 and v6 specs** and passes only when a single `.css` or `.hbs` file contains both strings (`gscan/lib/checks/051-custom-fonts-css-properties.js` — a lookahead-pair regex, read from source and reproduced on a fixture). Without it **every** theme Inflozo emits carries a standing warning, and G3's "warnings surfaced, always", FR-J6's 0-errors-0-warnings target and every category gate's automated sheet become unmeetable across the whole library simultaneously. The emitted form binds Ghost's variable as the preferred value and the project's font pairing as the fallback — `--font-heading: var(--gh-font-heading, "{pairing heading}")` — so the site owner's Ghost Admin choice wins where they have made one, per §1.2's carve-out 1b. This rule was absent from every earlier version of this document and was found by running gscan on a purpose-built fixture; the evidence is in `../../architecture/architecture-Inflozo-2026-08-19/MEASUREMENTS.md` §6.

**Stylesheet order relative to `{{ghost_head}}` (normative).** The theme stylesheet is emitted **before** `{{ghost_head}}`, and stays there. Ghost injects its own card stylesheet inside `{{ghost_head}}`, so anything Ghost emits wins an equal-specificity tie — which is precisely why FR-Q7 tells Ghost not to emit for the cards the user designed, rather than trying to out-specify it. **Moving the theme stylesheet after `{{ghost_head}}` is forbidden:** it would also place it after the user's own Code Injection, silently overriding every customer's custom CSS to win an argument that FR-Q7 has already settled.

**`partials/` root holds site chrome** — header, footer, post-card, pagination — the markup that is not a placed section, or that every template shares. **`partials/content-cta.hbs` is Ghost's paywall override point:** Ghost renders it in place of its own "subscribe to keep reading" block wherever the post's paywall line falls, which is exactly why the paywall is a **template surface with its own editor** (FR-H6) rather than a placeable section.

**Partial rules (normative):**

- **One partial per placed section.** A section instance placed on a template compiles to `partials/sections/{template}/{name}.hbs` and is invoked from that template. "One partial per placed section" replaces the undefined "instance group".
- **Byte-identical sections share one partial**, hoisted to `partials/sections/shared/`. Identity is over the **emitted `.hbs` text** after content and control substitution — not over section id, not over variant id. **A section placed on both `post.hbs` and `page.hbs` needs no exception to this rule**, because the resource is one object under two keys and Inflozo emits `{{#post}}` on both (FR-G3, FR-H7) — the emitted text is therefore identical, which is the whole reason the compiler standardises on that form rather than on `{{#page}}`: the same design emits the same text and merging it is correct. The one case that *does* differ is A24, whose `page.hbs` compilation wraps title and feature image in `{{#if @page.show_title_and_feature_image}}` (FR-I1) — that guard makes the two texts unequal, so byte-identity already keeps them apart. The compiler must never elide the guard to win a hoist.
- **Near-identical sections may share one partial with scalar hash params** (`{{> "sections/shared/cta" heading="…"}}`), and only where every difference is a scalar value. This is an optimization the compiler may take; emitting two separate partials is always correct.
- **Arrays are NEVER flattened into numbered hash params.** `item1_title`, `item2_title` is forbidden — Handlebars hash params carry scalars, and the flattening is unreadable in exactly the output FR-J1 requires to be hand-editable. A section whose content differs by a repeated list gets its own partial. Repeaters whose content comes from **Ghost** take **no params at all**: `{{#foreach}}` supplies the context (FR-J1, proven in the spike).
- **Layer names become filenames.** The per-instance layer name (FR-D5) is slugified to the partial's filename, with a numeric suffix on collision within its directory; when byte-identical instances merge into one shared partial, it takes the first instance's name in template-then-position order. The mapping is **fully deterministic** — the same project doc compiles to the same filenames on every machine and every run — which is what makes NFR-6(c1)'s committed `.hbs` snapshots diff cleanly instead of churning on names.
- **Custom template filenames are a frozen public API — the one exception to the rule above.** Section partial names are derived from layer names and may change freely on any recompile, because nothing outside the theme references them. A `custom-{name}.hbs` filename may **never** change once deployed: Ghost stores that exact string on every page that selects it and never revalidates or repairs it, so renaming an emitted custom template silently detaches every page pointing at it, each falling back to `page.hbs` with no error at compile, upload or render (`research-ghost-membership-pages.md` §0, §2). The filename is also the label: Ghost derives the Template dropdown's entry from it by a fixed transform — hyphens to spaces, title-cased, `custom-member-home.hbs` → "Member Home" — with no override anywhere in the theme, and `package.json` declares nothing about templates. Custom templates must sit at the **theme root**; one in a subdirectory never reaches the dropdown at all.

- **The naming and collision scheme (normative — a frozen public API needs a mechanism, not only a warning).** Everything below is authored **now** because none of it is fixable after the first user ships a theme.

  **Naming.** The user types a **display name** — "Member Home", "About Us" — and never types a filename. Inflozo derives `custom-{slug}.hbs` from it: lowercase, ASCII-fold, spaces and underscores to single hyphens, strip everything outside `[a-z0-9-]`, collapse repeated hyphens, trim leading and trailing ones. The **derivation is shown live at the naming step, filename and dropdown label side by side**, because Ghost's label transform is fixed and unoverridable — hyphens to spaces, title-cased — so the user is choosing the label Ghost will show them in the page editor whether or not they realise it. A name that slugifies to nothing, or to a reserved stem (`index`, `home`, `post`, `page`, `tag`, `author`, `error`, `default`, `private`, `robots`, `amp`), is refused at entry with the reason.

  **Collision.** Three kinds, and each is **prevented at creation, never resolved at compile** — a compiler that silently disambiguates is a compiler that silently detaches a page. **(1) Two names slugifying identically** ("Member Home" and "member-home") collide; the second is refused and the user renames, because auto-suffixing would produce `custom-member-home-2.hbs` and Ghost would label it "Member Home 2" without ever saying why. **(2) A name matching a `custom-{name}.hbs` this project has already deployed** — including one whose surface was since deleted — is refused, because Ghost pages may still point at that string; deployed custom-template names are retained per project for exactly this check and are never reused. **(3) `page-{slug}.hbs`** cannot collide, because Inflozo never emits that form (FR-I1) — Ghost matches it against the live slug at render time and **disables the Template dropdown outright** when one matches, which would override the user's explicit choice (read from the built admin bundle on both majors: `gh-psm-template-select.hbs` renders the select `@disabled=matchedSlugTemplate` with the line "Post URL matches {filename}" — VERIFY-AT-BUILD item 59).

  **Renaming a deployed template is not offered.** Not warned, not confirmed — **absent**. The display name may be edited (it is the project's own label); the filename, once deployed, is fixed for the life of the project. Where a user genuinely wants a different name, the honest path is a new template plus re-pointing the Ghost pages by hand, and the UI says so. **Drift detection (FR-J16) must not be the thing that catches this** — drift compares content and reports what already happened on a live site; a rename that reaches deploy has already broken every page pointing at it, and prevention upstream is the only mechanism that helps.

### 7.5 Data model sketch — illustrative, not exhaustive

**This is a sketch, not a specification.** It shows shape and RLS intent for the core entities and nothing more. **The Architect owns the full schema**; §8's E1 carries a dedicated schema story whose scope is the whole model, not the rows below. Where this sketch and an FR disagree, the FR wins.

| Table | Key fields |
|---|---|
| `profiles` | user_id, display_name, created_at |
| `projects` | id, user_id, name, slug, style_pack jsonb, dark_enabled, linked_site_id?, thumb_path (unused in v1 — FR-B1 ships static placeholders; retained so deferred capture needs no migration), language, updated_at |
| `project_templates` | project_id, template_key, doc jsonb (ordered section instances: sectionId, designId, content, controls, **parkedControls** — values retained against the design they came from, so FR-D19's park-and-restore round trip survives a reload and is visible to FR-D9's undo journal — darkOverrides, hidden), updated_at |
| `sites` | id, user_id, url, title, ghost_version, capability (full/preview_only), status, content_key, admin_key_vault_ref, **staff_token_vault_ref (nullable — the token is deferred to first deploy and may be declined permanently, FR-C1)**, **credentials_present** (which of the three exist, so a partially credentialed site is a stored state rather than an inferred one), **site_settings snapshot** (code-injection flag, Portal floating-button state, members/paid-members enabled, comments enabled — read at connect and refreshed by FR-C5, and the source the canvas shim and the pre-deploy checks both read), last_checked_at |
| `assets` | id, user_id, path, bytes, mime, w, h, hash, display_name, created_at |
| `deploys` | id, project_id, site_id, version, artifact_path, gscan jsonb, **status** (`compiling` · `checking` · `uploaded` · `live` · `failed` — `uploaded` is the partial success FR-J8 defines, and it is a first-class value, not the absence of `live`), **activated** (bool), **content manifest** (path → SHA-256 **plus a per-file fingerprint**, FR-J16), created_at |
| `subscriptions` | user_id, dodo_customer_id, dodo_subscription_id, plan, status, period_end |
| `suggestions` / `suggestion_votes` | id, user_id, category, title, body, image_path?, status, votes count |

All rows RLS-scoped to `user_id` (suggestions publicly readable). Registry, Style Pack presets, starters, the FR-Q6 string catalog and Orbit Weekly live in the repo as versioned data, not in the DB.

**Tables the FRs are known to require beyond the sketch.** This list exists so nothing is lost between the PRD and the schema; it is still not a complete schema, and the Architect may reshape any of it.

- **`notifications`** — FR-B7's per-user feed. Written by deploy outcomes, site health changes, FR-C5 compatibility notices and billing events; 90-day retention needs a cron job (§7.1). **Created in E1** (§8), because four epics write to it before E13 builds its UI.
- **`edit_locks`** — FR-D18 / `addendum.md` §AD2: one lock record per project, with holder, heartbeat, and the unsynced-**edit** count that appears verbatim in the take-over string.
- **Routes state on `sites`** — FR-I4's last-offered `routes.yaml` plus its byte-comparison verification state. There is no "unverified" limbo state to store; the comparison either matches or it does not.
- **`entitlements`** — FR-L2 names it directly and the sketch has only `subscriptions.status`. Table or derived view is the Architect's call, but the state machine (including the dispute-opened transition) needs a home that is not a status string.
- **Theme-settings definitions** — FR-Q1/Q2's custom-settings builder output per project: name, type, options, default, Ghost Admin group, and the control or token each is bound to. Compile validation reads this to reject dangling bindings.
- **Translation overrides** — FR-Q6's per-project catalog overrides, keyed on the dotted catalog key, carrying FR-J14's migration-map state for superseded keys.
- **An export record** — FR-J12's ZIP export currently leaves no trace at all: an `exports` row, or at minimum an `exported_at` stamp on the project.
- **`site_snapshots`** — FR-J13's pre-Inflozo snapshot, **keyed on `user_id`** and holding the **site record id** (never the raw URL — FR-C6), stored in the `site-snapshots` bucket (§7.1). RLS on `user_id` is what stops one user's archived theme becoming reachable by another when two accounts connect the same Ghost site. Orphaned rows carry a TTL; the overwrite rule is stated; FR-A5 offers the zip as a download before purge.
- **`project_site_bindings(project_id, site_id, theme_name, frozen_at)`** — FR-J10's frozen theme name, and **exempt from FR-J7's artifact pruning**. The name's only other derivation is the earliest `deploys` row for that pair, which pruning destroys; after that the next deploy re-derives a fresh name and Ghost Admin accumulates exactly the orphaned themes FR-J10 exists to prevent.
- **`deploy_jobs`** — FR-J8's five-stage progress UI with cancel in the first two stages: a **stage enum** and a **cancel flag**, written from the first stage rather than on success. FR-J7 stores artifacts only on success, so without this a failed compile has nothing for the UI to poll.
- **`asset_usages`** — FR-K4's "used in 3 projects" chip and delete warning, recomputed on doc upsert rather than derived by scanning every `project_templates.doc` (FR-K1's grid would otherwise cost N scans per page load). **Known staleness:** local-first sync (FR-D10) means a doc can reference an asset for up to one sync interval before the server learns of it, so the warning can be wrong by that interval — delete therefore re-verifies against the live doc before it commits.
- **Per-deploy variant manifest** — the `{section id, variant id}` set a deploy shipped, plus the `library_version` it compiled against. FR-J14's "the library has advanced beyond your last deploy" and its change summary have no other substrate, and FR-J16's drift comparison reads it. This is FR-J14/FR-J16 substrate only; FR-C5's compatibility notice remains one broadcast per Ghost release and reads nothing per-site.

### 7.6 Key risks & mitigations

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Supabase passkeys are experimental | Magic link is primary; passkeys feature-flagged; client lib pinned |
| Ghost(Pro) Starter blocks custom themes | FR-C2 probes `hostSettings.limits` at connect and sets Preview-only automatically, falling back to asking the user when the shape is absent; the deploy error remains the authoritative signal. **⚠ Nothing verifies this yet** — T4 is deferred until after MVP (§4), so the probe, the Preview-only state and the refusal message are all written against an unobserved payload, and the fallback (asking the user which plan they are on) is the *primary* path until the pre-launch gate clears the setting and the clearing path |
| Helper-shim drift vs Ghost releases | NFR-6(c2) shim contract tests against recorded real-Ghost output, per-commit and offline, plus the NFR-6(c3) nightly perceptual diff against a real Ghost target; `Accept-Version` pinned |
| `routes.yaml` cannot be uploaded by API on any Ghost version or host | Settled, not a hazard to manage: the guided manual card is the **only** path (FR-I4), and completion is verified by byte comparison of the live `routes.yaml` — there is no unverified limbo state |
| A gscan bump turns the whole shipped library red at once — **`GS100` most of all**, since FR-Q5 puts three referenced `@custom` keys in every theme Inflozo emits | Inflozo pins one gscan version at or above the newest bundled by any supported target; an upgrade lands only behind a library release with a full re-run of the inventory (FR-J6) |
| Dodo is a young MoR ($30 disputes, payout terms) | Idempotent webhooks, entitlement grace, revenue monitoring; swappable billing adapter |
| **Library scale slips the schedule** — the largest risk in this document, and it is a **timeline** risk, not a quality one | Shared primitives established once in the shell block (E9); the category owner gate catches a defective primitive at the category that introduced it rather than after the library inherits it; the render matrix and FR-J17 catch regressions cheaply. **Mitigation is a longer runway, never a smaller library** — §4's no-descope clause is governing, and Appendix F.3a states the commitment in frames so the runway can be planned against it. **This row must not be read as a descope prompt; that option was considered and declined** |
| **The plan assumes a delivery capacity that is not staffed** — every design authored, every category gated, and the owner is the sole approver by design (§4) | Stated here rather than left implicit. The gate sequence is deliberate, so the bottleneck is a chosen mechanism and not an oversight; the exposure is entirely in **elapsed time**, and it compounds with the unbounded pre-revenue period in F.3a. No mitigation is claimed beyond the owner's stated acceptance of it (§4) |
| **One malformed translation override 500s every page that renders that label** — Ghost's ICU `MessageFormat` constructor throws outside its own error handling and the throw escapes into `res.render` | FR-Q8 validates at **entry**, refusing to save a broken override, with a compile-time backstop (`appendix-h1-string-catalog.md` V9/V10). It is the one user-typed value that reaches a visitor's render path, so it carries the same validation obligation as any untrusted input (NFR-3, NFR-4) |
| **A silently mis-parsed NQL filter serves the wrong posts, with no error anywhere** — `((tag:news))` parses to a garbage key with no exception, and All/Any nesting is exactly what a filter builder emits | FR-I2 never emits redundant or nested parentheses and flattens single-child groups, **and its round-trip validation asserts the parsed AST matches the intended filter** rather than merely that parsing succeeded — a success-only round trip cannot see this class at all |
| **A gscan cascade misdiagnoses a valid file** — a malformed `visibility` string produces 19 errors including a bogus `GS010-PJ-PARSE` against a `package.json` that is fine | FR-J6 detects the cascade signature and replaces it with one Inflozo-authored explanation naming the real cause. This is the case where the verbatim-passthrough fallback is exactly wrong: verbatim means showing the user 19 errors and one lie |
| **An unverified assertion about an external platform enters this document with the authority of research** — it has happened **four times** (FR-I2's relative dates, FR-I2's per-collection page size, FR-I2's per-collection limit override, FR-I4's routes upload), and each one **deleted or damaged a working feature**. The confident *reason* attached to each is what stopped anyone downstream from re-examining it | **Standing rule, stated here rather than only in project history: a claim about an external platform is a hypothesis until read in that platform's source or executed against it.** The two verification passes that caught these are named in the preamble's precedence order and outrank the review findings they check. New external claims arrive with a citation or a fixture, or they do not arrive |
| **A design treats `{{total_members}}` as a number and breaks silently** — Ghost rounds down and appends `+`, so the value is a string on every real site | Named normatively in Appendix B and on A7 #12's descriptor; the shim renders the same shape on canvas (FR-H5), so the failure is visible while designing rather than after deploy |
| **Ghost injects surfaces the theme does not own and cannot fully style** — sodo-search's overlay, Portal's floating button, Ghost's native comments and its own announcement bar | Each is named in §1.2's carve-out and excluded from NFR-6(c3), so the fidelity claim is scoped rather than overstated. Where styling is reachable it is used — **the search trigger is always styled, and that is now the whole of what a theme owns of search** (ruling R-24): `sodo-search` renders **inside an iframe** with its own injected stylesheet, so theme CSS reaches nothing within it and only Ghost's accent colour crosses (`MEASUREMENTS.md` §29c). Where styling is not reachable, Portal's button is **shimmed on canvas** so designs are authored around it rather than into it. **Neither overlay's markup or class names may ever be depended on** — both are served from jsDelivr at a floating minor range and can change without a Ghost upgrade (register item 51) |
| **A dependency relicenses and puts GPL code inside themes users sell** — `typed.js` went MIT → GPL-3.0 at v3.0.0 and the GPL build is npm `latest` | FR-G7's answer is structural rather than procedural: **zero third-party runtime dependencies**, so compile CI asserts `assets/js/` holds only repo-authored files and the licence question cannot arise. The hard permissive filter (§7.1) governs any future proposal, re-verified at the pinned version; taking FR-G7's one conditional exception would replace the grep with an SPDX allowlist and a `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES` file in the zip |

**Verify at build time** — external-world facts this PRD relies on that can change, or that this session's research inferred without executing. Re-confirm each before the dependent epic starts:

1. **⛔ LAUNCH-BLOCKING — Ghost(Pro) 2026 plan lineup** and the Starter custom-theme restriction (FR-C2, §4 T4). Rides with item 2 and clears with it
2. **⛔ LAUNCH-BLOCKING — the `hostSettings.limits` shape** returned by `GET /admin/config/`, per host: undocumented, host-controlled, and the thing FR-C2's probe parses. **This is the one verify-at-build item that blocks public launch rather than an epic** (§4): no launch and no Ghost(Pro)-aimed marketing until a real Starter payload is captured and the detection verified against the recording (FR-C2)
3. **Theme-upload size limits per host**, measured on T1–T3, so FR-J3's theme-size budget can be set below the lowest observed (FR-J3, FR-K5)
4. **Supabase passkey API status** and `supabase-js` ≥ 2.105.0 behavior (FR-A2)
5. **Dodo Payments fee schedule and MoR terms** (Appendix F)
6. **The full Dodo event catalog**, and whether Dodo sends the annual renewal notice as merchant of record — if it does not, that notice becomes FR-P1 email #6 with an explicit FR-P2 carve-out, because it is statutory in several target markets (FR-L2, FR-P1/P2)
7. **gscan's current v6 warning set**, re-run before E7 exits and again at **every category gate**, since a single unavoidable library-wide warning makes those gates unmeetable (FR-J6). Note that gscan alone certifies very little — a deliberately broken theme scores 0 errors / 0 warnings on both specs — which is why FR-J17's quality gate exists alongside it
8. **Whether Ghost release notes are usable as compatibility data** — FR-C5's per-release broadcast assumes a parseable, reliable signal exists (FR-C5)
9. **Whether the Owner's Staff Access Token lifts `GET /themes/`** — if it does, real theme-name collision detection becomes possible and FR-J10's guarantee stops resting on Inflozo's own records alone (FR-J10, FR-J13)
10. **Snapshot re-upload acceptance**, including a theme that fails gscan — Ghost validates on upload, so the realistic snapshot case (an old fork on a since-upgraded site) may be rejected at the worst possible moment; confirm the behavior and the designed fallback (FR-J13)
11. **`partials/content-cta.hbs` as the documented paywall override point**, per connected Ghost version (FR-H6, §7.4)
12. **Ghost 6 API pagination behavior** — `?limit=all` removed, max 100 per page (Appendix B)
13. **Stand up a scratch Ghost install and confirm the four docs-vs-code conflicts** found in `research-ghost-binding-contexts.md`. Nothing in either research file was executed at runtime; every claim derives from source, theme code, gscan rules or docs. Chief among them is **`@even`/`@odd` parity, which the docs describe backwards** — the code makes the *first* item odd — and which would invert zebra striping across the entire library. `spike-compiler/` is static analysis and does not close this: it never ran a real Ghost.
14. **Confirm the membership mechanism against a running Ghost.** Nothing in `research-ghost-membership-pages.md` was executed at runtime either — every claim derives from Ghost 6.58 source, Portal source, shipped theme files, gscan rules or docs (§11.5). Four checks, in priority order: **(a)** retitle a page that has a template selected and confirm the template still applies — the load-bearing claim under FR-I1, FR-D6 and the whole A30 re-point; **(b)** `{{#get "tiers"}}` on a site with a hidden tier, which settles a **direct contradiction against `research-ghost-binding-contexts.md`** — that companion and Ghost's docs both state the tiers endpoint filters by visibility, and the membership research found no such filter in the code and believes both wrong (§11.1); until it is settled FR-H6's explicit `visibility:public` filter is correct either way; **(c)** the Template dropdown's label transform on a multi-word name (`custom-member-home.hbs` → "Member Home"), which FR-I1's naming rule and §7.4's frozen-filename constraint both depend on; **(d)** `data-portal="upgrade"` for a logged-in free member, confirming it falls through to Portal's default screen and that `account/plans` is the real upgrade action (Appendix B).

15. **What a theme may style inside `{{comments}}`'s native output** — A28's ten designs are scoped to the chrome around the thread (`sections-inventory.md` A28) precisely because this was inferred rather than executed. Confirm what markup Ghost renders for comments on a supported target, and whether theme CSS reaches it, before E10 opens the Comments category (FR-H3, FR-H5)
16. **Regenerating the style-guide fixture against the pinned Ghost target** — the fixture is snapshotted from Ghost's `render*Node` functions, so the snapshot method must be re-confirmed on each target bump, and the resulting diff reviewed rather than accepted (FR-H3, NFR-6(c1))

17. **Re-compute the Baseline floor before each category block, and re-verify licences at every bump** — the `widelyAvailableOnDate` pin holds the floor stable by design, but `web-features` data moves underneath it, so the resolved floor is recomputed and diffed rather than assumed, and any bump carries a render-matrix re-run (FR-G8, NFR-6(a))

18. **Confirm `{{total_members}}`'s rounding and `+` suffix on a live site, and that `{{content_api_key}}` renders in a theme context** — both are read from Ghost source and neither was executed; six designs and the whole zero-custom-setting result rest on them (Appendix B, `research-contested-variants.md`)

19. **✅ CLOSED BY EXECUTION 2026-08-19 — `GS100`'s exact trigger, proven on fixtures against both specs** (FR-Q5, FR-J6). Five fixture themes differing only in how the three built-ins are referenced were run through gscan 6.4.2 on the v5 **and** v6 specs. Four facts, all previously assumed: `GS100` is an **error**, not a warning, on both specs, so it blocks a deploy outright; it fires when **any** declared `config.custom` key is unreferenced, and two-of-three is not partial credit; a reference **inside a `{{#if}}` guard counts**, which is what makes FR-Q5's fallback chains a real reference rather than a token mention; and a reference **from a partial counts**, so it need not live in `default.hbs`. **FR-Q5's always-declare-always-reference design is confirmed sufficient** — the reference form scores 0 errors / 0 warnings on both specs. Re-prove on any gscan bump; the fixture is checked in. Evidence: `../../architecture/architecture-Inflozo-2026-08-19/MEASUREMENTS.md` §5

20. **Confirm the NQL build Ghost actually resolves at runtime** — the relative-date restore and the nested-parenthesis defect are both proven against `@tryghost/nql@0.13.x`, but Ghost's `package.json` declares `"catalog:"`, so its resolved build was **not** verified. Two normative rules in FR-I2 rest on it

21. **Confirm the announcement-bar seed against a running Ghost** — read `announcement_content` / `_background` / `_visibility` through a real Admin API integration and confirm all three come back (they are a non-core, non-secret settings group, read from source but not executed), and confirm that clearing the content stops Ghost emitting its bar script at all. FR-C4's seed and the one-click clear both rest on this (Appendix A §2)

22. **`GS051-CUSTOM-FONTS`, and any other gscan rule this document has never named** *(added 2026-08-19)*. This rule — a warning on both specs, requiring `--gh-font-heading` and `--gh-font-body` in one file — was in no version of this PRD and was found by running gscan on a fixture rather than by reading. It is now normative in §7.4 and carved out in §1.2. **The general item is the one that matters:** the reachable-rule shortlist FR-J6 maps against was derived from what this document expected gscan to check, not from what gscan actually checks. Before E7 exits, enumerate the **full** v5 and v6 rule sets from `gscan/lib/specs` and diff them against the shortlist, so the next unnamed rule is found by a diff rather than by a red build across the whole library.

**Assumed, unvalidated** — figures this PRD states with the same typographic confidence as the verified facts above, but which are not verified. Each carries whatever basis exists. None is a gate; all are revisited against real data once it exists.

| Assumption | Basis |
|---|---|
| G8 — ≥ 30% of signups reach first deploy within 7 days | Founder estimate. No Ghost-ecosystem activation data exists; no in-app analytics ship in v1 (NFR-8), so this is measured manually or not at all |
| G8 counter — 7-day rollback rate < 10% | Founder estimate |
| G9 — ≥ 3% free→Pro within 90 days | Comparable-product benchmark for freemium developer tools; no Ghost-specific data |
| G9 counter — refund/dispute rate < 2% | Founder estimate, and now interacting with a no-refund policy that does not stop chargebacks (FR-L2) |
| G10 — Pro steady-state monthly churn < 5% from month 2 | Founder estimate; Appendix F models 25% month-1 and 4% steady-state separately |
| Appendix F — 60% yearly / 40% monthly plan mix | Founder estimate. Sensitivity is only ±8% across the full 100%-monthly-to-100%-yearly range, so this is **not** the model's dominant uncertainty — the Test environment's cost is. Flagged for revisit after 3 months of real checkout data |
| G7 — cash break-even ≈11–15, payback ≈160–180 | Derived from the mix assumption, the stated recurring cost and F.3a's declared frame rate and amortisation window; not measured |
| Design-frame count (≈1,500–2,000) and the $25/frame nominal rate | Owner estimate; F.3a's payback figure moves proportionally with both |
| The pre-revenue duration | Deliberately unbounded (§4). The recurring cost runs for an unknown number of months with no revenue against it |
| Free-tier churn | **Missing, and F.7's paid-share ratio is undefined without it** — a cohort conversion rate is applied there as a standing population ratio |
| §1.5 — Fantasma's "155+ presets", pricing, and feature description | Public product material, observed August 2026. A dated observation; §1.5's own re-verify-at-publish rule applies to every reuse |
| NFR-1 — the 40-section template as a realistic worst case | Founder estimate of an upper-bound real project; the reference laptop and 4× throttle are the fixed half of the gate, the fixture is the assumed half |

---

## 8. Epic Breakdown (BMAD sequencing)

> Each epic lists goal → representative stories → exit criteria, and **the FR range it owns**. Every FR in §5 is owned — by exactly one epic, or, where an FR quantifies over the whole library, jointly by the shell-block epic and the gated library pipeline that between them cover every category — so epic membership is checkable rather than inferred. The SM agent expands stories with full acceptance criteria; scope is bounded by §5 and the appendices.

**Story granularity (normative).** In the library epics (E9–E11) **a category is a story, not a design** — one story per category, not one per design. A category story delivers every design in that category against one shared content model and one stylesheet, with **per-design control sets** (§7.3, FR-F3), and it exits on **§4's category owner gate**. Design-per-story would produce hundreds of stories carrying near-identical acceptance criteria and would fragment the shared primitives the library depends on. Everywhere else the default is one story per FR, split only where an FR carries two independently shippable surfaces. **A category story delivers its behaviour modules too** (FR-G7): the JS a design needs is authored beside it in the same story, never deferred to a module project, and the story is not done while a declared module is missing its no-JS degradation.

**The library epics run sequentially, not in parallel (normative).** There are no waves. §4 fixes the order — the shell block, then the gated categories in inventory order beginning with Heroes — and **each category's owner gate blocks the next category's start**.
