Two consequences bind the SM agent: a category story may not begin until its design-and-specification output exists (§4), and no two category stories are ever in flight at once. Throughput is deliberately traded for the guarantee that a defect in a shared primitive is caught by the gate that introduced it rather than inherited by everything after it.

**The five pilot sections (normative).** E4, E5 and E6 all gate on "the pilot sections", so they are fixed here and cannot be chosen at implementation time from the easy end of the library. They span the hard cases, not the common ones:

*The number is the identity — `A4 #2` is the second design of Heroes as the export numbers it; the names below were re-derived from `tools/export-roster.py` on 2026-09-04 after the inventory merge had renamed them, and that tool is the source if they move again.*

| Pilot | Why it is in the set |
|---|---|
| **A1 #1 Rail** (Headers & Navigation) | Site-wide singleton: lives on `default.hbs`, compiles to `partials/header.hbs` rather than a section partial, binds `@site.navigation` and `@site.logo`, and changes every template at once |
| **A17 #1 Three Up** (Post Grids) | Dynamic feed: `{{#foreach}}` over the native paginated context, extracts the `post-card` partial invoked with **no params**, carries the main-feed designation and pagination, and is where the native-context-vs-`{{#get}}` rule is decided |
| **A22 #1 Inline Row** (Newsletter / Subscribe) | Members-aware: `@member` gating, the show-to control, Portal `data-portal` actions, and FR-D16's member-state preview |
| **A24 #1 Centred** (Post Headers) | Wrapper context: valid only inside `{{#post}}` on `post.hbs` — the axis of FR-H7 that is easiest to get backwards, and that renders silently empty when it is |
| **A4 #2 Flush Left** (Heroes) | Static and control-dense: rich text with all four marks, a guarded media binding with `srcset` (FR-H8), and the heaviest control set in the library for §7.3's data-attribute mechanism |

**The post-body trio (normative sequencing).** The Post Content section (A25), the Ghost card design module (FR-Q7, styling A33's cards) and the style-guide fixture they are both designed against (FR-H3) are **one feature area specified in one pass**, not three independent pieces of work: A25's designs wrap the fixture, the card module styles what is inside it, and neither can be judged without the other rendering. Their build homes stay separate and each FR keeps exactly one owning epic — the fixture is **E4** platform work, the card module and its `cards.css` emission are **E7**, A25's twelve designs are a gated **E10** category — but two dependencies bind them: **A25's owner gate cannot open until the card module emits**, because a reading design assessed against Ghost's default card styling is being assessed against markup the finished theme will never render; and **both depend on the fixture existing in E4**. A25's design-and-specification session covers all three (§4).

**And they are emitted in a fixed order, with a stated ownership line** *(2026-08-31, decided as architecture; §A3 of `reconcile-designs-decisions.md`)*: **`cards.css` is emitted first, then the per-design stylesheets** — the browser order §7.4 already states, made explicit here because it is the thing that decides who wins on a `.kg-*` class. Per-design rules therefore override `cards.css` on the `.kg-*` classes they legitimately style, which is the intended direction. **The ownership line between A25 and A33: A33 owns the card interiors, A25 owns the column they sit in.** Ruling R-6 fixed *which claims* each may make; this fixes *which rule wins* when both are loaded, and the two are separate failures — a correct claim in the wrong cascade position still renders wrong.

**E0 · De-risking Spikes** — *Owns no FRs; owns two blocking spikes.* Both must complete before the work that rests on them, and both exist because this document has twice been damaged by building on an unverified premise.

**(a) The FR-D4 mark-emission spike — first, before anything.** Rich text is stored as text plus mark ranges, never as an HTML string, and the compiler is the single place those ranges become markup. A reviewer judged that path unimplemented and structurally impossible as currently designed. Every user-authored text prop in the product depends on it, so it is proven — in code, against real Handlebars, including the escaping path — before the shell block starts. **The escaping bug already found is in scope:** the current rule turns a user's literal `\{{` into a live expression, which defeats FR-J1's inert-emission guarantee outright and can produce a template that will not compile.

**(b) The platform-verification spike.** **Every** verify-at-build item in §7.6 executed against a real Ghost, with each result recorded — the whole list as it stands when this spike opens, since it grows as the document finds further unexecuted external facts. §7.6 instructs that these be re-confirmed before the epics that depend on them and, until now, assigned that to nobody — while item 14(a) is the load-bearing premise under the membership designs, FR-I1 and FR-O1, and the *previous* version of that same premise was found false mid-encode and forced a rebuild of five sections.

*Exit:* the mark path demonstrated end-to-end with a runnable test covering all four marks, paste normalization and the escaping cases; and every §7.6 item marked confirmed, refuted or still-open, with a refuted item raised as a scope change **before** its dependent epic begins rather than after.

**E1 · Foundations & Design System** — *Owns FR-A1, FR-B3, FR-B4, FR-B5, FR-P1 emails (1)–(2).* Repo, Next.js app, CI/CD to production Vercel, design tokens and components per the design references at `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/design/` — **read them in the preamble's stated precedence: prompt 2 wins over prompt 1 wherever they disagree, and every design artifact is *non-normative*, so this PRD and its normative companions win over any mockup** (`reconcile-mockups.md` records ~31 places where a mockup and the PRD already disagreed) — **but that precedence is about BEHAVIOUR, not about visual language** (owner, 2026-08-31). E1's tokens and components are **taken from the design export**, not re-derived: `Calibration Set` is the token and type scale, `Editor Sidebar Kit` is the control vocabulary, and `R Responsive System` is the collapse ladder. The PRD says what a surface must do; the export says what it is built from. A surface with no frame is **extrapolated from the nearest one that has** — same components, same tokens — and drawn in the same Claude Design project, never invented alongside it — magic-link auth, base navigation shell, Resend transport with the branded Supabase Auth templates.

> **Schema story (whole-model scope).** §7.5 is a sketch, not a specification, so this story builds the **full** schema and its RLS from §5 — not from §7.5's eight rows. It explicitly includes **`notifications`**, which E3, E7 and E12 all write to long before E13 renders it, plus `edit_locks`, `entitlements`, `deploy_jobs`, `asset_usages`, `site_snapshots`, `project_site_bindings`, theme-settings definitions, translation overrides, the export record, routes state on `sites`, and the per-deploy variant manifest. Without this story every later epic carries an un-storied migration.
> **Dashboard skeleton — cut line.** **IN:** the authenticated app shell (top bar, account menu, navigation), the projects list rendering name + updated-at only, create-blank, rename, delete with type-name-to-confirm (FR-B3), the plan cap check at creation (FR-B4), and the one-site-per-project rule (FR-B5). **IN**, because it no longer waits on anything: the **static Style-Pack placeholder** on each card (FR-B1 — there is no capture to build). **OUT**, because the things they display do not exist yet: linked-site badges and deploy-status chips (E3, E7), the other three creation paths (E13), the connected-sites strip, asset quota meter and changelog popover (E13), the notifications bell (E13).

*Exit:* sign in on production; the skeleton exactly as cut above; **RLS verified on every table the schema story creates**, not on a subset.

**E2 · Accounts & Passkeys** — *Owns FR-A2–A6.* Security page, passkey register/list/rename/revoke behind flag, email change with the upfront collision check, deletion cascade including the FR-J13 snapshot download offer and the suggestion-anonymization rules, 30-day rolling sessions and sign-out-everywhere.

*Exit:* passkey sign-in round-trip on production; deletion cascade verified end-to-end.

**E3 · Sites & Connections** — *Owns FR-C1–C6, FR-C8, FR-P1 email (3).* Connect wizard (Custom Integration **plus** the site Owner's Staff Access Token), server Admin proxy + Vault, connection validation against the authenticated `GET /admin/config/`, the `hostSettings.limits` Preview-only probe, auto-branding card, code-injection notice at connect, daily health checks feeding the "Reconnect needed" email, multi-site management, key rotation. **FR-C7's redesign proposals are not in this epic** — they need the runtime (E4), the packs (E6) and the starters (E11), and are owned by E13.

The connect probe reads the Portal floating-button state and asks when it cannot, storing it on the connection (FR-C2, §7.5). **Connect asks for one credential pair and stops** — the Staff Access Token is E7's, requested at first deploy — so this epic's wizard ends at a connected, previewable, partially credentialed site. *Exit:* T1–T3 connected and validated **with the token absent**, and the partially credentialed state round-tripped through FR-C8; on T4 the connect-time probe sets Preview-only and clears it when the probe changes. **The deploy-error half of the Starter path is verified at the E4/E7 joint gate below**, because FR-C2 makes the deploy error authoritative and no deploy exists until E7.

**E4 · Section Runtime Platform** — *Owns FR-F1–F8, FR-G3, FR-G7, FR-G8, FR-H1, FR-H3, FR-H5, FR-H7, FR-H8, and FR-Q6's string-catalog format.* **The `core` module and the module registry are platform work, not per-section work** — `core`'s registry, `data-i18n-*` reader, shared `IntersectionObserver` factory, `AbortController` teardown and reduced-motion gate all exist before the first category is built, as does FR-G8's Baseline tooling (`stylelint-plugin-use-baseline`, `eslint-plugin-compat`, `size-limit`), since every one of the stylesheets is authored against it. Registry format; the annotated-HTML authoring vocabulary and its documentation (§7.3, a shipped deliverable); **both renderers over one source**; the Ghost-helpers shim; the Orbit Weekly dataset; the schema-driven controls engine and its data-attribute CSS mechanism; the FR-H7 context matrix from `appendix-b1-template-contexts.md`; FR-H8's guards; and the NFR-6(c1) compiled-output snapshot harness together with the NFR-6(c2) shim contract tests.

> **The string catalog's keys, English defaults and `{{t}}` contract are defined here, not in E7.** E4 is where the library's first chrome strings are written, every design is constrained by the catalog and by the never-hard-code rule, so the format cannot arrive three epics later. E7 owns the Translations *surface* and the `locales/` emission.

*Exit:* the five pilot sections render editor-perfect on canvas, and their compiled `.hbs` snapshots are committed and diffing per-commit. **The fixture set ships here** (FR-H3): the style-guide post generated from Ghost's renderers and covering every class-affecting card design, the style-guide page, the comments fixture at its stated count, and the 32-post feed — each checked in, each diffing per-commit, and the style-guide post loading the theme stylesheet then the simulated `cards.min.css` in that order. Without it E5's canvas has nothing to put inside `{{content}}` and E7's card module has nothing to preview against. **"Compiles byte-identical" is not an E4 exit criterion** — see the joint gate.

**E4/E7 joint compile gate.** Held by both epics and evaluated when E7 lands. E4 cannot verify it alone (the compiler is E7's) and E7 cannot verify it against sections that do not exist, so neither epic exits until it is green: the five pilot sections compile from the same source they render from; the compiled theme passes gscan **0 errors / 0 warnings**; it deploys to T1–T3; and a deploy attempt against T4 fails with the friendly Starter message and sets Preview-only (FR-C2, §4) — **which is deferred with T4 itself and is not an exit condition for either epic**: the gate passes on T1–T3, and this clause is carried forward to the pre-launch Ghost(Pro) gate (§4) rather than blocking E4 or E7 on a target that does not exist.

**E5 · Editor Shell** — *Owns FR-D1–D22, FR-H2, FR-H4, FR-H6.* Canvas plus hover/selection chrome, layers, sidebar, template switcher, inline editing on FR-D4's text-plus-marks model, picker overlay, Variant Shuffle, Site Remix, undo/autosave, device + member-state + dark toggles, keyboard map, client-side Content API reads with SWR caching, the tiers-bound surfaces and the paywall template editor, and **FR-D18's edit lock with its take-over protocol** (`addendum.md` §AD2) — which E7's deploy and export both depend on.

**The two Ghost-surface shims are built here, against E4's contract** — a work split, not a second owner: **FR-H5 stays E4's**, which defines what the shims must render and how they stay inert, while Portal's floating button and Ghost's announcement strip are *canvas* work and land with the canvas. They draw from the connection's stored settings snapshot (FR-C2, §7.5), add no DOM Inflozo serializes, and are neither compiler work nor sections.

> **FR-H2's main-feed designation lifecycle belongs to E5.** Auto-designation of the first feed placed on a paginated template, re-designation when it is deleted or moved, the visible marker, the one-per-template exclusivity rule, and the zero-feed SEO-guard warning are all editor behavior. E7 emits what E5 designated.

*Exit:* full editing loop on the five pilot sections at 60 fps, measured on NFR-1's reference environment and 40-section fixture. **Play-loop gate (FR-D17, and the only gate on §1.2's central claim):** on the 40-section fixture, **20 consecutive Variant Shuffles across the placed sections followed by a full Site Remix re-roll preserve every content value *and every control value* with zero loss** — every prop and every control that held a value before holds the same value after, via the shared per-category content model and FR-D19's park-and-restore rule. **The round trip is asserted explicitly:** shuffle away from a design carrying a control the next design does not declare, shuffle back, and the parked value is restored exactly. And every re-roll obeys FR-D13's `bindingContext` / `compileTarget` restriction. Asserted programmatically over the project doc: no telemetry, no human judgment.

**E6 · Style Packs** — *Owns FR-E1–E5.* Token engine, 12 presets with hand-tuned paired dark palettes, the pack-switcher moment, per-mode token editors with FR-E3's live contrast check, the ~30-pairing font pool (Appendix D). **FR-E4's three-input mode resolution lands here** — system preference, the owner's server-rendered scheme class, the visitor's `data-mode` override, in that precedence — and it is the only place in the theme that selects on mode, since sections consume tokens exclusively.

*Exit:* live restyle < 300 ms; all 12 packs verified across the five pilot sections in both modes.

**E7 · Compiler, Deploy & Routes** — *Owns FR-I1–I6, FR-J1–J17 (including FR-J4's two-bundle emission and the `size-limit` gate), FR-K5, FR-Q1–Q5, FR-Q7–Q10, FR-Q6's Translations surface and `locales/` emission, FR-P1 email (5).* **The first-deploy credential step is E7's** — the Staff Access Token request, its decline path, and all three graceful degradations behind it (FR-J13's manual snapshot alternative, FR-J16's fail-open drift report, FR-I4's guided routes card), because each is a deploy-time surface and none of them exists at connect. **The always-reference mechanism is E7's** — every compile emits all three dark built-ins with their fallback chains, in every project, which is what keeps `GS100` unreachable (FR-Q5, FR-J6). Theme assembly per §7.3's mechanics and §7.4's structure, Synthesis Defaults, the gscan gate at the pinned version, artifacts, deploy/activate under the per-site advisory lock, pre-deploy drift detection, rollback, deploy history, ZIP export, the pre-Inflozo snapshot, library-update redeploy (FR-J12–J15), deploy-time asset bundling (FR-K5), Routes Manager with channels and the filter builder, Theme Settings and the custom-settings builder, the Translations surface, deploy-failure email.

*Exit:* pilot project deployed **and rolled back on all Ghost deploy targets (§4)**; routes round-trip through the guided card and verify by byte comparison of the live `routes.yaml`; the E4/E7 joint gate green.

**E8 · Asset Library** — *Owns FR-K1–K4, FR-K6.* Uploads with the native `canvas.toBlob` WebP path (encoder as fallback), quotas and the always-visible meter, `asset_usages` tracking with delete re-verification against the live doc, picker integration, replace/copy-URL/download.

*Exit:* quota enforcement and usage tracking verified end-to-end, including a replace that propagates across projects. (Deploy-time bundling is E7's FR-K5, so this epic does not gate the compiler.)

**E9 · The Shell Block** — *Owns FR-G1, FR-G4, FR-G5, FR-G6 for Group 1 (Headers & Navigation, Announcement Bars, Footers).* The complete site-wide chrome plus every template the compiler emits — not a minimal shell (§4). This epic is the **prerequisite for every gate that follows**: nothing renders on a real Ghost site without templates, a header and a footer, so the shell is what makes a category gate possible at all. It also establishes the components every later category reuses — nav, drawer, logo, button, link column, social row, inline newsletter form — which is why it is built once, carefully, before the library depends on it.

*Exit:* **three owner gates passed, one per category** (§4) — Headers & Navigation, Announcement Bars, Footers, each taken in turn and each blocking the next, exactly as a library category's gate does. Per gate: every design in that category passes §4's automated sheet — deployed and rendered live on T1 and T3, NFR-6(c3) comparison green, NFR-5 scan green, compile CI and FR-J17 green, render matrix green, FR-D17's preservation check green across the category's full design ring — and then the owner's own hands-on review on a live Ghost site. The epic does not exit on a single shell-wide sign-off; it exits when its third gate does.

**E10 · The Gated Library Pipeline** — *Owns FR-G1, FR-G4, FR-G5, FR-G6 for every category outside Group 1.* The remainder of the library, built **one category at a time in inventory order, beginning with Heroes**, each behind §4's blocking owner gate. Marketing categories, the Ghost content categories, the template-specific categories, the membership designs, and the non-placeable treatments — the Koenig card treatments delivered **against** the card design module (FR-Q7, owned by E7, which must therefore land first) and together with the post-content section they style, because they are one surface.

*Exit:* **every** category in the epic has passed its own owner gate. There is no epic-level shortcut — the epic exits when its last category does, and a category that has not passed cannot be carried forward as a known gap.

**E11 · Starters** — *Owns FR-G2 (the Free-tier floor, verifiable only once every category exists) and FR-O1–O4.* The 10 starter templates, composed from the finished library.

*Exit:* every starter deploys **0/0 gscan to all Ghost deploy targets (§4)**; at least Quiet and Ledger are Free end-to-end; FR-G2's Free-tier floor verified across the whole library.

**E12 · Billing & Entitlements** — *Owns FR-L1–L5, FR-P1 email (4) and the conditional annual-renewal email (6), FR-P2.* Dodo checkout with the explicit consent-to-immediate-delivery step, webhooks mapped to Dodo's real event vocabulary, the entitlement state machine including the dispute-opened transition, downgrade and block-until-user-deletes rules, the four contextual upgrade prompts, billing page.

*Exit:* live-mode $15 transaction; downgrade rules verified; entitlement transitions verified against real Dodo events.

**E13 · Suggestions, Notifications & Dashboard Completion** — *Owns FR-M1–M4, FR-B1, FR-B2, FR-B6, FR-B7, FR-C7.* Board UI (in-app and public), voting, moderation, admin claim; the FR-B7 notifications center over the table E1 created; and the dashboard cut line from E1 closed out — deploy-status chips, linked-site badges, all four creation paths, the connected-sites strip, asset quota meter and changelog popover. **FR-C7's redesign proposals land here**, where the runtime, packs and starters they compose all exist.

*Exit:* public read + authed vote on production; notification feed live and carrying the events E3, E7 and E12 already emit; every dashboard element in FR-B1/B2/B6 present.

**E14 · Marketing Site & Docs** — *Owns FR-N1–N5, FR-P3.* All pages, the sections gallery with live previews in same-origin iframes (§7.3), SEO/OG pipeline, MDX docs with the "Suggest an edit" relay, changelog on the dogfooded Ghost.

*Exit:* the gallery renders every design in Appendix A; every FR-N4 page exists and is non-empty; Lighthouse ≥ 95 on the marketing site (Inflozo's own host — NFR-2's no-Lighthouse rule scopes to *generated themes*, which run on the user's).

**E15 · Hardening & Launch** — *Owns no new FRs; owns the launch gates.* The full NFR-6(d) E2E suite; **final** render-matrix sign-off (NFR-6(a)) with the NFR-5 axe-core scan across the whole library and the app — a re-confirmation, since every design already cleared both at its category gate, not the first time either is run; **confirmation that NFR-6(c3)'s 30-day rotation has covered every design at least once**; the manual NFR-1 performance gate on the reference laptop; quota alarms; the PITR restore drill; legal pages including the Refund & Cancellation page; the §4 Test→Live cutover migration of suggestions, votes and the dogfood project.

*Exit:* Definition of Done (§4) met.

## Appendix A — Complete Section Inventory (normative)

The full normative inventory — per-category content models, controls, `bindingContext` / `compileTarget` declarations and every design descriptor — lives in **`sections-inventory.md`** (same folder). That file remains **Appendix A** for all cross-references in this PRD and downstream documents (e.g. "Appendix A §34" resolves to its A34). The totals live there and are generated from the design export by `tools/inventory-gen.py`; this document does not restate them (standing rule 3).

**Tiering.** The first two designs in every category are **[Free]** (68), plus **A29 #7** and **A31 #10**, re-tiered so the author-archive binding context and the `private.hbs` compile target each carry a Free option of their own (FR-G2) — **70 in total.** All others are Pro. A30 needs no re-tier: its 13 designs share one `bindingContext` and one `compileTarget`, and #1 and #2 are [Free] under the first-two rule already.

**Placeable sections vs non-placeable treatments.** The split is declared and counted in `sections-inventory.md`. Placeable designs are dragged onto a canvas (FR-D5/FR-D12). The non-placeable treatments are chosen elsewhere, apply once per project, and are excluded from the Section Picker rail, drag/reorder, the Layers panel, Variant Shuffle (FR-D13) and Site Remix (FR-D17). Every requirement quantifying over "every design" — FR-G1's launch deliverables, FR-G5's uniqueness bar, FR-G6 / NFR-6(a)'s render matrix, NFR-5's accessibility scan, E14's gallery — still covers all 28, rendered in the host context named below.

| Category | Count | Chosen where | Host context for the render matrix and the E14 gallery |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A32** Paywall / Content CTA | 12 | Standalone **Paywall Template** editor (FR-H6) — one design active per project | A gated `post.hbs`, rendered at the members-only cutoff via `partials/content-cta.hbs`; member state Anonymous and Free (FR-D16); live or sample tiers |
| **A33** Koenig Card Treatments | 6 | The **card design module** (FR-Q7), or Theme Settings where no Post Content section is placed (FR-Q9) — one treatment active per project | The **style-guide fixture** (FR-H3) rendered on `post.hbs` — a body exercising every Koenig card type, styled by the emitted `cards.css` |
| **A34** Pagination Styles | 10 | Pagination style control on the designated main feed (FR-H2) | A paginated `index.hbs` whose main feed (A17 #1) has more than one page of posts |

**The inventory (mirrored from `sections-inventory.md`, which is normative; on any conflict the inventory wins).** Per-design specifications and per-design control lists live only in the companion — this table is a summary, and a category listed here is not buildable until its design-and-specification session has landed its specs there (§4). **`post.hbs, page.hbs` is one binding context, not two:** the resource is one object reachable under either key, so there is no `page` value in FR-G3's enum — the post/page difference lives in `compileTarget`, which is why comments, post footers and related posts are `post.hbs` only. `bindingContext` and `compileTarget` (FR-G3) are surfaced here because they, not the category, are what Variant Shuffle, Site Remix and the Section Picker partition on — two designs in the same category do not necessarily belong to the same ring.

| A18 | Post Lists | 15 | Ghost Content Sections | `posts` | any |
| A19 | Featured & Spotlight | 15 | Ghost Content Sections | `posts` | any |
| A20 | Tag Collections | 15 | Ghost Content Sections | `tags` (+ `posts` where shown) | any |
| A21 | Author Showcases | 15 | Ghost Content Sections | `authors` (+ `posts` where shown) | any |
| A22 | Newsletter / Subscribe | 16 | Ghost Content Sections | none (+ `@member`; `tiers` on #8) | any |
| A24 | Post Headers | 16 | Template-Specific Sections | `post` (one object; `{{#post}}` emitted on both targets) | `post.hbs`, `page.hbs` — on `page.hbs` the title/feature-image markup carries the `@page.show_title_and_feature_image` guard (FR-I1) |
| A25 | Post Content Layouts | 12 | Template-Specific Sections | `post` (one object; `{{#post}}` emitted on both targets) | `post.hbs`, `page.hbs` |
| A26 | Post Footers | 15 | Template-Specific Sections | `post` | `post.hbs` |
| A27 | Related Posts | 12 | Template-Specific Sections | `post` (+ the `posts` its `{{#get}}` yields) | `post.hbs` |
| A28 | Comments | 10 | Template-Specific Sections | `post` (+ `@site.comments_enabled`) | `post.hbs` |
| A29 | Archive Headers | 14 | Template-Specific Sections | **per design** — `tag`: #1/#3/#4/#5 · `author`: #2/#7/#8/#9 · either: #6/#10–#14 | follows the context — `tag.hbs` / `author.hbs` |
| A30 | Members Pages | 13 | Template-Specific Sections | `tiers` + `@member` | `custom-{name}.hbs` (entry — page-backed, selected from Ghost's Template dropdown; no route — FR-I1) |
| A31 | Error & Utility | 10 | Template-Specific Sections | **per design** — `error` on the error targets · `private` on `private.hbs` · `none` on the utility pages | **per design** — `error.hbs`: #1–#7 and #10 · `private.hbs`: **all ten**, which is why #10's [Free] re-tier is withdrawn (R-17) · `custom-{name}.hbs`: #8 Elsewhere and #9 Directory. **Ids are contiguous 1–10** — the export filled the retired #8 slot with a real design. A `{{#get}}` is refused on both `error.hbs` and `private.hbs` (R-7). |
| A32 | Paywall / Content CTA | 12 | Ghost Native Elements | `post` + `tiers` + `@member` | `partials/content-cta.hbs` |
| A33 | Koenig Card Treatments | 6 | Ghost Native Elements | none | `assets/css` (`cards.css`) |
| A34 | Pagination Styles | 10 | Ghost Native Elements | `pagination` | `partials/pagination.hbs` |
| — | **Total** | **466** | | | |

**Group sums:** Structure & Chrome **45** + Marketing Sections **197** + Ghost Content Sections **94** + Template-Specific Sections **102** + Ghost Native Elements **28** = **466**. Generated by `tools/inventory-gen.py` from the design export — never typed (standing rule 3).

**Universal controls (on every section, outside each category's declared `Controls:` line):** Background role (Swatch Row), Vertical spacing (Compact/Comfortable/Spacious), Top divider (None/Line/Fade) — **exempt from FR-F3's ≈ 15-control cap**, never Quick Controls. A32 carries them (it renders as a block of its own); A33 and A34 carry none. **Member visibility** — show to Everyone / Logged out / Free members / Paid members — sits on every CTA-bearing category (**A2, A6, A22, A26**), adds no designs, and is how a member ask is placed on a canvas now that A32 is non-placeable (FR-D5, FR-H6).

## Appendix B — Ghost Data-Binding Catalog (normative)

What Inflozo may bind, per the Ghost Content API (Posts, Pages, Tags, Authors, Tiers, Settings) and theme template context. **The helper shim (FR-H5) must cover this surface; FR-H5's helper list is the shim's contract and governs on any difference** — this appendix catalogs the *data*, FR-H5 scopes the *shim*.

**`@site` (Settings):** title, description, logo, icon, cover_image, accent_color, url, locale, timezone, navigation[], secondary_navigation[], members_enabled, paid_members_enabled, comments_enabled. (The Content API `/settings/` response exposes the site language as **both `lang` and `locale`** — the same value under two keys. The editor and FR-Q6's language defaulting read `locale`; an implementation that reads `lang` gets the same string, and neither is a fallback for the other.)
**Core helpers that are not Content API resources (and cost nothing).** Four values a design may need come from Ghost's own **keyless, server-side** helpers rather than from the API surface below, which is why the sections that use them spend **zero** of the 20 custom-setting slots and need no key of any kind: **`{{total_members}}`** and **`{{total_paid_members}}`** — ⚠ **both render a *string*: Ghost rounds the number down and appends `+`** ("1,200+"), so a design must render the value verbatim and never format, compare or compute on it — and **`{{content_api_key}}`** / **`{{content_api_url}}`**, which supply the *browser-safe* Content API credentials a client-side design needs — now **A29's filter design** alone, since A23 is deleted (R-24) and A27's shuffle design has no counterpart in the export. All four are verified present in current Ghost source (`research-contested-variants.md`). A reviewer's claim that member counts are Admin-API-only and unavailable in template context was **false**, and was the second platform assertion in this project to fail verification.

**`excerpt` and `custom_excerpt` are different fields, and the difference is a trap.** `custom_excerpt` is what the author wrote; `{{excerpt}}` falls back to **500 characters of the post's plaintext** when no custom excerpt exists, which is a mid-sentence fragment. A design displaying an excerpt as prose is fine with either; a design that **frames** the excerpt — as a pull-quote, a standfirst, a card blurb in quote marks — must bind `custom_excerpt` only and use FR-H8's empty state when it is absent (A19 #11).

**Post/Page:** title, slug, url, excerpt, custom_excerpt, feature_image (+ feature_image_alt, feature_image_caption), published_at, updated_at, reading_time, featured, access, visibility, primary_tag, tags[], primary_author, authors[], comment counts (via `{{comments}}` context).
**Tag:** name, slug, description, feature_image, accent_color, url, count.posts (via `include=count.posts`).
**Author:** name, slug, bio, profile_image, cover_image, website, twitter, facebook, url, count.posts.
**Tier:** name, description, type (free/paid), currency, monthly_price, yearly_price, benefits[], welcome_page_url. `include=monthly_price,yearly_price,benefits` is a **no-op** — the tiers endpoint does not accept `include` at all and the Content API adds those three relations unconditionally — so it is emitted for readability and never relied on. **Tier presence never implies purchasability:** Ghost seeds an active $5/mo paid tier at install, so every paid CTA gates on `@site.paid_members_enabled` and every tier query filters `type:paid+visibility:public` (FR-H6, `research-ghost-membership-pages.md` §6). Prices are integers in the smallest currency unit; tiers return in ascending monthly price and are never paginated.
**`@member` (live-site runtime; editor simulates via FR-D16):** null | { name, email, status: free/paid/comped, paid flag }.
**Pagination context:** page, pages, total, next, prev → drives A34.
**Portal actions (Link Picker → `data-portal`) — the verified set:** `signup` · `signin` · `account` · `account/plans` · `account/profile` · `account/newsletters` · `signup/{tierId}` · `signup/{tierId}/monthly` · `signup/{tierId}/yearly` (a direct Stripe redirect, no modal) · `support` · `recommendations`. **Portal's *floating button* is a site setting, not one of these actions** — it is enabled in Ghost Admin, injected by `{{ghost_head}}`, and no theme markup produces it; Inflozo reads its state at connect and shims it on the canvas (FR-C2, FR-H5). Do not confuse it with a `data-portal` link a design places deliberately. **`share` is a Ghost 6-only Portal page and Inflozo does not use it (ruling R-29, closed by execution — `MEASUREMENTS.md` §29b/c).** Portal 2.69 on Ghost 6.58.0 resolves `#/share` and `#/portal/share` to a real share page; **Portal 2.51 on Ghost 5.130.6 has no such page at all**, so the same link falls through to Portal's default and **opens the sign-in modal** on every Ghost 5 site. Even where it exists it is a **shadow-DOM iframe no theme can style**, offering X · Facebook · LinkedIn · Threads · Bluesky in a fixed order with no Mastodon. So it is enumerated here as a fact about Ghost and is **not** a Link Picker destination: sharing is FR-Q10's own list. **`upgrade` is not a Portal value** — no branch parses it and it falls through to Portal's default screen; the upgrade action is **`account/plans`**. Ghost's own Source theme ships the broken value and it must not be copied. **Signing out is not a Portal action either:** it is `data-members-signout`, a members data-attribute, not a `data-portal` value. The theme's own forms use that same `data-members-*` family — `data-members-form` (`signin` · `signup` · `subscribe`), `data-members-email`, `data-members-name`, `data-members-newsletter`, `data-members-label`, `data-members-error`, `data-members-manage-billing` — which render Inflozo's own markup rather than a Portal modal and expose state as `loading` / `success` / `error` classes on the `<form>`; that is what makes a designed signup or signin form possible at all. **The signin seam:** the form is fully themeable, but `data-members-otc` hands the one-time-code step to a Portal modal Inflozo cannot design, so it is off by default and exposed as a control. The list above is read from Portal's source — Ghost's docs publish only four of these values — and is re-verified against each Ghost minor Inflozo certifies (§7.6, `research-ghost-membership-pages.md` §4).
**`@custom` in generated themes:** the dark-mode built-ins (FR-Q5) plus user-defined settings from the Theme Settings builder (FR-Q2) — Ghost allows 5 setting types and caps themes at 20 settings; Inflozo enforces the cap with a visible meter (P3). **The six contested designs spend none of them** — the capabilities they were thought to need are served by the core helpers above, so the cap is exactly as free after those designs ship as before. That was the question the research was commissioned to settle, and the answer is zero.

**Chrome strings — `{{t}}`, the catalog and `locales/` (FR-Q6).** Visitor-facing labels the compiler generates are **not** part of the data surface above: they are catalog entries, not Ghost resources. Their normative source is **`appendix-h1-string-catalog.md`** — every key, its English default, its placeholder set and its `{{t}}`-vs-`data-i18n-*` emission channel. Binding rules: `.hbs` output consumes them **exclusively** via `{{t}}` with `{placeholder}` hash params; strings written by bundled JS are resolved at compile time and emitted as `data-i18n-*` attributes on their module's mount element, because Ghost never runs `assets/js/main.js` through Handlebars. The theme always ships **`locales/en.json`**, plus the project-language file when the project language differs — both emitted from the one catalog in the same pass, with equal key sets by construction (FR-Q6). `en.json` is the **fallback of record**: Ghost falls back to the `en` *file*, so a theme without one renders raw keys wherever the active locale misses. `credit.*` is a locked namespace — in the catalog, never listed in the Translations surface, never overridable. The canvas resolves `{{t}}` through the same shim path as every other helper (FR-H5), so a string the user overrode reads correctly on canvas before it ships.

**Template context — where a binding is legal (FR-H7).** The catalog above says what *exists*; it does not say where it is *reachable*. The normative Template Context Matrix is **`appendix-b1-template-contexts.md`**, keyed on template × scope × connected Ghost version, and it is what FR-H7 implements against: the editor offers only bindings the matrix marks available, and an invalid binding is made unavailable rather than warned about. This is prevention rather than warning because Ghost compiles without strict mode — an out-of-context binding renders an empty string with no error at build, deploy or runtime, and passes gscan. `research-ghost-binding-contexts.md` is the citation-backed evidence behind that matrix; where the two disagree the research is right and the matrix is a bug.

**Query rules:** all editor reads via Content API with NQL filters (e.g. `tag:slug`, `featured:true`), `include=tags,authors`, `limit`, `order`, pinned `Accept-Version`. `{{#get}}` in shipped themes mirrors the same filters, and — because Handlebars is synchronous while `{{#get}}` resolves asynchronously — a `{{#get}}` filter may reference **only template-level context, never the current render context** (FR-H5, FR-G3): a registry-level constraint on what the library may express. Ghost 6 removed `?limit=all` across its APIs: every list read paginates (max 100 per page) — pagination is required on 6.x and works identically on 5.x.

---

## Appendix C — Control Vocabulary Reference (normative)

| Type | UI | Allowed values | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| *(Design picker — **not** a section control)* | mini-diagram thumbnails | the category's own designs | **This is FR-D19's design picker, not a member of the control vocabulary.** It was previously listed here as "Layout Picker" with no value set, which is what made the doc disagree with itself about whether "layout" meant a design or an arrangement inside one. It is present on every placed section, sits outside every declared `Controls:` list like the universal controls, is never a Quick Control, and is not promotable to a Ghost custom setting — a Ghost Admin select cannot swap a design's markup |
| Segmented | 2–4 labeled/icon segments | named options | e.g. alignment Left/Center |
| Stepper | − n + | small integer ranges | Columns, and **every item count** — ruling R-18 makes an item count **always a number picker, never a fixed enum** ("Three · Four · Six" is not a count). **A design may cap its own maximum, and the panel states the reason**: Post Grids tops out at 24 and says so. A locked value is **drawn disabled with the reason shown**, never hidden. Confirms D6. **One carve-out, stated once (ruling R-30): a count that selects between DRAWN LAYOUTS is a Named Select, not a stepper.** The test is whether every value has a frame somebody has looked at. A12's *Count per row* offers three · four · five because those are the three widths the category is drawn and checked at against a long role and a wrapping name — six across gives 196 px photographs. *How many items to show* stays a stepper; *which of three drawn arrangements* is a named set |
| Toggle | switch | on/off | show/hide anything |
| Named Select | dropdown | word values only | e.g. aspect Natural/Square/Wide |
| Swatch Row | role swatches | Base/Surface/Accent/Contrast/Image | never a raw color picker |
| Image Picker | opens Asset Library | user assets / bound image | per-mode swap capable. **Its focus control carries BOTH axes — Centre · Top · Bottom AND Centre · Left · Right** *(owner's ruling, 2026-09-03)*: a wide photograph cropped into a tall frame loses its sides, and a vertical-only control has nothing to say about which side survives. Raised by A13's 4:5 portrait crop and it reaches every design that crops. **Ghost never sees this** — it is a hint the compiler resolves, which is why it is a control and not a binding |
| Icon Picker | searchable grid | **curated Tabler set** (MIT — D1; the licence text ships in the emitted theme). Includes the nine Ghost social platforms | consistent stroke; **drawn inline, once per use** (ruling R-26) — no sprite file, no icon font. Each icon's markup is written where it appears, inherits `currentColor` with no extra rule, costs no additional request and works with everything switched off. A repeated icon repeats its path data; that cost is measured against FR-J's budget at E4 |
| **Item List** | list with Add · Remove · drag handle | the items of an **authored** array | *Register 44's gap, closed.* Add, remove and reorder the items of a repeating block the USER typed. **Never offered on a Ghost-bound repeat** — posts, tags, authors and tiers have nothing to add and get a count instead (Stepper + FR-H2's Data group); an Add button there would be a lie. **Remove never greys** (R-12) — at the floor it stays active and explains itself. Drawn in `P0-3 Item List Controls`. **Per-item *design* is impossible by construction and that is deliberate:** AD-3 writes a control's value onto the **section root**, so a design control necessarily applies to every item at once. Items therefore share one design and differ only in content — the owner's requirement, already guaranteed by the architecture rather than by a rule anyone has to remember |
| Date Picker | calendar popover | a date (site timezone) | countdowns, scheduled content — the value the `countdown` module reads, and the `<time datetime>` it degrades to with JS off (FR-G7) |
| Link Picker | typed search sheet | internal pages/posts/tags/authors · portal actions · URL · email | Ghost-aware (FR-F6) |
| Member Visibility | dropdown | Everyone / Logged out / Free members / Paid members | on every CTA-bearing category (A2, A6, A22, A26); compiles to a **server-side** `{{#if @member}}` and ships **no JavaScript** — client-side gating would be slower and a flash-of-wrong-content bug (FR-G7). Previewed via FR-D16 (FR-D5) |
| Text Field/Area | inline + sidebar | text + inline marks (bold/italic/underline/link), per-prop limits | floating selection toolbar on canvas (FR-D4). **Plain-text lock:** while the prop is bound to a Ghost Admin text setting, marks are stripped and inline formatting is disabled for as long as the binding exists — Ghost Admin text settings are plain strings (FR-Q3). Removing the binding re-enables formatting |

**The Remove button on an item list never greys out** (ruling R-12). At the minimum item count the
control stays **visible and fully active**; clicking it produces the floor and **the reason as one
sentence of text under the list** — "a set needs at least two". A disabled Remove teaches nothing: the
user cannot tell a floor from a bug. This is the reference case the rule below points at, and it is the
one deliberate exception to it.

**A minimum-width rule measures the TEXT COLUMN, not the cell that holds it** *(owner's ruling, 2026-09-02)*. A floor exists so a reader can read, and what they read is the column the words sit in — after the cell's own padding and insets come off. A design whose inner column falls under a floor therefore **greys the value it cannot honour, with the reason**, rather than offering a choice it will not draw. Stated once here because the arithmetic recurs: A17's 306 px excerpt floor is missed by three designs at their widest settings — 4 Cards, 6 Split Head and 17 Panel, at 270–290 px inner, each on a grid it invented — and measuring the outer width instead would have hidden all three. **The floor is exclusive, and the first statement of this rule got that wrong** *(corrected 2026-09-03, on the owner's ruling, after A17's session caught it)*: three lines needs a column **above** 306 px, so **at exactly 306 three lines is already refused and falls to two** — which is what A17's own shared floor and its disabled-control table had said all along, twice. The original example sentence implied 306 was sufficient. It was not, and the category was right.

**Greying has no "but this is a mode" exception** *(owner's ruling, 2026-09-03)*. Two shared editor panels HID controls where this rule greys them — the Data group's Count and Order vanish at Hand-picked rather than greying with *"the list you picked is the count"* — and two categories flagged the clash rather than copying it. **The shared panels change to match the rule, and every category inherits that.** The argument for hiding was that a control belonging to a mode you are not in is different in kind from one switched off; the owner refused the distinction, because a user cannot see which of the two they are looking at and a vanished control teaches nothing either way.

**Greyed or not offered at all — the test is whether the control could EVER do anything in this design** *(architect's ruling, 2026-09-03, generalising what had been a single named exception)*. **Could-never → it is not drawn, and the panel says why.** The visitor dark-mode switch under a pinned colour scheme was the first case and is no longer the only one: Image focus on a design that places a photograph at its own shape crops nothing, so focus can never have work to do there. **Could-but-is-not-now → greyed, with the reason.** The difference is structural versus stateful, and it is a property of the design rather than of the moment. **An EMPTY LIST is neither** — a picker with nothing picked yet is not a control something switched off, and it is drawn as the empty list it is.

**A greyed control whose value in force is not one of its own values names that value in its reason, and leaves no value selected** *(architect's ruling, 2026-09-03, ratifying the drawing P0 made provisionally)*. Order at Hand-picked is the case: the order in force is the one the user dragged, which is neither Newest nor Oldest. The alternatives are worse — inventing a third value the control does not have, or leaving the mark on a value that is not in force, which the shared control rules already forbid. So: no value marked, and the reason sentence carries it — *"the list you picked is the order — these posts render in the order you dragged them."*

**A control disabled by another is greyed, with the reason shown** (ruling R-33) — never hidden, never
silently ignored. The dependency is declared in the control schema (FR-F7), so the sidebar, the validator
and the compiler read one source; the reason is a sentence at the control, not a tooltip. Example:
Features' *Icon in accent* spends the accent, so the Action button below the set greys with *"not
available while the media uses the accent colour."* Same shape as R-12's Remove button. **Not offered at
all** is a different case and is reserved for a control the project can never use — FR-D7's visitor
`mode-toggle` under a pinned `color_scheme` (ruling R-34) — where the panel says why instead.

**Three universal controls sit on every section** — **Background role** (Swatch Row), **Vertical spacing** (Compact/Comfortable/Spacious) and **Top divider** (None/Line/Fade). They are declared once in `sections-inventory.md` rather than per category, are **exempt from FR-F3's ≈ 15-control cap** (which governs a category's own controls, so the effective ceiling is ≈ 15 + 3), and are never Quick Controls. Non-placeable treatments carry them only where they render as a block of their own (A32); A33 and A34 carry none.

**One control name means one set of values, library-wide** *(owner's ruling, 2026-09-03)*. A17 and A18 found `rowDensity` carrying **five** different value sets across one category, `thumbSize` three, and `order` two shapes — so a user who learns a control in one design meets a different control wearing the same name in the next. That is the failure the closed-vocabulary rule below already prevents for *scales*, generalised: **a name is a promise about behaviour, not a label for whatever a design happened to need.** Where two designs genuinely differ, they differ by name. Some designs lose a value they had; a user who learns a control once is worth more than a design keeping its private variant.

**Scale vocabularies are closed, and what may not be borrowed is the row, not the word** (ruling R-13,
narrowing D7). No surface invents a fourth value. **A distinct row *title* is required; the three *words*
may be shared** — "Card padding: Compact · Comfortable · Spacious" is legal, because the title says what it
affects and the words mean the same thing everywhere. An editor should learn one vocabulary, not one per
category. D7's "rename genuinely different ladders" therefore binds the **row**, and the gap names settle on
**Tight · Normal · Loose** library-wide (ruling R-14, reversing D26) — which is what this table already said,
so it does not move; the eleven specs on Tight/Standard/Wide are what change. Every named scale in the
product:

| Scale | Level | Values |
|---|---|---|
| Vertical spacing | section (universal) | Compact / Comfortable / Spacious |
| Spacing density | Style Pack | Compact / Comfortable / Airy |
| Site width | Style Pack | Narrow / Normal / Wide |
| **Gutters** | Style Pack | **Tight / Normal / Loose** |
| Gallery `gap` (A14) | section | Tight / Normal / Loose |
| Radius | Style Pack | Sharp / Soft / Round |
| Button style | Style Pack | Solid / Soft / Outline / Pill |
| Shadow | Style Pack | None / Subtle / Lifted |
| Link style | Style Pack | Underline / Accent |

Gutters deliberately share A14's `gap` labels and deliberately do **not** use "Wide": that value belongs to site width, and one token reading "Wide" while another also reads "Wide" is how a Style Pack editor ships two controls a user cannot tell apart.

Forbidden anywhere at section level: pixel/rem/percent inputs, hex/RGB pickers, font pickers, custom CSS, class names. These live only in the Style Pack editor (fonts/colors) — and nowhere else. **The vocabulary is disciplined, not closed** (FR-F1, §1.4 #4): new simple, named-value control types may join when sections need them (Date Picker and Member Visibility have already joined), but no control ever accepts a raw value — the discipline is on the *values*, which is what makes "impossible to make ugly" hold. Because every control's value set is closed and enumerable, control values reach CSS as data-attribute selectors rather than per-instance styles (§7.3). A slider with named ticks is sanctioned for app surfaces only — the Routes Manager, Theme Settings, and the Style Pack editor, where it sits alongside their date pickers and color popover — never as a section-level control. The hex-paste color popover remains exclusive to the Style Pack editor (P2).

---

## Appendix D — Style Packs (12 presets, paired light + dark) & the Font Pairing Pool (30)

Each pack = full token set (FR-E1). Fonts come from the curated 30-pairing pool below; packs 1–12 use pairings 1–12 respectively.

| Pack | Vibe | Heading / Body | Pairing |
|---|---|---|---|
| Paper | warm minimal default; ivory, soft ink | Fraunces / Inter | D1 |
| Ink | high-contrast editorial, B/W + one red | Libre Caslon Text / Source Serif 4 | D2 |
| Orbit | indigo tech, cool surfaces | Space Grotesk / Inter | D3 |
| Tangerine | playful coral + cream | Bricolage Grotesque / Inter | D4 |
| Slate | composed corporate blue-gray | Instrument Sans / Inter | D5 |
| Meadow | organic greens, rounded warmth | Gantari / Nunito Sans | D6 |
| Dune | sand + terracotta, sun-washed | DM Serif Display / DM Sans | D7 |
| Mono | brutalist grayscale, sharp radii | Archivo / IBM Plex Mono | D8 |
| Ocean | deep teal, airy light mode | Sora / Inter | D9 |
| Berry | plum + blush, magazine-feminine | Playfair Display / Karla | D10 |
| Neon | dark-first electric (light mode derived) | Unbounded / Inter | D11 |
| Quiet | near-monochrome literary calm | Newsreader / Newsreader | D12 |

Dark palettes are hand-paired (not algorithmic inversions): backgrounds deepen, surfaces lift, accents re-tuned for contrast, imagery scrims adjusted. **FR-Q5's fallback chains do not weaken this.** They resolve *unset owner overrides* — a site owner who never picked a dark accent gets the pack's hand-paired one, and only a site owner who never picked *anything* falls all the way back to the light value. The pack always supplies a designed dark palette; the fallback exists for the Ghost Admin fields on top of it. Packs must pass AA on every token pairing used by the library.

### D.0 Every token is computed or authored, and this appendix says which (normative — ruling R-32)

A pack declares far fewer values than the library consumes. The gap is closed by **computing** what
follows from what the pack already declared, and **asking** only for what is genuine taste. There is no
third state: a token that is neither is a token twelve packs will each answer differently.

| Token | Source | How |
|---|---|---|
| background, surface, text, muted text, border, accent, on-accent (×2 modes) | **authored** | the pack's identity; hand-paired per mode (FR-E1) |
| **on-contrast text** | **computed** | the readable pair for the contrast ground, from that ground |
| **accent-on-contrast** | **computed** | the accent re-tuned against the contrast ground to hold AA |
| **dark elevation** / **dark hover-surface** | **computed** | stepped from the dark surface token |
| **negative** / **plate** | **computed** | derived from the pack's own surface and border |
| **tabular figures** | **computed** | a font feature of the declared body family, not a colour choice |
| **drop-cap ratio** | **computed** | from the body face's cap height and the heading scale |
| **scrim strength** | **authored** | genuine judgement — how hard imagery is dimmed is the pack's taste |
| **pill radius** | **authored** | genuine judgement — it reads with the radius scale but is not implied by it |

That is **two authored decisions per pack** beyond the palette, against the twelve the reconciliation
found ~20 categories asking for. A new token enters this table marked computed or authored **in the
same commit that introduces it** (AD-30 owns the mechanism).

### D.a Emission rules (normative — these fix the bundle size, not the table)

FR-E1 requires every pairing to declare its two families, the **weights shipped**, and whether each family ships as a **variable** or a **static** face, because all three determine `@font-face` emission and theme weight. The rules below make those declarations mechanical rather than per-pairing judgment.

1. **Heading role ships roman only.** No design in the library sets an italic heading, so a heading italic face is never emitted. A **variable** heading family emits **one** file with its `wght` axis clipped to the declared range; a **static** heading family emits one file per declared weight (the pool declares one heading weight for every static heading).
2. **Body role ships roman + italic.** FR-D4's inline marks include italic, so a body family without a true italic cannot hold the body role — synthetic obliques are not acceptable output. A **variable** body family emits **two** files (roman + italic), each clipped to `wght 400–700`. A **static** body family emits **four** (400, 700, 400 italic, 700 italic).
3. **Same family in both roles** emits the body's two files only, provided the clipped range covers the heading weights. D12, D19 and D22 are the pool's two-file pairings.
4. **Non-`wght` axes are pinned at their design defaults** at instancing time (`opsz`, `wdth`, `SOFT`, `WONK`, `YTLC`), so a variable face never carries an axis the token system cannot drive.
5. **Ceiling: ≤ 5 font files and ≤ 200 KB of font payload per compiled theme** for a latin-script project. This is Appendix D's budget; NFR-2 asserts only that every shipped font is subset, preloaded and `woff2`-only, and FR-J3's theme-size budget is what enforces the total.
6. **Self-hosted, always.** The compiler bundles the woff2 files into `assets/fonts/`, emits `@font-face` with `font-display: swap`, and preloads the two roman faces. **No generated theme makes a request to `fonts.googleapis.com` or `fonts.gstatic.com`** — hotlinking is a documented GDPR liability for EU site owners and a runtime third-party dependency P5 forbids (FR-J3).
7. **Licensing.** Every family in the pool ships under the SIL Open Font License or Apache 2.0, both of which permit redistribution inside the theme zip the user downloads and installs. A family whose license does not permit that redistribution may not enter the pool.

### D.b Subsetting (normative)

**Content-driven subsetting is wrong here and must not be implemented.** A generated theme renders posts written *after* it was deployed — text the compiler has never seen. A subset built from the glyphs present at compile time renders every later post's unseen characters as tofu on the live site, and the failure appears weeks after deploy with no error anywhere in the pipeline.

**The workable answer is static subsetting by script range.** Default emission is **`latin` + `latin-ext`**, as two `@font-face` blocks per face carrying their `unicode-range`, so the browser fetches `latin-ext` only on pages that need it. Indicative sizes for a clipped variable woff2: `latin` ≈ 15–30 KB, `latin-ext` ≈ +8–15 KB, Cyrillic ≈ +10–20 KB, Greek ≈ +5–10 KB, Vietnamese ≈ +5–10 KB.

**Reconciliation with FR-Q6's "any language".** FR-Q6 lets a user translate the chrome catalog into any language and stores the language on the project. That promise and a latin subset collide, and the resolution is stated rather than left to discovery:

- **Latin-script project languages** ship `latin` + `latin-ext`, per the default above.
- **Non-latin project languages ship the full face** for the covering script — no Inflozo-side subsetting at all — because there is no content-independent way to narrow it. The size consequence is real and surfaced, not absorbed: a Cyrillic or Greek face runs roughly 1.5–2× a latin subset, and a **CJK face is 1–5 MB per weight** with no subsetting the compiler can perform to make it small. FR-J3's pre-deploy theme-size budget is where that lands, and a CJK project is told at the language selector that font payload will dominate the theme zip.
- **Coverage is checked, not assumed.** If the selected pairing's families do not cover the project language's script, the compiler falls back to the **system font stack** for that script and the language selector says so at the moment of selection — a theme never ships a `@font-face` that cannot render its own chrome strings.
- **RTL scripts remain blocked by Appendix G's deferral**, which the FR-Q6 acknowledgement gate enforces before the font question is ever reached.

**These rules bind the canvas too, not only the emitted theme.** The editor loads **the same subsets, from the same self-hosted files, with the same `unicode-range` splits and the same variable-axis instances** the compiler will ship — never a full face and never a webfont CDN. Loading a full face while designing and a subset after deploy is a fidelity defect with a long fuse: it looks identical until a glyph outside the subset appears, and it makes metrics differ wherever a variable axis is pinned differently on the two sides (the default pack's `opsz` is the live example). It also removes a permanent false-positive class from NFR-6(c3)'s nightly comparison, which would otherwise diff canvas-rendered text against theme-rendered text on every run. Each of FR-E1's ~30 pairings therefore carries a canvas-side subset alongside its theme-side one, built by the same step.

### D.c The pool — 30 pairings

`V` = variable face (axis range as clipped) · `S` = static face (weights as listed). "Files" is the count emitted per D.a.

| # | Pairing | Heading family | Heading face · weights | Body family | Body face · weights | Files |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## Appendix E — Starter Templates (10)

Full projects: Home + Post + Page + Tag + Author + 404 designed; pack pre-selected; Orbit Weekly mapped. No starter ships a membership template: membership pages are opt-in `custom-{name}.hbs` files (FR-O1, FR-I1), so Ghost's Portal serves those flows, no `routes.yaml` is emitted, and a starter's first deploy needs no step in Ghost Admin beyond the theme upload itself.

1. **Aurora** — newsletter-first personal brand (Tangerine): Signup Hero, Issue Preview, Featured Split, Post List Editorial Rules, big-ask paywall.
2. **Gazette** — magazine (Ink): Magazine Cover hero, Magazine Mixed grid, Topic Tabs, masthead authors, serif post templates.
3. **Signal** — tech blog (Orbit): Split Editorial hero, Bento grid, TOC-right posts, command-palette search, stats band.
4. **Foundry** — startup blog + marketing home (Slate): Center Stage hero, Features Bento, Logo Wall, Pricing Toggle Cards, CTA band.
5. **Quiet** — ultra-minimal writer (Quiet): Center Stage hero (A4 #1), Editorial Rules list (A18 #1), Narrow Classic reading (A25 #1), Author Bio Card close (A26 #1) — all [Free] (FR-O4).
6. **Pulse** — podcast (Neon): Poster Modal hero, Episode List, platform CTA band, guest (author) showcases.
7. **Bloom** — lifestyle/food (Meadow): Collage hero, Photo Square grid, Galleries Masonry, recipe-friendly Docs-style posts.
8. **Chapter** — author/book site (Berry): Portrait Intro hero, book (page) spotlight, testimonial wall, mailing-list close.
9. **Ledger** — business/finance publication (Paper): Split Editorial hero (A4 #2), Classic Cards grid (A17 #1), Big Number Row stats (A10 #1), Hard Stop Card paywall (A32 #2) — all [Free] (FR-O4).
10. **Studio** — portfolio + blog (Mono): Offset Card hero, Bento gallery, About Founder Letter, project (tag) collections.

Tier and paywall behavior in these starters (A7 live pricing, A32 designs, A22 portal actions — FR-H6) is accepted on **T1–T3 only**: Ghost(Pro) Starter has no paid subscriptions, so T4 can neither create tiers nor gate a post, and its acceptance scope is the Preview-only block path and nothing else (§4, Appendix F).

## Appendix F — Plans, Limits & Unit Economics

### F.1 Plan matrix

This table is the **sole definition of Free/Pro gating** (§5). A capability with no row here is available on both plans.

**Three states, two purchasable plans** *(Round 3, decision D7)*. `entitlements.state` has three values — `free`, `pro_active`, `pro_past_due` — and this table defined caps for two, leaving the 7-day grace window undefined in the one place AD-28 names as the resolver's **sole** data source. That is verbatim the divergence AD-28 exists to prevent ("they will disagree … on `pro_past_due`"). The third column is added below. **It is not a plan and is never shown as one:** FR-N1's public Pricing page lists Free and Pro, because those are the only two anyone can buy. `pro_past_due` is a temporary state of a Pro subscription, and what the customer sees is FR-L2's banner, not a different plan.

**The rule is simple, and deliberately generous:** during grace a past-due customer keeps **every** Pro capability. FR-L2's transitions already say so — `pro_active → pro_past_due` carries a banner, and only `pro_past_due → free` on grace expiry downgrades. Withdrawing capability the moment a card bounces would be the retention dark pattern F.2 forbids, and it would strand a customer mid-deploy over a bank decline. Where the third column reads "= Pro" it means exactly Pro, with no per-row exception.

| | Free | Pro — $15/mo · $150/yr | Pro, past due *(internal — 7-day grace, FR-L2)* |
|---|---|---|---|
| Projects | 1 | 25 | = Pro |
| Site connections | 1 | 10 | = Pro |
| Section library | canvas: the whole library · deploy/export: **[Free] designs only** — the first two of every category (R-17) | The whole library | = Pro |
| Asset storage | 100 MB | 5 GB | = Pro |
| Per-upload cap | 10 MB (stored optimized) | 10 MB | = Pro |
| Deploy history / rollback | last 3 | last 10 per project | = Pro |
| Theme ZIP export | ✓ (with credits, Free designs only) | ✓ | = Pro |
| Credit removal (white-label) | — | ✓ | = Pro |
| Dark mode authoring · Routes Manager · Style Pack editing | ✓ | ✓ | = Pro |
| **Theme Settings** (`posts_per_page`, custom-settings builder — FR-Q1/Q2) | ✓ | ✓ | = Pro |
| **Translations** (chrome-string overrides, locale file — FR-Q6) | ✓ | ✓ — the `credit.*` namespace is locked on **both** plans, which is what keeps Translations ungated without defeating FR-J15's white-label | = Pro |
| **Compatibility watch** (per-release broadcast — FR-C5) | ✓ | ✓ — not Pro-gated; the risk is not plan-dependent | = Pro |
| **Paywall Template editor** (A32 designs — FR-H6) | ✓ (design freely; A32 #1–#2 are [Free], the other 10 gate at the exits) | ✓ | = Pro |
| **Post Content section** (A25 — design picker, measure, TOC, drop cap, share rail; FR-D19, Appendix A §25) | ✓ (design freely; A25 #1–#2 are [Free], the other 10 gate at the exits) | ✓ | = Pro |
| **Ghost card design module** (FR-Q7 — every Koenig card, `cards.css` emission) | ✓ | ✓ — the cards a post author writes are Ghost's, and shipping them unstyled on a Free plan would degrade the reading experience Inflozo exists to improve | = Pro |
| **Koenig card treatments** (A33 — the 6 starting points) | ✓ (design freely; A33 #1–#2 are [Free], the other 4 gate at the exits, remediable by reverting — FR-L3) | ✓ | = Pro |
| **Pagination styles** (A34 — the 10 treatments) | ✓ (A34 #1–#2 are [Free], the other 8 gate at the exits, remediable by reverting — FR-L3) | ✓ | = Pro |
| Variant Shuffle · Site Remix · Member-state preview · Preview subject (FR-D22) | ✓ | ✓ | = Pro |
| Pre-Inflozo snapshot, restore and rollback (FR-J13, FR-J9) | ✓ — safety operations survive downgrade (FR-L3) | ✓ | = Pro — and exempt regardless, per AD-28 |
