| Starter templates | ✓ — Quiet and Ledger are Free end-to-end (FR-O4); the other 8 contain Pro designs | ✓ | = Pro |

### F.2 Billing policy (owner)

**No refunds. No mid-cycle cancellation.** Cancelling stops auto-renew; access continues to the end of the paid period and then downgrades to Free, entering FR-L3's block-until-resolved flow — the user deletes over-limit projects, connections and assets themselves, and **Inflozo never auto-deletes anything, on any path.** Cancellation always completes in ≤ 3 clicks (G10's counter-metric); there are no retention dark patterns.

Two consequences are load-bearing and are not optional implementation detail:

- **Checkout collects explicit consent to immediate delivery of the digital service and an explicit waiver of the statutory withdrawal right**, recorded with the order (FR-L1). Without that record the no-refund policy is not lawfully enforceable in EU/UK markets, and the public statement of the terms is FR-N1's Refund & Cancellation page. Whether Dodo, as merchant of record, already collects this is a verify-at-build item.
- **A dispute-opened transition exists regardless of the policy.** Chargebacks happen whether or not refunds are offered, so FR-L2 moves `pro_active → free` **immediately** on a dispute rather than at period end — a disputed account must never sit `pro_active` with a full term remaining. G9's counter-metric (refund/dispute rate < 2%) is measured on this transition.

### F.3 Cost basis

> **Read this first.** **The dominant cost of this product is authorship, not infrastructure.** This appendix prices the infrastructure well and the library not at all. F.3a states the build cost that F.3b's monthly lines do not contain, and F.5 therefore reports **two** break-evens — a cash figure that says when the servers pay for themselves, and a payback figure that says when the whole endeavour is above water. Reporting only the first, as earlier versions did, flattered the picture by an order of magnitude.

#### F.3a Build cost (one-time, and it dominates)

The library is designed and specified before it is built (§4), and every design is carried through a category owner gate. That work is the product's real cost basis.

| Input | Quantity | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Design frames | **≈1,500–2,000** | per design: desktop, mobile, tablet where it differs, dark mode, and its control panel — plus per category a tokenisation proof, a stress frame and the shared field list (`design/claude-design-prompt-3-library.md`) |
| Designs specified | the full inventory | content fields, per-design control sets, binding, responsive rule, empty states — produced in the **same pass** as the design, not a second pass |
| Category owner gates | one per category | each blocking, each including a real-Ghost deploy and the owner's hands-on review (§4) |

**[ASSUMPTION]** **This is owner time, not cash.** No line above is booked as a cash cost, because the owner performs the design and the gating. The cash-equivalent is stated only to size the commitment honestly: at a nominal **$25/frame** the design pass alone represents **$37,500–50,000** — between 278 and 370 months of the entire recurring cost below. **[ASSUMPTION]** F.5's payback figure amortises it over **24 months**. Both the rate and the window are assumptions with no evidence behind them; they are declared so a reader can substitute their own rather than inherit mine.

The consequence that matters is not the money. It is that **the pre-revenue period is deliberately unbounded** (§4: quality outranks schedule, and launch moves before scope does), so F.3b's recurring cost runs for an unknown number of months with no revenue against it. **The runway question is how many months of F.3b the owner can fund, not how many subscribers repay it.**

#### F.3b Run cost (recurring, permanent, with Test as its own line)

| Line | Monthly | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Supabase Pro (organization) | $25 | includes 100 GB storage (then ≈ $0.021/GB), 250 GB egress + 250 GB cached egress (then $0.09 / $0.03 per GB). **Allowances are org-wide**, so Test's storage displaces Live's rather than adding to it |
| Vercel Pro (team) | $20 | priced per seat. **The $20 usage credit is team-level**, so Live and Test draw on **one shared pool** — a second environment is free in *seats* and not free in *usage*, which §7.1's long-running-compile posture makes material |
| Resend | $0–20 | free tier covers launch send volume; Pro tier past ~3k sends/month |
| Sentry | $0 | dev tier at launch |
| **CI (GitHub Actions + artifact storage)** | **≈$35–80** | ⚠ **the second-largest line, and the only one that scales with library size rather than customers.** Vercel provides no general-purpose runners, so this is real spend, not an existing lane. Driven by NFR-6(a)'s cadence: the stated nightly-plus-changed-only cadence lands in this band; a per-commit full matrix would take it to **$140–315/mo**. FR-J14's post-GA library drops grow it **every month, permanently, with no offsetting revenue mechanism** |
| **T1** — self-hosted Ghost 6.x droplet | ≈ $9 | owner-provided (§4) |
| **T2** — Ghost(Pro) **Publisher** | **$29** | list, billed yearly; monthly billing is higher |
| **T3** — self-hosted Ghost 5.x droplet | ≈ $9 | **permanent** — the only thing testing NFR-7's public 5.x claim, and a second droplet is its entire cost (§4) |
| **T4** — Ghost(Pro) **Starter** | **$15** yearly / **$18** monthly | **deferred until after MVP** (§4). Not incurred until the pre-launch Ghost(Pro) gate is taken |
| **Baseline subtotal** | **≈$107 at launch · ≈$120 at modelled volume (band $107–130)**. **During the pre-launch build period the floor is $15 lower — ≈$92** — because T4 is not incurred until §4's Ghost(Pro) gate is taken, and that gate falls *before* public launch, so T4 is inside the launch figure and outside the build-period one. The build period is deliberately unbounded (§4), which makes this the band the runway is actually spent at | the launch figure books no Resend cost, because the Resend row's own note says the free tier covers launch volume |
| **Test environment (permanent — §4)** | **≈ +$10–15** | the Ghost fleet moves wholesale into Test rather than being bought twice, and Vercel is per-seat, so only Supabase duplicates — at the additional-project compute rate on the same organization, not a second $25 organization |
| **Total permanent fixed** | **≈$135–190/mo (band $117–210)** | the spread is almost entirely CI |

**PITR** (NFR-4) is included in Supabase Pro's plan at the retention NFR-4 requires; no separate line. **[NOTE FOR PM]** confirm the retention window matches NFR-4 before launch — a longer window is a paid add-on.

**Variable cost is genuinely small, but the margin claim was wrong.** Deployed sites consume no Inflozo egress (P5); editor asset traffic is CDN-cached; snapshot and deploy artifacts and notification rows are storage-cheap; thumbnail capture ships as static placeholders in v1 (FR-B1), so no headless-browser service exists to cost. But the retired ">95% infra margin" claim did not survive checking: **fully loaded, a Pro subscriber costs ≈$0.79/mo against $12.61 net → ≈93.7% margin**, and the per-account and marginal figures both sit *below* the old "$0.15–0.60" floor. The margin is excellent; the specific numbers were not.

### F.4 Net revenue per Pro customer

Dodo's schedule is 4% + 40¢, plus 1.5% international, plus 0.5% subscription.

| | Domestic | International | Midpoint | Per month |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Monthly $15 | $13.93 | $13.70 | $13.81 | **$13.81** |
| Yearly $150 | $142.85 | $140.60 | $141.73 | **$11.81** |

**The stated plan-mix assumption is 60% yearly / 40% monthly** (FR-L1 pre-selects yearly), giving a **blended net ≈ $12.61/mo per Pro customer** — 8.7% below the $13.81 a monthly-only model would assume.

**[ASSUMPTION] The midpoint assumes a 50/50 domestic/international split**, which is declared nowhere else and rests on no data. A fully international book nets **$12.50** blended — 0.9% below the stated figure, so the assumption is immaterial to every conclusion drawn from it, but it should not sit undeclared in a table read as fact.

**Sensitivity: the mix is not this model's dominant uncertainty.** The full range from 100% monthly to 100% yearly is $11.81–$13.81, **±8% about its midpoint** (−6% / +10% against the 60/40 blend). A mix assumption that is wrong by 20 points moves blended net by about 40¢. The uncosted variables that actually move the model are churn (F.6) and the Test environment (F.3), not the mix. **Revisit after 3 months of real checkout data.**

### F.5 Break-even — two numbers, because one was misleading

**G7's historical figure answered a question nobody asked.** "Break-even at 9–10 subscribers" priced servers against revenue while ignoring both the CI lane and the entire cost of building the library. Two figures replace it.

**(1) Cash break-even — when the recurring bill is covered.** F.3b ÷ $12.61 blended net:

| Run cost | Subscribers |
|---|---|
| $135/mo (CI at the low end) | 10.7 → **≈11** |
| $163/mo (midpoint) | 12.9 → **≈13** |
| $190/mo (CI at the high end) | 15.1 → **≈15** |

**≈11–15 Pro subscribers**, and the spread is **almost entirely CI cadence** — the single decision (NFR-6(a)'s cadence, §4) that moves this number more than every other correction combined. The earlier "9–10" was computed on a baseline that excluded the permanent Test environment; since Test is permanent from the first customer and break-even requires at least one customer, **the state that figure described cannot exist**.

**(2) Payback — when the build is repaid too.** F.3b plus F.3a amortised over 24 months, ÷ $12.61:

- 1,750 frames at $25 → $43,750 ÷ 24 = $1,823/mo + $190 run = $2,013 ÷ $12.61 → **≈160**
- 2,000 frames at $25 → $50,000 ÷ 24 = $2,083/mo + $215 run = $2,298 ÷ $12.61 → **≈182**

**≈160–180 Pro subscribers.** **[ASSUMPTION]** Entirely governed by F.3a's declared frame rate and amortisation window; substitute your own and this figure moves proportionally. It is **an order of magnitude above the cash figure**, and that gap — not either number alone — is the honest description of this business.

**Which number to use.** Cash break-even is the operational one: below it, the product costs money every month it exists. Payback is the strategic one: it is where the time spent building the library is recovered. **Since the owner performs the build, payback is opportunity cost rather than cash out**, so it does not threaten solvency — but it does answer "was this worth building", and that question deserves a number rather than silence.

### F.6 Churn and blended LTV

The no-lock-in stance is deliberate — themes work forever, export stays open — so an **export-and-cancel cohort is expected**, and it is modeled here rather than left as an instruction to model it.

**Stated assumptions:** **25% month-1 churn**, **4% steady-state monthly churn from month 2**. G10 targets steady-state churn < 5% **measured from month 2**; the month-1 spike is this appendix's number and is not counted against that target.

The two cohorts behave differently, and the billing policy (F.2) is the reason:

| Cohort | Mix | Tenure model | Expected tenure | Net/mo | LTV |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Monthly | 40% | 1 month paid, then 75% continue into a 4%/mo geometric (1 ÷ 0.04 = 25 further months) | 19.75 months | $13.81 | **≈ $273** |
| Yearly | 60% | The first 12 months are **banked** — no refunds and no mid-cycle cancellation (F.2), so a month-1 export-and-cancel is a stopped renewal, not a lost term. The spike applies at the **first renewal**; survivors then renew at 0.96¹² = 61.3%/yr | 2.94 terms ≈ 35.2 months | $11.81 | **≈ $416** |
| **Blended** | | | | **$12.61** | **≈ $359** |

Stated gross of acquisition cost — no paid-acquisition line exists in this model. **The 4% steady-state assumption is the single largest lever here**: it implies a 25-month average life for a monthly subscriber, and it is a founder estimate, not an observation. A 6% steady-state cuts blended LTV to roughly $280.

The offsetting retention levers are all value-based: post-launch library cadence (FR-J14), the per-release compatibility broadcast (FR-C5), and yearly-default checkout (FR-L1) — the last of which is exactly what makes the yearly cohort's LTV 1.5× the monthly cohort's despite netting less per month.

> **19.75 is correct — do not "fix" it to 19.0.** A review proposed that the monthly tenure double-counts month 1. It does not: `1/p = 25` counts months *within the geometric phase*, and that phase begins at month 2, so `1 + 0.75 × 25 = 19.75` is the intended and internally consistent convention. Three independent checks confirm it — the yearly row uses the identical convention and reproduces 2.94 exactly; the 6%-churn sensitivity reproduces at ≈$281 under this convention and ≈$213 under the proposed one; and F.6's own stated 25-month phase life agrees. **Applying the proposed correction would break the only table in this appendix that reconciles end to end.** Recorded here because the error is plausible enough to be re-proposed.

### F.7 Capacity-headroom scenario (not a revenue projection)

The 1,000 Free + 200 Pro shape is a **capacity-headroom scenario**: it answers "does the infrastructure hold at that population?", and nothing else. It is **not** a forecast — 200 of 1,200 is a **16.7% paid share against G9's ≥ 3% target**, more than five times the goal, and reading it as revenue would overstate the plan by the same factor.

- **As capacity:** ≈ 1,200 accounts consume ≈ $140–190/mo of infrastructure. ⚠ **The old "comfortably inside Supabase Pro's included storage" claim was wrong by ~11×** — the scenario implies ≈1,100 GB against a 100 GB allowance. **The dollar band survives anyway**, because overage prices at $0.021/GB ≈ **$21/mo**, giving ≈$156 total — still inside the band. Both halves matter: the *storage headroom* claim is retired, and the *cost* conclusion stands. Do not restate the infrastructure figure at ten times its value on the strength of the first half.
- **Unmodeled and Inflozo-borne by design:** deploy artifacts (200 × 25 × 10 plus 1,000 × 1 × 3 ≈ **53,000 objects** at 0.5–2 MB → 26–106 GB) and pre-deploy snapshots (≈3,000 at ~3 MB ≈ 9 GB), together ≈**$0.7–2.4/mo** in overage (FR-J7, FR-J13). Small, but previously absent rather than judged small.
- **Egress is uncomputed.** 250 GB plus 250 GB cached are included, then $0.09/$0.03 per GB. Editor asset traffic for ≈1,200 accounts has **no stated quota bounding it**, so there is no ceiling to check the scenario against. **[NOTE FOR PM]** either bound it with a quota or compute it before this scenario is relied upon.
- **At G9's actual target:** ⚠ **this line is not merely optimistic, it is undefined as written.** G9 is a *conversion rate within a cohort's first 90 days*; "1,000 Free implies ≈31 Pro" applies it as a *standing population ratio*, which are different quantities. Steady-state Pro stock is `(0.03 × monthly signups) ÷ 0.04 = 0.75 × monthly signups`, while Free accumulates every non-converting signup that never churns — so **without a Free-churn assumption the denominator grows without bound and the paid share tends to zero**. **[ASSUMPTION — MISSING]** state a Free-churn rate, or delete the ratio. Until then no runway number should be planned against this line.
- **At the scenario's shape:** 200 Pro × $12.61 = ≈ **$2,522/mo net** (not the ≈ $2,764 a monthly-only model produces at $13.81).

Every behavioral and market figure in this appendix — churn, plan mix, paid share — is a **founder estimate carrying no external source**, and is enrolled in §7.6's assumed-and-unvalidated list on those terms.

## Appendix G — Deferred (explicitly out of v1)

- shareable preview links
- custom font uploads (the curated 30-pairing pool is the whole font surface — Appendix D)
- team seats/collaboration
- **in-app compiled-code viewer** (export covers v1 — FR-J12). Consequence, stated so no one hunts for it: FR-L3's exit gate names "any surface exposing compiled theme code", and **no such surface ships in v1** — the Routes Manager's YAML pane and its `routes.yaml` download are explicitly exempt (FR-L3, FR-I2, FR-I4), so that clause gates nothing until the viewer ships and exists only to bind the viewer when it does
- account-level custom Style Pack library (custom packs are per-project — FR-E3)
- AI copy or layout suggestions
- theme export marketplace
- per-breakpoint editing (FR-D8)
- nested/columns free-form layout
- comments on suggestions (FR-M2)
- app dark theme (the app chrome ships light; *authored sites* fully support dark)
- ActivityPub/social-web section group
- brand extraction from arbitrary URLs (auto-branding reads the connected Ghost site only — FR-C4)
- public deploy quality badges/reports
- auto-captured canvas thumbnails (v1 ships static Style-Pack-derived placeholders — FR-B1)
- live-site theme preview UX (deploy-without-activate exists, but no preview surfacing — previews would require a working Ghost install)
- promotion of non-accent color tokens to Ghost Admin (v1 is accent-only — FR-Q5; other tokens have no dark built-in counterpart)
- **RTL layout** (v1 generated themes are LTR). This is the one deferral with a **gate rather than a silence**: selecting an RTL language code — or linking a site whose `@site.locale` is one — requires a blocking acknowledgement that generated themes are LTR-only, recorded and repeated as a pre-deploy warning (FR-Q6). Translation itself ships in v1: section copy is user-authored via text props (any language), and chrome strings are user-overridable via the Translations module (`{{t}}` + a single `locales/` file, `appendix-h1-string-catalog.md`). Dates render via `{{date}}`, which follows the site's locale.

This list is binding in both directions: nothing elsewhere in the PRD calls "deferred" anything absent from it, and no FR delivers anything on it.

---

## Appendix H — Voice & Microcopy Canon (normative)

Copy across app and marketing is short, warm, confident, lightly playful — easy to understand with a bit of fun (owner directive). This canon governs Inflozo's own surfaces **and** the English defaults in `appendix-h1-string-catalog.md`, which is the normative source for every compiler-generated visitor-facing string that ships inside a user's theme (FR-Q6).

Canonical strings, reused verbatim: deploy button **"Ship it"** (subsequent: "Ship update"); success toast **"Live! Your site just got gorgeous."**; empty dashboard **"Every great site starts somewhere. Yours starts with hundreds of gorgeous sections."**; gscan step **"Checking your theme (Ghost will love it)"**.

Rules: exactly **one confetti moment** in the product — first successful deploy (respects reduced-motion); destructive and billing flows stay serious, never cutesy; errors are human and name the fix ("Ghost said no — this Admin API key no longer works. It was regenerated or removed in Ghost Admin." + Reconnect CTA — a 401 `Unknown Admin API Key`, the failure Ghost actually returns for a regenerated or deleted key; Admin API keys never expire, so "your Admin key expired" names a cause that does not exist — `MEASUREMENTS.md` §37).

**The deferred credential is asked for as an offer, never as a blocker.** At first deploy: *"One more step unlocks a safety net — a copy of your current theme before we replace it, plus a check that nothing else has changed it."* Declining is a plain button, not a link in small type, and the decline is acknowledged once with what it costs — no snapshot, no drift check, manual routes upload — and then never raised again for that project (FR-C1). Nothing in this flow implies the user has done something wrong.

**Permanence is stated plainly, once, at the moment of choosing.** The custom-template naming step says what it means — *"This name is permanent. Ghost remembers it on every page you assign, and renaming it later would quietly unassign them all."* — and then stops. No repetition on later screens, no confirmation theatre, and the rename control is simply absent rather than present-and-refusing (§7.4, FR-I3).

**The unit is called a *design*, in every user-facing string and every page of the marketing site.** Not a layout, not a variation, not a template, not a block — one word, so a user reading the sidebar, the picker, the gallery and the docs is never asked to work out whether three terms mean three things. "Variant Shuffle" keeps its name as a *feature*; nothing else carries the older word (Appendix I).

**Product copy about the library is count-agnostic.** "Hundreds of gorgeous sections" is the canonical phrasing, and no user-facing string quotes a design total. Two reasons, and both are structural rather than stylistic: FR-J14 commits to monthly library drops, so any number baked into copy is wrong within a month of GA — the inventory is a GA **floor** stated in FR-G1, never a number in microcopy — and "sections" in visitor- and user-facing copy is the loose, plural sense, while the precise units are **categories** and **designs**, counted in Appendix I. A string quoting a design total would be stale *and* would conflate the two units the glossary exists to separate.

**The persistence indicator's labels are the drawn ones** (FR-D10, `B Missing Surfaces` B6): **"Saved on this device" · "Syncing" · "Synced" · "Retrying · {n}s"**, plus the no-local-storage fallback "Syncing every change to the cloud". FR-D10's *Saved locally* is the state's name in requirements prose and is never printed.

**The canvas-state markers are app copy, not theme strings.** Three surfaces state what the canvas is showing, and none of them ever reaches a generated theme, so none belongs in `appendix-h1-string-catalog.md`: the synthesized-template marker **"Auto-generated — edit anything to make it yours"** (FR-D6), the paginated-preview indicator **"Page 2"** with a "Back to page 1" return (FR-D21), and the gated-body indicator **"Gated content — shown with sample text"** (FR-D16). They are informational, never apologetic — the canvas is telling the user what they are looking at, not excusing it.

**"Live" in the success toast is the site-is-published sense**, not §4's Live *environment*. The two senses never appear in the same surface — the toast is user-facing, the environment is an internal deployment target — and the glossary names both so the collision is visible rather than latent.

---

## Appendix I — Glossary (normative)

One line per term; the defining requirement governs on any nuance.

| Term | Meaning | Defined in |
|---|---|---|
| Section | A category of placeable design blocks. 34 of them | FR-G1 |
| **Design** | **The one word for the unit.** One structural design within a section category — the thing the picker selects, the thing a story builds, the thing the render matrix renders. **"Layout" and "variation" are not synonyms for it; they are retired.** Every design in the library sits inside one of Appendix A's categories, each carrying a per-design specification before it is built. Three fixed identifiers keep the older word and mean this: **Variant Shuffle** (the shipped feature name), **variant manifest** and **variant id** (deploy-record fields). Nothing else does | FR-G1, FR-G5, §4 |
| Non-placeable treatment | One of the 28 designs (A32 paywall designs, A33 Koenig card treatments, A34 pagination styles) chosen outside the canvas and applied once per project — absent from the Section Picker, Layers, drag/reorder, Shuffle and Remix, yet still inside every "every design" gate | FR-G1, FR-D5, Appendix A |
| Design picker | The mini-diagram thumbnail ring in the sidebar, showing position ("Design 7 of 18"). Present on every placed section, outside every declared `Controls:` list, never a Quick Control, never promotable. Formerly and confusingly called the "Layout Picker" | FR-D19, Appendix C |
| Universal control | One of the three controls present on every section — Background role, Vertical spacing, Top divider — declared once in `sections-inventory.md` and exempt from FR-F3's ≈ 15-control cap | FR-F3, Appendix C |
| Variant Shuffle | Cycling a placed section through its category's sibling **designs**, content preserved, within its `bindingContext` / `compileTarget` partition. **The feature keeps its shipped name** — it is what the design directory labels S6 — while the unit it moves through is a design; the two are not in conflict, and no third word is introduced | FR-D13, FR-D19 |
| Behaviour module | One of FR-G7's registry — `research-section-js-libraries.md` §2.1 is the list and the count (one shared `core`, the rest feature modules). Vanilla, repo-authored, bundled only when a design that declares it is placed, removed with that design, and carrying a written no-JS degradation | FR-G7, FR-J4 |
| Baseline (Widely Available) | The browser floor every section stylesheet and every FR-G7 module targets, pinned by date rather than by version list — `widelyAvailableOnDate: 2026-08-18`, resolving to Chrome/Edge 121, Firefox 122, Safari/iOS 17.2 | FR-G8, NFR-7 |
| Frozen filename | A `custom-{name}.hbs` name, permanent from its first deploy: Ghost stores the exact string on every page that selects it, never revalidates it, and derives the Template dropdown label from it. Distinct from **section partial** names, which are derived from layer names and may change on any recompile, and from FR-J10's per-site **theme name** freeze | §7.4, FR-I3 |
| Annotated HTML | The section authoring format: plain HTML carrying Inflozo directives, authored by the dev team, rendered directly by the canvas and serialized to `.hbs` by the compiler. Handlebars is never parsed or executed anywhere in the product | FR-G3, FR-J1, §7.3 |
| Style Pack | The project's token set (colors ×2 modes, fonts, radius, spacing, width, buttons…) | FR-E1 |
| Token | A named Style Pack value compiled to a CSS custom property; sections consume tokens only | FR-E4 |
| Main feed | The one section per paginated template bound to Ghost's native paginated posts context | FR-H2 |
| Site-wide singleton | A shared single instance (header, announcement bar, footer) compiled into `default.hbs` | FR-D5 |
| Synthesis Defaults | The normative default section stacks used to compile templates the user hasn't touched | FR-D6, Appendix A companion |
| Pre-Inflozo snapshot | The archived previously-active **theme**, captured before Inflozo's first upload to a site (first-upload trigger, deploy-only included — not activation-time), read with the Owner's Staff Access Token; where that token is absent or revoked the designed degraded path applies instead | FR-J13 |
| Cloud snapshot | The authoritative synced **project document** in Supabase — a different artifact entirely from the pre-Inflozo snapshot. On lock acquisition it replaces the local doc whenever its revision differs from the local base revision, and that replacement clears the local undo journal | FR-D9, FR-D10, `addendum.md` §AD1 |
| Drift detection | The pre-deploy comparison of a content manifest (path → SHA-256, line endings normalized) of the live theme against what Inflozo last deployed to that site×theme; any difference stops the deploy and names the changed files. Never a zip-byte comparison; fails open | FR-J16 |
| Test environment | The permanent post-launch stack — the pre-launch infrastructure plus Ghost targets **T1–T3, and T4 once it is acquired post-MVP** (§4) — on test domains. **All** testing runs here from the first customer onward; it holds no real-user data and is a standing, costed line item | §4, Appendix F |
| Live environment | The post-launch production set provisioned fresh at go-live (new Supabase project, Vercel production, live Dodo) and holding the production domains. Real-user data exists only here, and no test suite ever points at it. Distinct from a "Ghost deploy target" and from the "Live!" of the deploy toast | §4, Appendix H |
| Locked elsewhere | Read-only because another tab, browser or device holds the project's edit lock. Remedy: request editing, or take over. Independent of plan | FR-D18 |
| Plan-locked | Read-only because a Free account is over its project cap and this is not the one project it kept editable. Remedy: upgrade, or delete down to the cap. A plan-locked project stays viewable and stays exportable | FR-L3, FR-J12 |
| Edit lock | The single-active-editor lock per project across tabs/devices; deploy/export require it | FR-D18 |
| Open canvas, gated exits | Free users may edit anything, including Pro sections; enforcement only at deploy/export | FR-L3 |
| Preview-only | A connection that cannot deploy (Ghost(Pro) Starter); set and cleared automatically by the `hostSettings.limits` probe, with the deploy error authoritative. **⚠ Known untested until §4's Ghost(Pro) gate clears** — the probe is written against a payload nobody has observed, so asking the user is the primary path meanwhile | FR-C2 |
| Promotion | Exposing a control or the accent token as a Ghost Admin `@custom` setting | FR-Q3 |
| Orbit Weekly | The bundled license-clean sample publication every section previews with — 32 feed posts, 8 of them featured, sized so the default `posts_per_page` yields three pages and every Data-group Source returns a usable set, plus the fixture set below | FR-H3 |
| Style-guide fixture | The generated post body carrying every Ghost editor card in every class-affecting variant. Snapshotted from Ghost's own renderers, checked in, regenerated on a target bump. What `{{content}}` resolves to on every canvas, and the surface A25's designs and FR-Q7's card panels are designed against | FR-H3 |
| Comments fixture | The stated-count thread (14 comments across 9 threads) `{{comments}}` renders as on the canvas, so A28's count header has a real number | FR-H3 |
| Preview subject | Which post, page, tag or author a single-resource canvas renders. The fixture by default; user-chosen once a site is connected; named in the content-source pill | FR-D22 |
| Post Content section | A25 — the dedicated designable section wrapping `{{content}}`, carrying its own design picker on `post.hbs` **and** `page.hbs`, and owning measure, table of contents, drop cap, share rail and type scale. It does **not** own card styling; that is the card design module's | FR-Q7, Appendix A §25 |
| Owner gate | The blocking per-category review §4 defines: an automated sheet, then the owner's own hands-on comparison against real Ghost. As many gates as there are categories, run in sequence | §4 |
| Per-design specification | The specification each design carries — descriptor, structural descriptor, archetype, responsive rule, content fields, controls, data behaviour at 0/1/many, empty states, behaviour module, a11y — produced by its category's design-and-specification session and landed in `sections-inventory.md` before that category's build story opens | §4, Appendix A companion |
| Deploy artifact / rollback | A stored compiled theme zip; rollback redeploys one in one click, no edit lock needed | FR-J7, FR-J9 |
| Routes Manager | The visual `routes.yaml` builder (collections, channels, custom routes, taxonomies) | FR-I2 |
| Chrome strings | Compiler-generated visitor-facing labels, cataloged in `appendix-h1-string-catalog.md` and user-overridable via the Translations module; ship via `{{t}}` + `locales/en.json` plus the project-language file when it differs | FR-Q6 |
